# 🎱 Royal Billiard 8 — Masters League

An 8-ball pool game for phones and computers. The game is `index.html`; the online part (real opponents, friends, invite bonuses, global leaderboard, real-money purchases) is the small Node.js server in `server/`.

## Features
- **Always landscape and full screen:** on the first tap the game goes full screen and locks to landscape. On phones that can't lock (iPhone Safari), the game rotates itself. When installed as an app (PWA), it opens full screen in landscape. While the page is turned (a phone held upright, e.g. inside Telegram with the screen's rotation locked) the phone's own keyboard would stay upright at the side of the turned page, so every text box there gets the game's own keyboard (Latin / Cyrillic / digits / emoji) on the page's bottom edge; its ⌨ key hands the box to the phone's keyboard.
- **Languages:** O'zbekcha, English, Русский, Türkçe, Español, Português, Français, Deutsch, Bahasa Indonesia, العربية, हिन्दी, 한국어, 日本語, 中文, ไทย (Thai), Tiếng Việt (Vietnamese) (🌐 button). Picked automatically from the player's device language, or from their country (found from the device time zone) when the device is set to English. Everything on screen follows the chosen language — menus, settings, popups, toasts, tutorial, in-game banners, names on the tables and in the leaderboards' default player names — so no word is left in Uzbek. To add or change a text: put a line `uz key‖en|ru|tr|es|pt|ar|id|hi|fr|de|ko|ja|zh|th|vi` into `tools/xt.txt` (15 values; 13 if en/ru are already in DICT; 2 = only th|vi) and run `python3 tools/build_i18n.py`. Thai and Vietnamese (all 1807 strings, boot texts, `CC_LANG` TH->th / VN->vi, picked automatically in Thailand / Vietnam) are machine-quality drafts: a native speaker should read them once; the chat phrases are translated too, but the quizzes (server) fall back to English in these two languages. Test: `node tools/test_lang_thvi.js`. Server announcements (`/admin/news`, `POST /api/news/post`) may carry per-language texts in an optional `i18n` object (`{"en": {"title", "text", "btn"}, "de": {…}}`); a language without its own copy falls back to English.
- **Country:** detected automatically (time zone, then browser region) and shown as a flag on the leaderboards; players can change it in the profile.
- **Online 1 on 1:** real players are matched per city. Both devices get the same rack, shots and aim are streamed live, and chat works between players. If nobody is found within 15 s, a computer opponent (marked 🤖) plays instead.
- **Friends:** each player has a unique ID code. A friend code or a name in *Suggested friends* sends a **friend request** — the other player gets a pop-up with **Accept / Decline** (declined: nothing is added and nothing is said; accepted: both appear in each other's friend list, and the asker is told). **Share** opens Telegram's chat picker inside the Mini App (the link `t.me/<bot>/<app>?startapp=f_CODE` makes a new player a friend at once, with the invite bonus; anybody else sends a request). Friends can **chat** (pop-up with quick phrases, unread badges, swear filter, 80 messages / 60 days kept per pair) and start a table together with one tap; one gift a day to each friend, gift requests, and who is online (active in the last 2½ minutes). **Bot messages:** a player who signed in through the Mini App and allowed it (Telegram's pop-up, asked once when the Friends screen opens; or `/start` in the bot chat) gets a message from the bot — in their game language, with a button that opens the right screen — when they are away from the game and someone sends a friend request, accepts theirs, writes to them, or invites them to a table (friends only). At most 15 a day, one per sender per few minutes, nothing while the game is open; switch off in Settings → *Notifications in Telegram* or with `/stop`; a blocked bot is skipped.
- **Invite bonus:** the inviter gets 🪙 20 000 and the invited friend gets 🪙 8 000 (coins only: 💎 are never given away). It works with an invite link (`?ref=CODE`) or by entering the code in the first 7 days.
- **Weekly leaderboards (like 8 Ball Pool):** My League / Friends / Country / World tabs, ranked by coins won this week (Monday–Sunday UTC). Prizes for the world top 3 each week: 🪙 1 500 000 / 750 000 / 400 000 (country top 3: 400 000 / 200 000 / 100 000).
- **Leagues:** Bronze → Silver → Gold → Platinum → Diamond → Master → Grandmaster. Everyone starts in Bronze; each week the top 20% of a league move up and the bottom 20% move down.
- **Win streak bonus** (retention; `streakPct` / `streakNext` / `STREAK_STEPS` in `index.html`, `node tools/test_streak.js`): `profile.wstreak` (wins in a row, ended by a loss) used to be only a number in the profile. Now the 3rd win in a row pays **+10%** of the match prize on top, the 5th and every later one **+25%** (coins only - diamonds are never given away; not in lobby tournaments, trials or lost matches). The bonus arrives as a toast ("Win streak x3 · +10%") and as a flame row under the prize on the result screen (`G.rw.streak`); the steps are one line to change. The medals for streaks (3, 5, 10 ... in a row) stay as they were.
- **"Next goal" strip on the home screen** (retention; `homeGoals()` / `updateHomeGoal()` / `#hGoal` in `index.html`, `node tools/test_home_goal.js`): under the mode cards a gold pill names the reward that is nearest - "Win streak 2/3: +10% bonus", "Trophy Road: 2 more 🏆", "Season Pass: 20 ♞ to level 5", a daily mission "... 1/4" - with a progress bar; a tap opens the right screen (streak / mission: Choose a table, Road, Pass, tournaments, minigames or clubs by the mission's kind). Things that wait to be taken (a finished mission, Pass tiers, a Road prize) come first as "... COLLECT!" (missions, then Pass, then Road); everything else is ranked by the **wins it still needs** (a mission "win 2": 2; a Pass tier: points / the Pass points of one win at the player's usual table, `profile.lastCity`; the Road: trophies / the trophies of one win there; a streak: wins to the next step), ties by progress. It refreshes whenever the home screen is built or `updateHomeDots()` runs, and a failure in it only hides the strip. The pill sits in the free band between the cards and the box slots of the owner's mock-up (design coordinates 520,422 / 820 x 86), so it scales with the stage. Texts are in all 16 languages (`tools/xt.txt`: Next goal, Trophy Road: N more, Season Pass: N to level, Streak, Keep your streak).
- **Golden Hour ("Oltin soat")** (retention; `GOLDEN` / `goldenHour()` / `goldenNow()` in `index.html`, `node tools/test_golden_hour.js`): every day **20:00-21:00 by the player's own clock** a won city match pays **+50% of its prize** on top (coins only; not in lobby tournaments, trials or lost matches; it stacks with the win-streak bonus as a second bonus). A daily appointment: the evening has a reason to open the game, and the players of one time zone (the channel can announce it) come at the same hour, so a real opponent is found instead of a computer one. Where the player sees it: the **home strip** names it while it runs ("Golden Hour: +50% on wins - 45m left", green, the bar is the time left) and teases it 20 minutes before; the header of **Choose a table** always shows a chip (the schedule, or "+50% · 30m" while it runs); the **result screen** gets a star row and a toast. The clock is `goldenNow()` (tests move it), the hour / percent / lead time are one line (`GOLDEN`). It reads the device clock like the daily gift does (`today()`); a server clock would need an offset from an API reply - not done. The strings are in `tools/xt.txt` (16 languages). The channel post that announces it is not written yet (the bank's `feat` kind, then `python3 tools/build_channel_bank.py` and the banner script).
- **Daily Shot ("Kunlik zarba")** (retention; `startDailyShot()` / `luckyEntry()` / `dailyShotFinish()` / `dailyShotTop()` / `shareDailyShot()` in `index.html`, `/api/dshot/*` and `dshotDay` / `dshotBook` in `server/server.js`; `node server/test_dshot.js` and `node tools/test_daily_shot.js`): the plain Lucky shot is one random layout per try. The daily shot is the **same layout for every player each day** - the server gives the Tashkent day and a seed (`POST /api/dshot/day`), the page builds the layout with `luckyLayout(avRng(seed))` (the generator takes a seeded random source; without one it is random as before) - with **3 attempts** a day, ranked by how close the gold ball stops to the target (px; `9999` = the gold ball was not touched; ties: the earlier shot). `POST /api/dshot/submit` counts the attempts (a 4th is refused, another day's is refused), keeps the best, answers with the place; `POST /api/dshot/top` lists the best 20 and this player's place (`db.meta.dshot`, the last 4 days; it survives a restart). **Coins only**: a ring pays what the plain shot pays, the centre 5 000 coins (the plain shot pays 💵 3 there); a miss 50. Entry points: the Lucky shot card in Minigames opens a small choice ("Daily Shot (3)" / "Lucky Shot") while attempts are left (so the grid of the Minigames screen did not change), and the home "Next goal" strip offers it first. The result window shows the place ("Best: 22 px · #1 of 40"), "Again (2)", "Leaderboard" and "Share" (Telegram's chat picker with the player's own invite link, or the phone's share sheet; not on portals - social out-links are forbidden there). The score is what the page reports, like every score in this game (nothing on the server can replay a shot); the server only keeps the attempts and the board honest. Needs the server (offline: the plain Lucky shot). Strings in `tools/xt.txt` (16 languages). **Never a dead end**: a finished lucky/daily shot leaves the bare table behind a window, so every window of it must lead back - the result window and the board both carry "Menyu" (`toMenu("mini")`), a closed/failed Share or Leaderboard (and the offline fallback) re-opens the result through `G.reopen` / `dailyShotBack()`, and a 1 s watchdog re-opens the result if no window has been open for 3 s (block 10 of `tools/test_daily_shot.js` checks ten such cases; it fails on the old code).
- **Club chest ("Klub sandig'i")** (retention; `CHEST_TIERS` / `clubChest()` / `addChestWins()` / `POST /api/clubs/chest` in `server/server.js`, `chestHTML()` in `clubHome()` of `index.html`; `node server/test_club_chest.js` and `node tools/test_club_chest.js`): until now a club only competed for the top 3 of the week. Now the **wins its members make this week fill one shared chest** with three steps that scale with the club's size: `max(5, 1 x members)`, `max(15, 3 x members)`, `max(30, 6 x members)` wins (a club of 3: 5 / 15 / 30; of 6: 6 / 18 / 36) paying **20 000 / 60 000 / 150 000 coins** (coins only, a server grant `kind: "clubchest"` that arrives with the next sync). Every member who has **won at least once this week** takes each reached step once a week (a member who has not won sees it but the server answers `no_wins`). **Where the wins come from:** the page reports its lifetime `wins` with every sync; the server counts the increase since the previous report on a counter of its own (`u.winsSeen`; the first report back-dates nothing) into `u.cw = {week, n, got}` - at most 20 per sync and 40 per member per week. Verified online matches could not be used: most matches are against the computer, and the weekly money prizes keep their own verified numbers (`addWeekly`). The wins are what the page reports, like every score in this game: a forged count only fills a chest of coins. Where it shows: a card on the club screen (progress bar with the three steps, each with its coins and a Collect key; "Win at least once this week..." for a member who has not won) and the home "Next goal" strip ("Club Chest: COLLECT!" when a step waits, "Club Chest: 9 more wins" otherwise; `Net.chest` comes with every sync). The member list of `GET /api/clubs/mine` carries each member's wins of the week (`cwins`). Strings in `tools/xt.txt` (16 languages).
- **322 cues in 5 tabs** (every generated cue has its own colour and pattern pair; a cue button next to the spin ball lets you switch or buy cues during a match): Standard (coins or 💎), Victory (collect 4 pieces by winning in a city or higher), Surprise (legendary cues, pieces from Surprise Boxes), Country (a flag cue for each of 199 countries) and Owned. Collection power grows with every cue.
- **Avatars and frames:** 100 avatars (20 free, the rest for coins or 💎) and 9 avatar frames.
- **Expensive economy:** 18 cities like 8 Ball Pool — London, Sydney, Lisbon, Tokyo, Las Vegas, Jakarta, Toronto, Cairo, Dubai, Shanghai, Paris, Rome, Bangkok, Seoul, Mumbai, Berlin, Istanbul, Osaka (100 000 000 entry / 200 000 000 prize).
- **Tournaments:** Asia Cup (32 players), South America Cup (32), Europe Cup (48), World Cup (60) — knockout brackets with byes, 5–6 rounds.
- **Clubs** (unlocked at level 6): create or join a club (up to 50 members), club chat, members ranked by weekly winnings; members of the week's top 3 clubs get 🪙 20 000 / 10 000 / 5 000.
- **Season pass ("King's Gambit"):** a monthly season with 30 tiers, a free and a premium track (700 💎), daily missions, exclusive cues and frame.
- **Cloud save:** the whole progress is saved on the server (Postgres) a few seconds after every change and when the page is hidden; a newer save on the server is restored on start. Settings → *Get key* gives a recovery key; on a new phone *Restore* with the ID + key signs in to the same account.
- **Settings:** sound, vibration, cue sensitivity, tap to aim, aiming wheel, guideline in offline games, power bar side, cheering, friend requests, online status, friend-online notifications. Coin cues go up to **5 billion**; premium cues go up to **1 500 cash** (💵) or the same value in 💎. Cash is hard to earn (1–5 at a time).
- **Currencies:** 🪙 coins (free), 💵 cash (earned in play or bought, a real-money-grade currency) and 💎 diamonds (the premium money that is never free), with the game's own artwork. The top bar shows all three; the 💵 chip (with the balance) opens the cash packs.

### Money (the owner's rule)

- **💎 diamonds: 50 💎 = $1; 💵 cash: 20 💵 = $1** (a cash price is the 💎 price / 2.5). 💎 are sold in six flat packs - 100 / 250 / 500 / 1 000 / 2 500 / 5 000 💎 for $1.99 / $4.99 / $9.99 / $19.99 / $49.99 / $99.99 (50.0-50.3 per $, no bonus ribbon, no card surcharge: `FLAT_USD` on the server, `FLAT_CARD_SKUS` in the page); 💵 in six packs - `cash_15` 20 💵 / `cash_50` 50 / `cash_110` 110 / `cash_256` 250 / `cash_800` 800 / `cash_2000` 2 000 for $1.99 / $4.99 / $9.99 / $19.99 / $49.99 / $99.99 (+ VIP points, shown on the Money page next to the coin packs). Both also come inside the VIP / starter bundles.
- **💎 are never given for free; 💵 are earned.** Free rewards pay 🪙 coins and 💵 cash (where they paid cash before: the scratch card's bonus, the 5/5 quiz bonus +1 💵, the invite bonus 3 / 5 💵, the weekly world top 3 (1 500 / 750 / 400 💵), the country top 3 (400 / 200 / 100), the club top 3 (20 / 10 / 5), mystery boxes, the wheel, level-ups, records). Never 💎: in the page only `applyGrant` (a server grant of `kind: "purchase"`) and the two one-time save conversions may raise `profile.gems`; `node tools/test_economy.js` scans the source for that. On the server only `fulfill()` puts 💎 into a grant (`dm: 1` = today's scale); refunds use `take` (never `gems`).
- **Where 💵 works (and where it does not).** 💵 buys frames (premium, horror and ordinary), the elite + special tables (`cashOkT`), gifts, mystery boxes, sticker packs, the surprise box, the limited cue, the scratch-card try, club emblems, past-season rewards, the premium pass, the Gambit coin bundles, and the Elite-shop "50 💵 for 💎" exchange. **It does NOT buy premium / horror / country cues (and the 💎-priced standard cues), premium tables or avatars: those cost 💎 (or Stars / real money) only.** Everything else that costs 💎 keeps its 💎 price: a price is the item's dollar price × 50 (`diaOfCash`). Whoever wants more 💎 than they have gets the "Not enough diamonds" window with the six packs.
- **Coin cues are locked by ⚜ Collection Power (the 8 Ball Pool rule).** The 45 coin-sold Standard cues come in nine price steps of five (4 000 / 18 000 / 90 000 / 440 000 / 2.2 M / 11 M / 52 M / 350 M / 1.7 B 🪙). Every step has a `needCP`: 0 for the first, then 55% of the ⚜ of every cue in the cheaper steps, rounded to 10 (150 / 310 / 500 / 720 / 950 / 1 210 / 1 500 / 1 810). While the player's ⚜ is below it the cue stays locked even with the coins in hand: grey picture, a padlock button "🔒 +N ⚜" (N = what is still missing) and "⚜ now / needed" under it; a tap says why and buys nothing. The lock is in the buy code, not only on the screen (`cueGap` / `cueLockedToast` in `index.html`, called by the shop row and by the in-match cue picker), so a stale button cannot buy either. 💎 cues, cues the player already owns and every other cue category (country, victory, surprise, VIP, premium) are never locked; ⚜ comes from owning cues, so a player with premium cues is rarely held back. `node tools/test_cue_gate.js`.
- **Premium cues live on the Premium page, priced in 💎 only.** The 💎-priced cues that used to sit on Standard (Fire, Dragon, Galaxy, Legend ...) are listed on Premium (and in the in-match picker's Premium tab); Standard keeps the coin cues. The rows of the Premium page show the 💎 price and the main money price (Stars in Telegram, $ elsewhere) but no card-transfer button (Horror and Country cues are the same: 💎 + Stars; other things - tables, frames, avatars - keep theirs; the Android app shows none, it has no card payments).
- **The diamond store is a big window** (`openDiamondStore()` / `dsCard()` in `index.html`; opened by the 💎 chip of the top bar and by "Not enough diamonds", like the Recharge page of Mobile Legends): six big pack cards (100 ... 5 000 💎, more gems and a chest on the bigger packs, the price as the green bar at the bottom, +VIP points), the balance on top. The Money page of the shop uses the same cards; in the Android app the prices on them are Google's. The other cue pages (Standard, Horror, Victory, Surprise, Country) have gold-framed rows in their own colour, like Premium (`.cr-<category>`).
- **Saves.** `migrateDiamonds` (flag `dm1`) only rescales the old 💎 balance once (`profile.gems` × 0.25 → today's scale); 💵 balances stay as they are. A reward-table entry that still lists gems pays 🪙 (1 gem = 100 🪙, `normRew`). Exchanges: 💵 10 → 🪙 10 000, 50 → 60 000, 250 → 350 000, 1 000 → 1 600 000; 💎 25 → 💵 10, 125 → 💵 50; 💎 25 → 🪙 10 000, 125 → 60 000, 625 → 350 000, 2 500 → 1 600 000 (10 cards on the Money page, also in the Android app).
- **Old orders.** SKUs of the removed old gem packs (`legacy: true`, hidden from the shop) are still delivered if a late payment arrives, as 💎 (old gems × 0.25); `fulfill()` of a cash SKU pays 💵 (`cash` in the grant, no 💎).
- **Where 💎 / 💵 can be bought.** In the browser / Telegram: by card, Stars or card transfer (the packs above). **In the Android app: with Google Play Billing** (the twelve packs are consumable in-app products `gems_100` ... `gems_5000` and `cash_15` ... `cash_2000`; `POST /api/play/verify` checks the purchase token with the Google Play Developer API, pays once through `fulfill()` and consumes the purchase; `server/play.js`, `PLAY_SERVICE_ACCOUNT` env var; see `android/README.md` for the Play Console / Render steps and what is not done yet). An app build without the plugin, a phone without Google Play, and Y8 (it forbids third-party payments) cannot sell 💎 or 💵: the "Not enough diamonds" window then says buying is not open yet instead of showing packs that do nothing (on Y8 premium things are for ads). The app shows no card buttons; its ads (AppLovin MAX rewarded video + a short ad every 2nd game, "watch N ads" only where 💵 buys, never 💎) switch on when the AppLovin ids are in Render - see `android/README.md`, "Ads (AppLovin MAX)", test `node tools/test_ads_app.js`.
- Tests: `node tools/test_economy.js` (page, also the no-card window) and `node server/test_economy.js` (server); Google Play Billing: `node server/test_play.js` (server against a fake Google) and `node tools/test_play_ui.js` (the app's page with a fake plugin).

- **Shop:** Special Offers (starter pack, diamond packs), Coin packs and Diamond packs with VIP points, Free Rewards, Minigames, Cues & More. Real money via Stripe; 💎→🪙 exchange.
- **Gameplay:** 3D rolling balls, spin, synthesised pool sounds tuned to real recordings, 3D cues, a tournament bracket, the daily reward route, lucky shot (a new layout every time: the white ball, the gold ball and the target are placed anywhere on the cloth, now and then with a second ball in the way; every layout can be solved), wheel, watch mode, achievements, and levels.
- **Quizzes with a gift** (`/api/quiz/day`, `/api/quiz/one`, `/api/quiz/answer` in `server/server.js`; `quizDaily()` / `quizOne()` in `index.html`; `node tools/test_quiz.js` and `node tools/test_quiz_ui.js`): Free rewards has the **Kunlik viktorina** card - five billiard questions a day (the same five for everybody, questions a player already answered are left out); every right answer pays **300 coins**, all five right **+1 000 coins**, at most 10 paid answers a day, every question pays once per player. The server holds the right answers (the page gets them only after answering) and pays with a server grant, so the coins are added by themselves the next time the game syncs. The channel's quiz posts (`Q...` of `channel_bank.json`) carry the button **"🎁 Sovg'ani olish · Get your gift"** with `startapp=q_<id>`: it opens the SAME question in the game and a right answer there pays the same 300 coins (a channel poll is anonymous, Telegram never says who voted, so the answer is given again in the game; the link is also counted as the ad source `ch_quiz`). New questions: `tools/channel_bank/quiz*.txt` -> `python3 tools/build_channel_bank.py` -> `node tools/build_quiz_cards.js`. **Picture cards:** every checked quiz has a 1080 x 1080 card (`server/channel/qc_<id>.jpg`: question in Uzbek and English, options A-D, the gift line); the channel sends the card first and the quiz poll right under it (a poll cannot carry a picture; if the card is missing or refused, the poll goes alone). The same cards can be used for Instagram / Facebook posts.
- **The raised cue (massé):** the "Kiy balandligi" slider of the spin screen lifts the butt of the cue. Seen from above, `drawCue()` then makes the stick shorter (never under half) and a little thicker, keeps it ON the aim line behind the cue ball and shows the lift with the shadow, which slides away from the stick towards the butt. (It used to be sheared up the screen, which left the aim line and squeezed the stick into a hair-thin line at some angles.) `node tools/test_cue_draw.js` draws the cue at 24 angles x 5 heights on a wide and on a tall screen and checks the direction, the thickness and the length against the flat cue.
- **A hall of its own for every table** (`paintRoom()` + `HALL` in `index.html`, painted once with the background; `node tools/test_halls.js` checks all 157 tables on a wide and on a tall screen): a seed made of the table's and the city's id gives the hall its floor (8 kinds: checker / diamond tiles, marble slabs, planks, basket-weave parquet, hexagons, terrazzo, deck planks), a rug in the family of the table's colour, the walls seen in perspective on both sides and at the back (4 wall styles: panels, pilasters, tile wainscot, wood) with an arched window that shows the **skyline of the city** (Eiffel Tower, Big Ben, Sydney Opera House, Tokyo Tower, Burj Khalifa, domes and minarets ... one silhouette per city, a generic skyline otherwise) at night, curtains, gold or silver mouldings, wall lanterns with a glow, (on a tall screen - a phone held upright - the back wall rises on: fluted pilasters, a big window with drapes between two smaller windows or gilded mirrors, a gold cornice and a coffered ceiling), vases of flowers (roses or daisies in natural colours) on marble pedestals, bouquets in the corners, petals, sparkles, a warm light from above and a fine grain. It only paints what the screen shows (a few ms), the floor, the rug and the decoration draw from separate random streams, so zooming never changes a hall.
- **Floor pictures: one for each of 156 tables** (`FLOOR_SRC` / `FLOOR_FOR` / `floorTiles()` in `index.html`, `tools/add_floor_patterns.py`, `tools/dump_tables.js`): the 156 floor pictures made by the owner (the corrected set `Billiard_156_tuzatilgan_JPG`: flat tile / parquet / marble / checker patterns, 512 px JPG; the first AI set of photos was replaced) are the whole background of the table: the picture only (no drawn walls, windows, lanterns, vases or rug), the table's shadow, a shade at the top (names and coins stay readable) and an oval shade towards the edges. `node tools/dump_tables.js > tables.json` lists the tables with their hall colours, `/usr/bin/python3 tools/add_floor_patterns.py <folder with naqsh_*.jpg> --tables tables.json --map map.csv` cuts the thin frame line off every picture (`--margin`, `--margin-for 087=0.2`), pairs pictures and tables by colour (the best overall pairing, so a red table gets a red marble and no picture is used twice), packs the pictures 20 to a WebP sheet (8 sheets, 3.4 MB, a 2 px rim repeated around each picture) and writes `FLOOR_SRC` (bare base64 WebP, so the portal builds turn the 8 sheets into 8 files, not 156), `FLOOR_FOR` (table -> [sheet, number]) between the `FLOOR_PATTERNS_BEGIN/END` lines. In the game each picture is laid as 240-unit tiles centred on the table, every second tile mirrored (no joins show, marble veins meet like book-matched slabs), an oval shade towards the walls; until its sheet has loaded only the dark base shows (a few ms). The Rose table keeps its hall picture. `node tools/test_halls.js` checks that every table has its own picture, that all sheets decode, the numbers are valid and no picture is used twice.
- **Hall pictures** (`ROOM_SRC` / `ROOM_FOR` in `index.html`, `tools/add_room_plate.py`): a table can stand in a real picture of a hall instead of the drawn one. The picture is scenery only (16:9, the middle of it empty floor): the game draws the table, the bars and the buttons itself, the picture is scaled to cover the whole screen and centred on the table. `/usr/bin/python3 tools/add_room_plate.py <picture> <name> --for e_ros,... [--erase x0,y0,x1,y1 --marble]` stores it as a WebP in `ROOM_SRC` and lists the tables in `ROOM_FOR` (`--erase` paints a table / buttons that were baked into a sample out of the picture). Only the Rose table (`e_ros`) uses one now (`rose`, made from the owner's sample); every other table has its own drawn hall.

## Run it yourself
```bash
cd server
npm install
npm start            # http://localhost:8080
```
Open `http://localhost:8080` and the game connects to the server automatically. Opening `index.html` as a plain file also works, but online features are then off.

## Put it on the internet for free (Render + Neon)
Player data is stored in Postgres when `DATABASE_URL` is set; without it the server uses a JSON file (local use).
1. **Database:** create a free project at neon.tech and copy the connection string (`postgresql://…?sslmode=require`). The server creates its tables itself.
2. **Server:** on render.com choose **New → Blueprint** and pick this repo. `render.yaml` sets everything up (free plan, root `server`).
3. Fill in `DATABASE_URL` (the Neon string) and `APP_URL` (`https://billiard8.onrender.com`). Leave the Stripe keys empty for now.
4. The log should say `storage postgres`. Open the URL and play.

The free Render plan sleeps after ~15 minutes without visitors; the first visitor then waits about 30 s. Data is safe in Neon either way. A paid instance removes the sleep.

## Fast loading: the light build (`web/`)
`index.html` is one 40 MB file (every picture and sound is inside it). Opened from Render it would be downloaded again and again, so the repo also holds the **light build** in `web/`:
a 4 MB page plus the pictures (`img/<hash>.webp`) and sounds (`a/<hash>.wav|mp4`) as files. `server/server.js` serves `web/` itself (same origin, so the API works as before):
* the page is downloaded once (about 2 MB gzipped), the pictures only when a screen shows them; their names are hashes, so they are sent with `Cache-Control: public, max-age=31536000, immutable` and the phone keeps them - an update downloads only the changed page and the new pictures;
* `sw.js` (service worker, where the browser allows it) keeps the pictures too (cache first) and never copies a huge page; a new cache name drops the old 40 MB copy;
* **after every change of `index.html` run `SAME_ORIGIN=1 INLINE_UNDER=6000 NO_ZIP=1 python3 tools/build_web.py web` and commit `web/`.** The file `web/.src` holds the hash of the `index.html` it was made from; if they differ (you forgot) the server ignores `web/` and serves the old single-file page, `/api/health` shows `"web": false`;
* `node tools/test_web_serve.js` checks it all: light page, immutable pictures, 304, Range for the video, the game boots and a match starts, a second visit downloads nothing, a stale build is ignored.

## Publish on Y8.com
**Build the upload zip with `python3 tools/build_y8.py`** (-> `bilyard8-y8.zip`, ~34 MB: a 2.5 MB `index.html` + `img/` + `a/` + `icon.svg`). The old Y8 zip was one 40 MB page with every picture inside it, and the player saw a black screen for about a minute while the browser read it; the light page opens in seconds. On Y8 there is no service worker, no HD pack and no download screen (`index.html` skips them on y8.com / in a frame); instead the logo carries a thin **"Loading… N%"** bar (real stages: page read, scripts run, page loaded, menu up), the Y8 rules stay (no card buttons, prices are "watch N ads"), and **one start ad** (`y8Preroll()`, `Y8.showAd` type `start`) is shown when the menu is up: once per visit, not on the very first launch (tutorial first), not for ad-free players. Test: `node tools/test_y8_build.js` (a Y8-style frame with a stub SDK).

The game has the Y8 SDK built in (login, cloud save, ads, leaderboard, achievements). It stays off until you fill in `CONFIG` at the top of the script in `index.html`:
```js
const CONFIG = {
  server: "https://billiard8.onrender.com",   // your Render URL (online play from Y8)
  y8AppId: "…",                               // https://account.y8.com/applications/ → create an app
  y8GameId: "…",                              // sent by the Y8 team after approval (ads)
  y8Leaderboard: "Leaderboard",
};
```
In the Y8 dashboard create a leaderboard table named `Leaderboard` and these achievements (key → name):
`first_win` First Win · `double` Double Pot · `breakpot` Power Break · `runout` Clean Table · `champ` Tournament Champion · `lucky` Bullseye · `streak7` Loyal Player · `rich` Rich · `collector` Collector.

What the game does with it: logs the player in (their Y8 name, progress saved to Y8 cloud), shows an interstitial ad after every 2nd match (at most every 90 s), offers rewarded ads (a video pays `VID_COINS` = 300 coins; +1 lucky shot and +1 wheel spin in minigames; 10 per day), submits total winnings to the Y8 leaderboard and awards achievements. On Y8 the Stripe packs are hidden; online play, friends and our global leaderboard still use your Render server.

Upload a zip with `index.html` and `icon.svg` at its root.

### Real-money payments (Paddle — preferred; works for sellers where Stripe is unavailable)
Paddle is the merchant of record (takes the card payment, handles tax, pays you out). The server builds each transaction itself from the SKU price, so no products need to be created in the Paddle catalog.
1. Paddle → Developer tools → Authentication: create an **API key** (permissions: Transactions, Customers, Addresses, Prices, Products read+write; Notifications read) and a **client-side token**.
2. Paddle → Developer tools → Notifications: add a destination `https://your-domain/api/paddle/webhook` for the event `transaction.completed` and copy its **secret key**.
3. Set on the server: `PADDLE_API_KEY`, `PADDLE_CLIENT_TOKEN`, `PADDLE_WEBHOOK_SECRET` (live) — or the same names ending in `_SANDBOX` for testing (sandbox is used when no live key is set, or when `PADDLE_ENV=sandbox`).
4. Checkout opens from `/pay?_ptxn=…` (served by the game server; the domain must be approved in Paddle → Checkout settings for live payments). If Paddle is configured it is used instead of Stripe.

### Real-money payments (Stripe, alternative)
1. Create a Stripe account and get your **Secret key**.
2. Add a webhook endpoint `https://your-domain/api/stripe/webhook` for the event `checkout.session.completed` and copy its **signing secret**.
3. Set `STRIPE_SECRET_KEY` and `STRIPE_WEBHOOK_SECRET` on the server.

Prices live in `SKUS` in `server/server.js` (the server is the source of truth). Purchases are credited to the player's account by the webhook, and the game picks them up automatically.

> ⚠️ **App stores:** if you publish to Google Play or the App Store, their rules require their own in-app billing for digital goods (Google Play Billing / Apple IAP), not Stripe. Stripe is fine for the web version.

### Telegram payments (inside the Mini App)
- **Stars** (default, always on when `TELEGRAM_BOT_TOKEN` is set): `POST /api/stars/invoice`; `STARS_PER_USD` (default 55) sets the price.
- **Card provider through Telegram Payments** (GlobalPay, ...; so'm, so offered to Uzbekistan players only - `isUzPlayer()` in the page, `p2pUz` on the server; abroad nobody sees so'm, the foreign card shows dollars): off until `TG_PAY_TOKEN` is set in Render. It is the provider token BotFather shows under *Payments* - **use the provider's TEST token first** (no real money). Then the buy button in Telegram asks "Stars or card" and `POST /api/tgpay/invoice` makes the invoice. `TG_PAY_CURRENCY` (default `UZS`) and `TG_PAY_PER_USD` (default 12700; the card price is rounded to whole 100 so'm) set the price. Telegram's own rules say digital goods in bots / Mini Apps are sold with Stars: check them before putting a live token in.

### Card-to-card payments (a transfer to the shop's own card, no payment provider)
In Telegram the buy button also offers "Card transfer" and / or "Via ATM" when these are set in Render (both OFF otherwise):
- `P2P_CARD` - the card number players pay to. Several cards (up to 4, e.g. Humo + Uzcard) go in one value separated by commas: `9860 1606 4515 2044|Aziz A., 8600 1111 2222 3333` (`number|name` gives a card its own holder name; the kind - Humo / Uzcard / Visa / Mastercard - is shown from the first digits). `P2P_HOLDER` - the name shown under cards without their own (optional). **Who sees which card:** `number|name|uz`, `|world` or `|all`; by default Humo / Uzcard are shown to players in Uzbekistan (or with no country set) and Visa / Mastercard to players of every other country. A foreign card works with the receipt flow only (the price is shown in dollars, the admin confirms): money from abroad never arrives as one exact so'm amount, so it cannot be matched from an SMS. A card marked `all` is shown to everyone. Either card can be paid: the order is recognised by its amount, so the phone must forward the SMS of every card. `TG_PAY_PER_USD` (default 12700) - so'm per 1 USD of the card price.
- **Card transfer (automatic)** - needs `P2P_NOTIFY_KEY` (a long random secret). The player gets a unique, not round amount (price + 1..99 so'm) and must send exactly that. The shop phone forwards every bank SMS to `POST https://<server>/api/p2p/notify?key=<P2P_NOTIFY_KEY>` (JSON `{"text": "<the SMS>"}`, the SMS as a plain body, or whatever shape the forwarder app sends: the text is found in any string field, also nested ones like `{"payload": {"message": ...}}`; Google Play does not allow apps that forward SMS to a URL, so these apps come from GitHub / F-Droid: Incoming SMS to URL Forwarder (bogkonstantin), TextBee, httpSMS, SMS Gateway for Android). An order is paid when its exact amount appears in an SMS. The order lasts 30 minutes.
- **Via ATM (receipt)** - needs `ADMIN_TG_ID` (your Telegram user id: send `/id` to the bot) and that you pressed Start in the bot. The player sends a photo of the receipt, the bot sends it to you with Confirm / Reject buttons. The order lasts 3 days.
- By hand: list the open orders with `curl $SERVER/api/p2p/pending -H "x-admin-key: $ADMIN_KEY"`, then `curl -X POST $SERVER/api/p2p/confirm -H "x-admin-key: $ADMIN_KEY" -d '{"id":"<order id>","ok":true}'`.
- Limits: 3 open orders per player; every payment is counted once (a repeated SMS or button press pays nothing more). Refunds are manual. Check the bank's rules about receiving shop payments on a card, and Telegram's rules for digital goods, before using it.

## Before a large launch
- The server keeps all players in memory and saves changed rows to Postgres every 2 s — fine for tens of thousands of players on one instance.
- Coins earned in matches are currently counted by the player's device. For a competitive economy, make the server the authority for match results and balances.
- Add a Privacy Policy and Terms of Service page (required by the stores and by Stripe).

## Controls
- **Aim:** drag anywhere on the table and the cue turns with your finger. A quick tap aims at the tapped point.
- **Fine aim:** use the ANIQ/FINE wheel, the mouse wheel, or ←/→.
- **Shoot:** pull the power bar down and release, or hold **Space**.
- **Spin:** tap the white ball in the top-right corner.

### Card prices of in-game items (`server/prices.json`)
Every cue / avatar / frame / table / sticker pack that is sold in the game for 💎 diamonds can also be bought with a card. The dollar price comes from
one rule (💎 = dollars × 50, rounded to 5; the card price is the dollar price plus the card surcharge) and is generated from the game's own catalog: after changing any price or cue tier in `index.html` run
`node tools/build_prices.js` (needs playwright) and commit the new `server/prices.json`. The server charges exactly these amounts (the client price is never trusted).
Cue strength follows price: cash cues sit on 8 dollar steps ($1.99 ... $74.99) and the four stat bars come from the step; coin cues come in 9 steps of 5 cues.

## Watching a live match (spectators)
A watcher plays the two players' shots on his own screen (the same deterministic physics) and is put right by the shooter's `state` (all ball positions, the turn, the groups) after every shot. What makes it hold together, and what broke on Y8 once (tested by `node tools/test_spec_sync.js`, MODE=normal|slow):
- **Message numbers.** The server numbers `shot` / `state` messages **per seat** (each seat counts 1, 2, 3 ...). A player gets messages from one seat only, so the page's duplicate filter (`rxN`) used one counter per match; a watcher gets BOTH seats and half of the shots / positions were thrown away as "duplicates": the balls jumped, the turn went wrong, the cue stood still. The server now sends spectators no `n` at all (this cures old pages / apps too, once the server is deployed) and the page keeps one counter per sender seat.
- **No shot is dropped** (`specShot()`): a shot that arrives while the previous one still rolls on a slow screen shows the shooter's final positions of the previous shot at once and goes on from them (or waits in `G.specQ` when they are not here yet). The `shot` message carries the shooter's shot number `no`, so the physics dice (`simSeed`) are the same for a watcher who came in late.
- **The turn.** Whoever sends `aim` holds the turn (a watcher whose own simulation got the turn wrong is put right by the first `aim`); a watcher never ends the match from his own simulation (`specEnd` from the server does), sends no `aim` and does not count balls into his own statistics.
- **The turn clock.** The ring round the avatar of the player who has the turn goes down for a watcher too (`drawPanel`).

## Chat phrases in every language
The ready-made chat phrases (`CHAT`, and `DM_QUICK` for friends' messages) travel as their Uzbek source text and every reader sees them in his **own** language: a 1x1 match bubble, the spectators' chat and the friends' messages all go through `qT()` / `T()` (16 languages, all phrases translated in each). Text typed by hand is shown exactly as written (no machine translation yet; a paid translation service would be needed, and voice translation would need speech-to-text + translation + speech, so it is not built). The phrase panel (`#chatPanel`) keeps its tabs and typing row and scrolls its list when the screen is low (a phone held sideways). Test: `node tools/test_chat_phrases.js`.

## English in a game played in Chinese: phrases that only had Thai + Vietnamese (the owner's tester, Nov 28)
**What the tester saw** (the Pack Stats screen of his test platform): *"The Who are you popup showed in English while the rest of the game was in Chinese on my phone."* That popup is the first-run boy / girl question (`genderAskWait()`: `T("Siz kimsiz?")` ...).
**Cause.** `T()` looks a phrase up in `XL[lang]` (built from `tools/xt.txt`), then in `DICT` (English / Russian), so a phrase with no entry for a language shows the **English** text. `xt.txt` has three kinds of lines: 15 values (`en|ru` + the 13 languages `tr es pt ar id hi fr de ko ja zh th vi`), 13 values (the 13 languages) and **2 values (`th|vi` only: "the 11 older languages already have it in the core table")**. 185 lines were of the last kind, and **85 of them were not in the core table at all**: they had been added with Thai + Vietnamese only, so in the other 11 languages (**tr es pt ar id hi fr de ko ja zh**) they were English. They were not only that popup: the open lobbies and the voice room, the daily / channel quiz, "Delete my account", the card-to-card payment check ("I paid - check"), the HD pack, "Official channel", the stake / reward notes.
**Fix.** The 85 lines are 13-value lines now (`pro_xt14.py`, rewritten in place; `python3 tools/build_i18n.py` rebuilds the compressed table). Words follow the rest of the file (lobby = Lobi / sala / ردهة / ロビー / 大厅 ..., cue = sopa / taco / عصا / キュー / 球杆 ..., coin = jeton / moneda / عملة / コイン / 金币 ..., the so'm of a payment note = UZS in Latin scripts, سوم / 숨 / スム / 索姆). **From now on add a new phrase as a 15-value line** (en, ru and all 13), never as 2 values; the 100 two-value lines that are left are all in the core table.
**Test:** `node tools/test_lang_gaps.js` (58 checks, ~1 min): every line of `xt.txt` has 2 / 13 / 15 values, no empty value, the `{0}` `{1}` placeholders of the key are kept; in the page **no DICT key (2111) lacks a translation in any of the 13 languages** and no literal `T("...")` of the source stays Uzbek (`ID` is the one allowed); the first-run popup, made by the game's own `genderAskWait()`, is in zh / ja / ko / ar / hi with no English word on it. It fails on the old file (21 failures, the popup in English in all five). What it cannot see: text that is not passed through `T()` at all (hard-coded English or Uzbek in the code), and a translation that is merely poor.

## Hosting layout (current)
- **Game server (API, WebSocket, lobbies, accounts):** Render (`server/`, `render.yaml`), `https://billiard8.onrender.com`. Environment: `DATABASE_URL`, `TELEGRAM_BOT_TOKEN`, `TELEGRAM_APP_LINK`, `TURN_*`, Stripe keys.
- **Game page (static, light build):** Cloudflare Workers static assets (`wrangler.jsonc`). Cloudflare runs `python3 tools/build_web.py web_site` after every push to the production branch; the page is ~2 MB, the pictures (including the table-rail and cue art) and sounds are separate files under `img/` and `a/` that never change (cached for a year). The game loads the table / cue art in the background after the menu is up (`bgStart()` in `index.html`; a match gets its own table and cue first), and inside Telegram the page calls `ready()` as soon as Telegram's script is there and pings `/api/health` so a sleeping Render server wakes up while the page loads. The Render server only answers API calls, so its free bandwidth lasts.
- **Telegram Mini App:** `@Billiard8PoolBot`, web app URL = the Cloudflare address; players sign in silently with their Telegram account.
- **CrazyGames / Y8:** `python3 tools/build_crazy.py` (single file) and the Y8 zip (see above).
- **Playgama:** `PORTAL=playgama INLINE_UNDER=6000 python3 tools/build_crazy.py <out_dir>` -> `bilyard8-playgama.zip` (index.html, `img/`, `playgama-bridge-config.json` at the archive root). The same portal game on the Playgama Bridge SDK (v2 CDN script first in `<head>`): the game code starts only after `bridge.initialize()` and one `bridge.storage.get([...])` of the 8 save keys; every save goes through `bridge.storage` in batches (no localStorage at all, checked at build time); the language is `bridge.platform.language`; `isAudioEnabled`, audio and pause events mute the game; `game_ready` is sent when the logo splash is gone; the two ⛶ full-screen buttons stay (Playgama's page has none; hidden when the frame may not go full screen; no automatic full screen on the first tap; on a phone the button also locks landscape); an interstitial is requested once when the game opens (placement `game_start`, once per visit, also for a brand new player - unlike Y8 - and not for players who bought no-ads; it does not wait for rewarded-video support), at every match end (`between_matches`), when the player presses the lobby button "Do'st bilan" (the ad is asked for; the lobby screen opens when it is over or after 1.5 s at the latest, so a skipped ad never keeps the player waiting), serves it, mocks `bridge.playgama.com` and checks the entry ad (returning and new player), the match-end ad, the lobby-button ad (also when Bridge shows nothing), no ad for an ad-free player and no localStorage.

## Ads in the Telegram Mini App (Adsgram)

`tgAdsInit()` in `index.html` loads Adsgram when the server's `/api/auth/config` has block ids: `ADSGRAM_BLOCK_ID` (rewarded video, the reward is verified through `ADSGRAM_REWARD_KEY`) and `ADSGRAM_INT_ID` (an interstitial block, "int-..."). With the interstitial block the game shows a short ad **before a solo match** (`y8StartAdOk()`: not the tutorial, not an online match, not for players with fewer than 3 games, and not within 150 s of another ad) and after every 2nd match end (at most every 90 s, `y8Break()`). Without `ADSGRAM_INT_ID` no interstitial is shown. The Playgama build uses the same hook (`Y8.startAds = "bridge"`: Bridge decides whether one really shows).

## Ad-link counter

Give every paid ad its own link: `https://t.me/Billiard8PoolBot/Play?startapp=c_NAME` (letters, digits, `_`, `-`, up to 24). The server counts opens and new players per NAME. An ad can also send people to the bot first: `https://t.me/Billiard8PoolBot?start=c_NAME`. The bot counts each Telegram id once ("bot Start"), and the Play button in its welcome message opens the game with the same `startapp=c_NAME`, so the game counts the open and the new player as usual. Telegram never presses Start for anyone, so the one-tap path is the direct game link above: on a brand-new player's first launch (menu shown, no games played yet) the game asks Telegram's own "allow this bot to message you?" pop-up once (`requestWriteAccess`), and an "Allow" makes the bot write its welcome message with a Play button (`/api/tg/allow`); a refusal is not asked again. Read it by typing `/src` to the bot (only `ADMIN_TG_ID` gets an answer) or at `/api/src/stats?key=ADMIN_KEY`.

## Channel posts (the auto-poster)

The game's public channel is `https://t.me/Royalbilliard8` (button in Settings and under the bot's welcome message; `TELEGRAM_CHANNEL_LINK` overrides the address). The server keeps it alive with **three posts a day for as long as it runs** (about 100 days of posts without a repeat, then the lists start again). It is off until you switch it on: make the bot an **administrator of the channel with the right to post messages** (channel > Administrators > Add), set `CHANNEL_AUTOPOST=1` on the server and redeploy. Keep the server awake (UptimeRobot on `/api/health` every 5 minutes): a sleeping free instance cannot post. A server that slept through a slot catches up later the same day, one post per 45 minutes, and what is left after 22:45 is dropped.

**When.** Three slots in Tashkent time: about **10:00, 14:00 and 20:30** (a small jitter of up to 15 minutes, fixed per date, so a restart does not move it). At least 45 minutes between two posts, at most slots + 2 a day. Only the first post of a day makes a sound; the others go out silently (`disable_notification`).

**What.** The kind of a post depends on its slot:

| slot | kind | what it is |
|---|---|---|
| morning | `feat` | a feature of the game (voice room, open lobbies, gifts, stickers, cues, tables, frames, avatars, ranking, cups, pass...) with a banner |
| afternoon | `quiz` | a Telegram **quiz poll** (billiards rules, history and stars (Mosconi, Reyes, O'Sullivan, films, famous people who loved the game), geography of the 30 city tables, facts of the game) with the right answer and a short explanation |
| evening | rotates `city` / `shop` / `tip` / `poll` / `story` | one of the 30 city tables with its real numbers and its banner; a shop showcase (cues, tables, frames, avatars, stickers, gifts, the season pass); a playing tip; a poll; a short scene or challenge |
| any | `dated` | tied to a weekday or a date and takes that day's slot: Monday morning "a new week" (ranking, Showdown and the Table of the week reset on Monday 00:00 UTC = 05:00 Tashkent), Sunday evening "the week closes", some Fridays, the start and the last day of every Season Pass month, 8 Dec, New Year, 14 Jan, 14 Feb, 8 and 21 March |
| evening | data | news made from real data (the weekly top three, player-count milestones, see below), when there is any |

Every post carries an inline **"▶ O'ynash · Play"** button and the same link in the text. The link is tracked per kind: `https://t.me/Billiard8PoolBot/Play?startapp=c_ch_<kind>` (`feat`, `quiz`, `city`, `shop`, `tip`, `poll`, `story`, `dated`, `data`), so `/src` (see "Ad-link counter") shows which kind brings players. A poll may refuse a button; the server then sends it without one and remembers that.

**The bank.** `server/channel_bank.json` (about 300 posts) plus the older `server/channel_posts.json` (59 posts, ids `L00...`, edited so that none says "one post a day"): feat 92, quiz 131, city 30, shop 24, tip 25, poll 18, story 14, dated 29. Nothing repeats until a whole list has gone out (the last 3 posts and the last 2 topics of a kind are also kept apart); a post of a particular date always beats a weekday post. Every number in the texts is read from the game's own data (cities, prizes, caps of the limited editions, ranking prizes, daily-cup hours, gifts, stickers, medals...); nothing is invented: no testimonials, no player counts, no promise that sounds like real money (a lint rejects such words), only virtual coins / 💵 / 💎. English comes next to every Uzbek text. The raw game screenshots are in `server/channel/` (`p..` first set, `t_<city>` the 30 tables, `c_..` cues, `s_..` shop, `m_..` match, `l_..` lobby, `x_..` pass / road / missions / friends / Showdown / profile); the posts use their **banners** (below).

**Banners.** Every text post goes out with a 1280x720 banner `server/channel/b_*.jpg` (139 of them) instead of a plain screenshot: a big gold headline in the game's own font, the game's own art (trophies, chests, coins, gifts, avatars, frames, cues, rings, city landmarks, its logo picture) and a small framed screenshot where that explains the feature, a green "BEPUL O'YNANG" button and the bot's name. They are drawn inside the game's own page (so the art and the font are the real ones) by `node tools/build_channel_banners.js` from `tools/channel_banners/specs.js` (one entry per topic; the 30 city banners come from `cities.json`; every post without a picture of its own gets one made from its title and topic, with a different picture for each tip). The script redraws only the banners whose entry changed (`server/channel/banners.json` keeps the hashes); `--force` draws all, `--only key,key` some, `--sheet out.png` writes contact sheets to look at. At start the server maps a post's picture to its banner (`b_<name>.jpg`, or `b_a_<post id>.jpg` for a post that had none); a post whose banner is missing simply uses the plain picture. Run `python3 tools/build_channel_bank.py` first when posts were added (the banners of the new posts are made from the bank file), then the banner script.

**Look of a post.** The headline is bold, the Uzbek body is a Telegram quote block, the English line is a collapsed quote block (tap to read), then the link and hashtags. If Telegram refuses the quote blocks the server posts that one in the old plain look.

**Editing the bank.** The texts live in `tools/channel_bank/*.txt` (one block per post, format in the header of `tools/build_channel_bank.py`; `cities.json` holds the numbers of the 30 tables). After a change run `python3 tools/build_channel_bank.py` from the repo root: it rebuilds `server/channel_bank.json` (the options of every quiz are shuffled, with a fixed seed, so the right answer is spread evenly over the four places) and lints it with the same checks the server uses (Telegram limits: quiz question 300, option 100, explanation 200, photo caption 1024; a missing picture; a bad id). If a post fails, the server prints `channel bank: N problems` at start and skips only those posts.

**Owner commands** (only `ADMIN_TG_ID` gets an answer): `/chan` shows today's posts (and the plan), the total, how much of each list has gone out in the current round and the next three days (times and kinds); `/chan now` posts the next one at once, `/chan now quiz` (or `feat`, `city`, `shop`, `tip`, `poll`, `story`) one of that kind. If the bot is not an administrator yet it says so once every 6 hours.

**Settings** (all optional): `CHAN_SLOTS="10:00,14:00,20:30"` or `CHAN_PER_DAY=1..6`, `CHAN_JITTER_MIN` (15), `CHAN_GAP_MIN` (45), `CHAN_END` (22:45), `CHAN_MAX_DAY` (slots + 2), `CHAN_SILENT` (1; `0` = every post makes a sound), `CHAN_NAMES=0` (no player names, see below). Test knobs: `CHAN_TICK_MS` (5 minutes), `CHAN_FIRST_MS`, `CHAN_TIME_SHIFT_MS` (moves the clock).

**Tests.** `node server/test_chan_plan.js` (79 checks, including: no post mentions 💵 (cash is never advertised in the channel), every text post has a banner and a caption within Telegram's limit) runs the planner and the real bank through 100 simulated days with a fake clock (exactly 3 posts a day, no repeats, 45-minute gaps, catch-up, the weekly rhythm, every dated post on its date, size limits, wording). `node server/test_chan_flow.js` starts the real server against a mock Telegram API (`TG_API_BASE`) with the slots set a few minutes into the past and checks that three posts go out (photo, quiz poll, evening kind), only the first one with sound, the button and tracked link, `/chan`, `/chan now`, and that a restart does not post the day again (add `nopollkb` to test polls that refuse the button, `noquote` to test that the posts go out plain when Telegram refuses quote blocks). Real Telegram itself could not be reached from the development sandbox, so the first live post is the real test: send `/chan now` and look at the channel.

### Posts made from real game data

With the same switch the server also posts from what really happens in the game. These take the **evening slot** (instead of the regular evening post) while any are waiting; a post older than 3 days is dropped:

- **Weekly results.** When a week closes (Monday 00:00 UTC) the top three of the world ranking are announced, with how many players competed. Fewer than three ranked players: nothing is posted. No prize amounts are ever mentioned.
- **Player-count milestones.** When the number of players who have played at least one game passes 50, 100, 250, 500, 1000, 2500, 5000, 10000, 25000, 50000 or 100000. The first run only records the current count, so nothing old is announced.

**Names need consent.** A player's name (and level) appears only if they switched on *Settings > Show my name in the channel* (`prefs.chanName`, off by default, stored on the server; a client that does not send it counts as "no"). Everyone else is "a player" / "bir o'yinchi". The post tells readers how to opt in. `CHAN_NAMES=0` on the server turns names off completely.

## Social networks (Instagram / Facebook / Threads / Pinterest via Metricool)

Portrait pictures and a 3-month plan made from the same specs and texts as the channel:

- `node tools/build_social_images.js` draws `social/p_<key>.jpg` (1080x1350, 4:5, feed posts) and `social/v_<key>.jpg` (1080x1920, 9:16, stories / reels / Pinterest) for every spec of `tools/channel_banners/specs.js` (only what changed; `--only p03,t_tas --kind p`, `--sheet <prefix>` for contact sheets). The art is the banner art (`B.render(spec, {social:true})` in `tools/channel_banners/page.js`) enlarged inside a portrait frame by `B.renderSocial`; a spec can move it with `soc: {s, hx, cy}`.
- `node tools/build_social_plan.js` writes `social/metricool_<YYYY-MM>.csv`, one file a month in Metricool's own import format (columns of `tools/metricool_template.csv`: Planning > menu > Import CSV > Download template). The free Metricool plan allows 20 posts a month, so `--per-month 20` (default) picks that many days of each month: holiday posts on their own dates first, the rest evenly spread, 19:00 Tashkent, no repeats. Texts come from `server/channel_bank.json` (only posts that have a picture in `social/`); Facebook + Instagram always, Threads only with `--threads` (it has to be connected in Metricool) and when the text fits its 500 characters; no brand name in the file (Metricool uses the brand that is open; `--brand` sets one); the picture is the 4:5 one. The game link is tracked as `...startapp=c_so_<topic>` (visible in the game's `/src`). Picture columns hold public `raw.githubusercontent.com` addresses of `social/` (`--base` changes them, e.g. when the branch is merged to main).

## Avatars by gender and the new-friend offer

- The 76 portrait avatars alternate: even = boy, odd = girl (`avGender()`; the premium pictures carry an m / f / n string, masked and helmeted ones are neutral). The first picture is still random, so the menu asks once **"Who are you? Boy / Girl / Keep the current one"** (`profile.gender`, `profile.genderAsked`); a default portrait of the other gender is swapped for one that fits, a bought premium picture stays. On portals (CrazyGames / Playgama builds) the question waits until the tutorial is done. The avatar picker in Profile has *All / 👦 / 👧* chips and opens on the player's own gender.
- Accepting a friend request now offers *"Play with your friend? Join the lobby"*: one tap opens a private table inviting that friend (the friend gets the usual invitation pop-up). Inside the lobby there is now a voice room (below).
- The menu shows **"N online"** (green dot, beside the mail icon) from `GET /api/online` (players connected to the server right now), refreshed every 30 s while the menu is open; hidden when the server does not answer.
- Friends screen: the ID box wraps its Copy / Share buttons under the code instead of letting them stick out (long Russian words on a narrow phone).

## Lobby voice room (talk before the match, up to 8 players)

- The lobby screen has a **🎤 Voice room** button under *Share / Challenge a friend*. Tap it: the browser asks for the microphone, then everybody in the lobby who has also joined the room hears each other (like the voice room of Mobile Legends). Buttons: the big one mutes / un-mutes, 🔊 silences the others for me, ✕ leaves the room. Each player slot shows 🎤 (in the room) / 🔇 (muted) / … (connecting) and a green ring around whoever is speaking; the line under the buttons says how many people are in the room, so a late joiner sees at once that friends are already talking. The menu's lobby chip shows 🎤 while the room is on.
- No microphone (blocked, missing, Telegram's iOS browser): the room still works as **listen-only**; tapping the button again retries the microphone. Same explanations as the in-match voice (`Voice.micFail`).
- How it works: a mesh of WebRTC calls (≤ 8 players, Opus, a few kbit/s each) - `LobbyVoice` in `index.html`. The server only relays the signalling: WS message `lvoice` (`hello` / `bye` / `offer` / `answer` / `ice` / `mute`, optional `to` = one member, otherwise everybody else in the lobby), accepted only from a member of that lobby and rate limited (`bucket(ws, "lvoice", 240, 100)`). The audio goes phone to phone, ICE servers (STUN / TURN) come from `/api/ice` like the in-match voice, so **mobile networks need the `TURN_*` variables** on Render.
- The player with the smaller id makes the offer; `hello` with `r` = "I started from scratch" resets old calls, `q` = "who is here?" (answered with the real hello). Calls are checked every second and rebuilt up to 5 times; hello is repeated every 8 s while somebody is not connected.
- While a match is on screen the in-match 🎤 uses the same, still open, microphone: the room says `bye` and comes back (hello again) the moment the player returns to the lobby / menu - without waiting for anything (see "One microphone" below). Leaving the lobby ends the room. Portals that mute the game (`__B8_MUTE`) mute the friends' voices too.
- Tested with 3 real Chromium instances against the real server (fake microphone device): all three connected pairwise, audio packets flowing both ways, mute / speaker / leave / late join / listen-only / suspend-resume (`lv_test.js`, 45 checks).
- **The microphone picture.** The 🎤 emoji is replaced everywhere (toasts, pop-ups, texts in every language, slot badges, the lobby chip) by one flat picture: a light microphone (capsule, U-shaped holder, stand) in a round dark-teal badge - compact and the same on every phone. `micShapes` / `micUri` / `MIC_SPAN` in `index.html`; a small `MutationObserver` turns any text node that contains 🎤 into text + `<span class="mico">`. The buttons use the same glyph (`Voice.IC.mic`). Muted = the same picture with a red slash (`.mico.off`), connecting = faded (`.mico.wait`).
- **Reward cards on the match result screen.** The little cards that pop up after a match ("-1 cup", "Showdown +20 points · +1 token", new medal...) used to sit in the middle and cover the player's picture and the table name. On the result screen they now go to the free top-right corner, smaller, and come one after another (one at a time) instead of stacking. CSS `body:has(#res.on) #toasts` and the cap in `pumpToast()`.
- **Reward pictures are shown whole.** The pictures that replace the glyphs in toasts and texts (cup 🏆, knight ♞, wheel, chest, friends...) were cropped by the toast's round-avatar rule (`object-fit:cover; border-radius:50%`): the knight had no head, the cup no crown. `.toast .ti img.ei` and a global `img.ei{object-fit:contain}` fix it.
- Audit of cropped / squashed pictures: a Playwright scan opened every screen and tab (plus reward pop-ups and every toast icon) and flagged each `<img>` / small `background-size: cover` box whose CSS crops or stretches it. Besides the toast pictures, only the Road-to-Cups city icons were squashed 12% (now `object-fit: contain`); what is left is intentional (city photos on the table cards, full-screen backdrops, round avatars, the home screen art that is scaled to the stage on purpose).

## The lobby as one room: voice at the table, watching, chat, joining a running tournament

The report from a friends' lobby (one friend inside Telegram): "the microphone does not turn on even in a normal browser", "in the next match his microphone does not work and he is frozen in the match", "the next player must not just wait - he must be able to come in and watch our lobby match, but nothing shows up for him", "all 8 must be able to talk", "add writing messages too".

- **Voice goes on at the table.** A match *of the lobby* (played or watched) no longer pauses the lobby's voice room: `LobbyVoice.matchOn()` is false for it, so all members - the two at the table, the ones waiting, the watchers, up to 8 - stay connected to each other the whole time (before, the room said `bye` and only a 1-to-1 call between the two opponents was left; whoever waited was cut off). The in-match 🎤 / 🔊 buttons of a lobby match are the room's microphone / speaker (`Voice.lobbyRoom()`; lit = my microphone is open in the room, no separate 1-to-1 call is made). Ordinary tables (random opponent, challenge) keep the 1-to-1 call.
- **A refused microphone is explained in a window.** The player who tapped 🎤 gets a window (not a 3-second toast) with the reason and the steps: permission denied / no microphone / busy / "open the game in its own window"; in Telegram's built-in browser: *"Telegram did not allow the microphone - tap Allow when Telegram asks, check Apps -> Telegram -> Permissions -> Microphone, then reopen the game; otherwise open the link in a normal browser; listening works"*. He still hears the others as a listener (`Voice.micFail(e, lobby, tap)`, `inAppBox`).
- **Watching from the lobby.** The live match of the bracket is a row with a clear **👁 Watch** button (`.lbw`, a blue pill) and a hint line; a stray global CSS rule `.live{position:absolute;...}` (the red "live" badge) used to throw that row to the top-left corner under the top bar, so the waiting player saw nothing - the row class is now `lblive`. A member who is not in the bracket sees "Tournament in progress" instead of "Waiting for the next round". Watching keeps the lobby voice room and has the lobby chat button.
- **Joining a running tournament.** The server no longer answers `lobbybusy` while a tournament runs: a free seat is enough. The newcomer sits in the lobby, gets the bracket (`tourstate` now goes to every member and carries `prizes`, `pool`, `fee`, `ids`), watches, talks and writes, and plays in the next tournament. The Open lobbies list shows a started lobby with a blue **👁 Watch** button instead of a grey "Started".
- **No frozen opponent at the next match.** A winner who stayed on the result screen (did not tap *Continue*) left his next opponent alone at the table. The result screen (or a reward window over it) now goes on by itself 6 s (12 s over a window) after the next bracket match is known; the *Continue* button counts down (`LB_AUTO_MS`), and there is no ad break in front of a bracket match that is waiting. Both phones send `hi` ("this match is on my screen"); until the opponent is there, the player whose turn it is not sees "Waiting for the opponent...".
- **Lobby text chat** (`LobbyChat`, WS `lchat` / `lchatlog`): a 💬 button under the voice buttons opens a chat drawer with an emoji row; at the table (and while watching) a round 💬 button with an unread badge sits at the table's bottom-left corner **just beyond the zoom + / - keys, centred on them** (the owner marked it on his Telegram screenshot: it used to cover the zoom +; `#lbChatG`, test `node tools/test_chat_key.js`; a watcher keeps the old corner above his microphone key, class `.spec`; the corner feed of new lines starts after the key), and new lines also appear in the corner feed. The server relays a line to every member (`bucket(ws, "lchat", 5, 2500)`, the swear filter `cleanChat`, 120 characters), keeps the last 40 per lobby and sends them to a member who comes in or comes back (`lobbyWelcome`). A ready-made phrase is shown in the reader's own language (`qT`), typed text as written.
- Test: `node tools/test_lobby_room.js` (6 real Chromium browsers, fake microphone, 37 checks): the room stays whole through the match, the waiting member hears the players, the Watch button is really visible and tappable, chat reaches everybody and works from the table, a late joiner gets the history and the bracket, the winner goes on by himself and the final really plays, refused-microphone windows (also with a Telegram user agent), the lobby is open again with everybody still in the room.

## Open lobbies + Quick join

- **Public lobbies.** A lobby can be *public*: ticked at the table chooser ("Open lobby (everybody sees it)"), toggled by the host inside the lobby at any time (WS `lpub`), or opened by Quick join. Private stays the default (friend duels, codes). The flag is `L.pub` on the server and `pub` in `lobbystate`.
- **`GET /api/lobbies`** (signed-in players): the public lobbies - `{code, city, fee, n, max, state, host: {uid, name, avatar, frame, country, level}, voice}`; open lobbies with free seats first, the busiest first; `voice` counts the members who are in the lobby voice room (`ws.lvOn`, set by `lvoice` hello / bye); host names go through the chat filter. At most 40.
- **Open lobbies screen** (`#openScr`; Friends hub banner "Open lobbies" -> Open, or ⚡ Quick join): one row per lobby (table + entry fee, host, seats, 🎤 voice count, **Join**), filters *All / Voice room / Seats free / My friends*, refresh every 5 s, **my connection time** (round trip to the server, green / yellow / red) in the header. Full / started lobbies and tables the player cannot afford are greyed. Leaving a lobby that was joined from the list returns to the list.
- **⚡ Quick join:** the busiest open lobby with free seats that I can afford; if it fails (full, gone) the next two candidates are tried, and when none is left a **public lobby of my own** is opened at the best table I can afford, so the screen is never a dead end when few people are online.
- The list shows no connection / ping indicator (removed: on a slow mobile network it only scared players).
- Tests: `ol_test.js` (3-5 real browsers against the real server, 25 checks).
- A started lobby (tournament running) can be entered too: the row has a blue **Watch** button (see "The lobby as one room").

## Adapting to the phone (Vivo Y31 report)

- **Root cause of the "different on another phone" layouts:** a phone held upright shows the page turned 90° (`html.rot`), and there the browser's own `vh` / `vw` units are the wrong axes (a 392x733 phone sized the result screen for a page 733 px tall that is really 392 px tall) - so the layout depended on the phone's shape: overlapping titles / chips / buttons on the result screen. Now every `N vh / vw / vmin / vmax` in the style sheets is rewritten at start-up (on the style sheets' own text, ~25 ms) to `calc(N * var(--u-vh))` etc., and `resize()` sets `--u-vh` / `--u-vw` to 1% of the page's real height / width (or `1vh` / `1vw` when the page is upright, so desktops and landscape phones are unchanged).
- **Small pages are scaled, not cut:** the menus zoom down to at most 80% when the page is smaller than ~700x340 (`uiz`), instead of overlapping or dropping pieces.
- **Menus are 12% smaller on phones / tablets** (main pointer coarse): `uiz` = 0.74 x the size-based value (never below 0.62), so more of every list fits on the page (the owner: "the menus are too big"). A computer keeps its size.
- **The band between the compact and the roomy layouts (page 521..560 px tall = a 393 px phone, 412 px phones):** the menus' height classes come from the page's height divided by `uiz`, so a 393 px phone is 531 px "tall" - just above the `mh520` compact rules, which were written for phones of 520 px and below. Screens that have compact rules only for `mh520` get their roomy layout there. **Minigames** was one: the grid was ~150 px taller than the screen and its second row of cards was cut off (owner's screenshots, portrait and landscape; the landscape one looked fine only because the pictures had not loaded yet). `html.mh560:not(.mh520)` now gives the Minigames grid compact cards (pictures 54 px / 104 px, smaller text and paddings, `#mini` top padding 62 px) so everything fits, nothing else changes: bigger screens keep 110 px pictures, phones below 520 keep their compact cards. Test: `node tools/test_mini_fit.js` (7 sizes: no scrolling, every card on the screen). **"Choose a table"** was the same gap and the owner saw it as "everything big, the beautiful cities pushed to the top": the roomy city card fills the card with stats and squeezes the city picture (`.cban`) into a ~58 px strip. Before the menu scale (11-07) his 393 px phone got the compact card (`mh400` + `mh520`); now the band has twins of both blocks (`.mh560:not(.mh520) .city ...`: big picture, short stats, the fee on the PLAY key, four cards in a row). Test: `node tools/test_cities_fit.js` (picture >= 100 px and >= 40% of the card, 4 cards fit, bigger screens keep the roomy card). The friend hub ("Do'st bilan") got its own `mh560` rules in the commit of Nov 12 23:59; a site deployed before that still shows it big (Settings -> the bottom line "v <commit>" says which commit the site runs). Other screens that only have `mh520` rules can show the same gap: add `.mh560:not(.mh520)` twins for them when the owner points at one.
- **Menus open at once and never flash** (`tools/test_instant_menus.js`): the owner's screen recording showed 1-2 dark frames whenever a window opened (the diamond store, also from the cue row) and a pause when a heavy screen opened. (1) No opening animation at all: `.screen.on` had a 0.24 s fade-in and `#modal .panel` / `.dstore` a zoom-in that started from nothing (scale 0) - a frame that starts from nothing is the dark flash. (2) The infinite animations of the lists are compositor-only now: the shine on the shop tiles moves by `transform` (it moved `left`: layout + paint every frame) and a ready prize glows steadily (a pulsing `box-shadow` repainted the whole Pass / Pass-like list every frame). (3) **Season Pass**: it built 400 tiers x 2 cells, every one with its own picture (avatar / frame / cue / table canvases), in one go - 1.4 s on a computer, 8 s on a 4x slower phone. Now only the tiers around the player's place (+-9) are complete; the others are empty frames (`.fcell`, same size and colours) that `passArtFill` completes a few per frame, nearest to the visible part first, as the list is scrolled (the Take buttons of late-filled tiers are wired the same way); table pictures are drawn one at a time (`tableThumbSoon`). Opening: 1.4 s -> 0.05 s (4x slower: 8 s -> 0.35 s). (4) **Trophy Road**: the marker code read each prize's `offsetLeft` right after adding a tick to the page, so the browser laid the whole road out again for every tick (1.5 s on a 4x slower phone). The places are read once now (the prizes have fixed widths): 1.5 s -> 0.4 s. (5) **"Choose a table"**: the first six city cards are made at once, the other 24 (off the screen to the right) a few per frame (`buildCities`, the online counts are applied to cards that arrive later too): first frame 0.74 s -> 0.42 s cold, 0.48 s -> 0.28 s warm on a 4x slower CPU. Measured only by emulation in Chromium (CPU slowed 4x with the DevTools protocol), not on a real phone.
- **Top bar:** the profile card used to lose its name / XP bar (only the avatar stayed) on a narrow page. Now the order is: language, cue banner, "+" buttons, sound / info, then the name (level star and XP bar stay), then the full-screen button, and only as the very last resort the whole profile text (`nf1`-`nf9` in `fitNav`).
- Checked by emulating phones held upright at 392x733, 392x640, 360x600 and landscape 915x412 / 1280x720 (screens compared before / after).

## Start (sign-in) screen on wide phones (20:9)

- **What the owner saw:** on a 20:9 phone in landscape (2392x1080) the old start screen was one full-screen logo picture, and the heading + Google / Facebook / guest buttons were laid out *under* it from a position computed from the screen's **width** - so on a very wide screen they were pushed below the bottom edge.
- **New design** (`#loginSplash`, `loginSplash()` in `index.html`): the owner's Istanbul night picture is the background (`art/login_bg_istanbul.webp`, `IMG.ls_bg`, cropped to fill, never stretched), a **small cut-out logo** sits at the top (`art/login_logo.webp`, `IMG.ls_logo`: the old logo art with its blue hall removed - GrabCut + hand-carved haze + edge colours taken from inside the logo, so there is no blue fringe on the dark picture), and the heading, the three buttons and the note live in **one column sized from the screen's height** (`cqh` units of the screen box): logo <= 27% of the height, buttons 36-52 px tall. The buttons are one row, wrap to two on a narrower phone and stack in a column on an upright screen; the page's own padding keeps them clear of the camera cut-out / system bars (`env(safe-area-inset-*)`).
- Before the server answers, Google / Facebook already stand in their final places (dimmed) so nothing jumps; a sign-in the server has not set up (`GOOGLE_CLIENT_ID` / `FACEBOOK_APP_ID`) is dropped instead of left as a dead button; "guest" always works.
- Google's own button (rendered 300x40 by Google) lies invisibly over the picture-matching one; `fitGoogle()` scales it to the button's **layout** size (not `getBoundingClientRect()`: an upright phone shows the page turned by 90 degrees and a turned rectangle has swapped sides).
- Test: `node tools/test_login_layout.js` (B8_ROOT=<repo copy>, SHOTS=<folder> keeps screenshots) - 18 screen sizes from 568x320 to 2200x990 and upright phones, Uzbek / Russian / English, the waiting / only-Google / only-Facebook / nothing-set-up states, taps reaching the right element, "guest" remembered after a reload.
- **Sign-in inside the Android app goes through the phone's own browser.** Google refuses OAuth requests from embedded WebViews (`disallowed_useragent`), Facebook does not support login there either, and both libraries open pop-up windows that a Capacitor WebView never hands back - so the two web buttons cannot work in the app. In the app (`window.Capacitor.isNativePlatform()`) the picture-matching buttons call `appLogin(provider)` instead: it opens `SERVER/auth/<google|facebook>/start?s=<state>&c=<sha256(v)>` in a Chrome tab (Capacitor **Browser** plugin) and polls `POST /api/auth/claim {s, v}`; the server redirects to Google (`response_type=id_token`, `nonce` = state) / Facebook (`response_type=token`) with `redirect_uri = <server>/auth/done`; that page reads the token from the URL fragment, removes it from the address bar, posts it to `POST /api/auth/stash` (verified at once with the same checks as `/api/auth/login`; a Google token must carry the sign-in's own nonce) and opens `royalbilliard8://auth` (the workflow adds this intent filter; launch mode `singleTask`) to bring the game back; the app receives the token **once**, only with the secret `v` (the deep link carries nothing), and logs in like the web page does. A cancelled sign-in is reported; closing the tab stops the waiting after 8 s. The web / Telegram builds keep the normal pop-up buttons.
- **To switch on (owner, once):** add `https://billiard8.onrender.com/auth/done` (= `APP_URL` + `/auth/done`) as an **Authorized redirect URI** of the Google *Web application* OAuth client (Google Cloud Console -> APIs & Services -> Credentials) and as a **Valid OAuth Redirect URI** in the Facebook app (Facebook Login -> Settings). **Facebook button is OFF by default** (`GET /api/auth/config` returns `facebook: ""` unless `FACEBOOK_BUTTON=1` is set in Render): Meta keeps the app in Development mode until the business is verified ("Подтверждение компании", needs company documents), so only the app's admin / testers could sign in and everybody else saw Facebook's error. Google, guest and Telegram sign-in are unaffected; the server side of Facebook sign-in stays set up (`FACEBOOK_APP_ID` / `FACEBOOK_APP_SECRET`), so accounts linked to Facebook can be reached again by setting `FACEBOOK_BUTTON=1`.
