- **Nov 21, "still no ad" after the new build was live: the owner's phone kept the OLD index.html in its cache.** GameMonetize's host answers `index.html` with `cache-control: max-age=315360000` and `expires: 2037` (ten years): a browser that has loaded the page once does not ask again, so the Verify window, "Cached" / "uncached" links and a re-opened tab all showed the old game (the "uncached" name only means GameMonetize's CDN is bypassed). **After every upload: clear the browser's cached files (Chrome: Settings > Privacy > Delete browsing data > All time > "Cached images and files" only) or test in an incognito tab with the game's own link `https://html5.gamemonetize.com/<gameId>/`.** The pictures are content-hashed, so only index.html is affected. (Real players who opened the game before an update keep their old index.html for as long as their browser keeps it: GameMonetize's setting, nothing in the game can change it.) The same page on an Android Chrome profile (touch, portrait, mobile UA) was run on the real SDK: ad requested, overlay on top, no-fill display ad on top, no page errors.
- **Nov 21, the ad showed on the owner's phone ("Reklama qo'ydi") - and the pictures were broken in the Verify window: GameMonetize has TWO copies of the game folder.** `uncached.gamemonetize.co/<gameId>/` (the Verify window, the "uncached" link) holds **only the files of the LAST upload**: after the delta2 zip it had index.html + the 647 delta pictures, the other 1501 answered 403 (this host answers 403 for a missing file); `html5.gamemonetize.co/<gameId>/` (the public "Cached" copy) is a merge: old and new files together, all 2148 answered 200 with the right size and type. So a delta zip completes the public copy but leaves the test copy half empty; **the test copy needs ONE full zip** (and "Request activation" should not be pressed before the Verify window shows the whole game: it is not known whether activation copies the uncached folder over the cached one).
  - **What GameMonetize applied and what not (the working theory):** applied: 35.2 MB / 1502 files / index.html 5.77 MB (Nov 16), 374 bytes (test page), 4.3 MB / 648 files / index.html 3.95 MB (delta2). Not applied: 36.07 MB / 1531 files / index.html 6.12 MB (v2.5 - v2.7 full) and 4.2 MB / 30 files / index.html 6.12 MB (delta1): the one thing that fails delta1 and the full zips but not delta2 is **index.html > about 6 MB** (a single-file limit?); the zip size (35.2 MB passed) and the file count (1502 passed) were not what blocked. Not proven; the limit itself is not written anywhere we can read.
  - **The full build that stays inside everything that passed:** `PORTAL=gamemonetize INLINE_UNDER=4500 /usr/bin/python3 tools/build_crazy.py <out>` (needs numpy + Pillow: `/usr/bin/python3` has them, the plain `python3` has not and the build then stops with a message). `SQUEEZE` is now on by default for GameMonetize (`tools/build_crazy.py` `_squeeze`: every picture re-encoded only if it still passes PSNR >= 36 dB and a block check, smooth ones such as felt are left alone: 214 pictures, the zip 36.07 -> 34.05 MB; `SQUEEZE=` (empty) switches it off, the tests do); the inline JS comments are stripped (`tools/jsrename.py` `strip=True`, -0.1 MB); `INLINE_UNDER=4500` keeps pictures over 4.5 KB as files (the threshold trades index.html size against the number of files: 6000 -> index 6.08 MB / 1490 files, 4500 -> 5.58 MB / 1563 files, 3000 -> about 4.9 MB / 1685 files). Result: **zip 34.06 MB, index.html 5.58 MB, 1563 files.** The build prints a WARNING when index.html is over 5.8 MB. Checked: the real SDK (ad requested, overlay on top), home and shop screenshots look the same as the unsqueezed build, no page errors.
- **Nov 21, the owner (the ad at game open works): "the 📺 key of the spin says watch an ad and no ad comes" - the 📺 keys now work on GameMonetize through an interstitial.** GameMonetize has no rewarded video, so the build used to answer every 📺 key with "Ads are not available right now". Now `Y8.adsReady` is true in that build and `showAd({type: "reward"})` (tools/build_crazy.py, `GM_JS`) calls `sdk.showBanner()` and **pays only when the SDK really showed an ad** (SDK_GAME_PAUSE came, then SDK_GAME_START; it waits up to 7 s for the ad request). The SDK cannot tell a watched ad from a skipped one, so any shown ad counts. **The SDK refuses a second ad inside its own gap (130 s on GameMonetize's domains, 30 s elsewhere): it answers SDK_GAME_START at once without a pause; the key then pays nothing and says "Reklama hozir mavjud emas" with the line "next ad in ~N s".** So a key tapped right after the entry ad (or after a between-match ad) is refused, a later tap works. The "watch N ads" chains (extra spin: 5 ads, Golden Shot / Surprise Box: 10 ads) were designed for rewarded video; with a 130 s gap 5 ads take about 10 minutes: if the owner wants it shorter on GameMonetize, the chain length (`TRY_ADS`, `GOLD_ADS`, `BOX_ADS`) can be cut for that build only (an economy decision: 💎 are never free). Also on now in this build: the "📺 ×2" button after a won match, "watch an ad: +1 💵" (2 a day). Tests: `node tools/test_gm_ads.js` (25 checks, 4 of them for the keys: pays once, refused inside the gap = no prize and not stuck, works again; with the old script 4 fail) and `tools/test_gm_real_sdk.js` (15: on the real SDK a key right after the entry ad is refused, no 2nd ad request).
- **Nov 21, "it still does not work" for the 📺 key (v29): the display-ad case.** When the VIDEO ad has no fill (what the owner's phone gets, like Icy Tower's screenshot) the SDK cancels it (`AD_CANCELED`, `SDK_GAME_START`, no `SDK_GAME_PAUSE`), retries once with its ADX tag, and then shows its own DISPLAY ad (`#promo`: black screen, "You can skip this in N secs"), which sends NO event. v29 paid a key only after `SDK_GAME_PAUSE`, so the ad was on the screen and the key said "no ad". Now (`GM_JS` `showAd`, v30): after an ad error (counted in `window.__gmN.err` from `AD_CANCELED` / `AD_ERROR`) a key waits up to 9 s for a second video ad or for `#promo`, and pays when the player has CLOSED the display ad (it covers the game: no way round it). A refusal inside the SDK's gap has no error event and still ends at once. Test: `tools/test_gm_real_sdk.js` (17 checks; the new one runs the real SDK with a failing IMA: entry display ad closed, 140 s later a key gets the display ad and pays on SKIP; the v29 script fails it with `gave 0, failed 1`).
- **A pocketed ball "returned to its starting place":** not GameMonetize-specific, the same code runs everywhere. When the **8-ball is pocketed on the break** it is put back on the table (the usual rule), but its message was only the small pill ("8-shar joyiga qaytarildi"), which the next turn's text replaced in the same moment, so the ball seemed to come back by itself. Now a banner in the middle of the screen says it (after any other banner that is still up). A ball that flies off the table, or the cue ball, already had a "foul" banner with its reason. In 16 + 16 AI matches (the plain page and the GameMonetize page) no other ball was ever put back. Test: `node tools/test_eight_break.js` (14 checks; without the banner 4 fail).

## Y8 screenshots, Nov 17: garbled emoji labels, Lucky Shot with 3 chances, the dark screen before the entry ad
- **"The language is English but Uzbek words stand at the top" (Y8: Leaderboard tabs "ðY'Y Do'stlar", Cues & More "ðŽ± Kiylar" ...):** those are exactly the 12 pieces of static text with an emoji, and only they were garbled (the game's own emoji, written in its script, were fine). Cause: the FIRST English-HTML Y8 zip (commit c3c0a9f) kept the text raw (non-ASCII) in `window.__SEN` and put that script **before** `<meta charset>` (byte 1386, past the 1024 bytes a browser reads for the encoding): a host / in-app browser that sends no charset decoded it as windows-1252, so the labels came out garbled and their translation lookup failed (they stayed Uzbek in the English UI). Fixed in d2d079e: `__SEN` is plain ASCII (`\ud83c\udfb1`) and stands **after** `<meta charset>` (byte ~75 in the Y8 page), so no decoder can garble it (`tools/test_portal_html.js` and `tools/test_html_lang.js` check it). The owner had uploaded the OLD zip: every zip had the same file name, so his phone's file picker offered the old one. **Rule: every zip sent to the owner has a UNIQUE name (`bilyard8-<portal>-v<version>-<commit>.zip`).**
- **Lucky Shot gave 3 chances ("it used to be one chance per 24 hours"):** the 3 were the "Kunlik zarba" (daily shot: 3 attempts a day) that the Lucky Shot card offered first and the home "next goal" strip too. `CONFIG.dailyShot` (default **false**) switches it off: the Lucky Shot is one free chance every 24 hours again (more for ads / money). The daily shot's code and server stay (`node tools/test_daily_shot.js` runs it with the flag on); to bring it back set `dailyShot: true` (better as a separate card, not inside the Lucky Shot). Test: `node tools/test_lucky_one.js` (8 checks).
- **"The screen goes dark, then an ad":** on Y8 the first click starts the ad (`y8FirstFire`); the darkening is **Y8's own ad overlay** while its ad loads (the game only mutes its sound in `beforeAd`), so the game cannot remove it.

## Levels 4 times slower, and a bot match pays no coins (the owner, Nov 17: "levels rise much too fast, they must rise slowly" and "when you play a bot no coins must be given")
- **Levels:** `xpNeed(l) = 400 + (l - 1) * 320` (it was `100 + (l - 1) * 80`): every level needs 4 times the XP (to level 10: 15 120 XP, it was 3 780; level 20 about 3.6 times). A table win gives about 85-135 XP, so level 2 is about 5 wins (it was 2) and level 10 about 180. The level-up bonus is the same (100 x the new level in coins). Players keep the level they have; only the bar of the next level is longer. `addXp` is the only place that touches XP / level (the XP comes from a won match, 12 for a lost one, 30 for a tournament round, 100 for a cup). **Consequence:** the tables open by level (`CITIES[].lvl`: Dubai 15 ... Moscow 55), so the high ones now open about 4 times later; lower those `lvl` numbers if that is too slow.
- **A bot match pays no coins.** A table match is "against a bot" (`G.bot`, set in `startMatch` for `type "ai"` with no `net` and an `isAI` opponent) when no real player was found in 15 s, or the server cannot be reached (`findOpponent` -> `bot()`). The entry fee is taken before the search (the player sees it held), and `bot()` **gives it back** with the toast "Entry fee refunded - no coins given"; the VS screen then shows no stakes and no coins flying into a pot, and `G.pot` is 0 (no pot in the match HUD). A **win** pays no prize and none of its bonuses (win streak, Golden Hour, table / set bonus, Elite +25%, Elite-table coins), adds nothing to the lifetime winnings (`profile.won`; the weekly rating is the server's own count of real matches anyway), and its result screen has no coin row, no x2 button and no coin flight; a **loss** takes nothing. **Kept** (practice, not a punishment): XP, the win itself (`profile.wins`, the streak count), trophies, Pass points, the box and pieces, missions. **Unchanged:** a match against a REAL player (`G.net`), lobby matches, and tournaments / daily cups with AI opponents in their bracket (they still pay their prize).
- Test: `node tools/test_bot_free.js` (32 checks, ~2.5 min, no game server: the curve; a bot win with every bonus switched on pays 0 coins; the same match against a real player pays the prize and all bonuses (the control); the fee is held while searching, goes back with the bot, stays taken when a real player is found; the Lounge stake; the VS screen). With any one piece undone 2 to 9 checks fail (the bot flag, the refund, the old curve, the streak bonus, the Elite-table coins, the VS stakes). `tools/test_streak.js`, `test_golden_hour.js`, `test_double.js` and `test_y8_build.js` start their matches against a bot opponent object, so they clear `game.bot` first (they stand for a real opponent).

## Voiced + moving stickers (the owner, Nov 17: "stickers must have a sound and move"; he chose AI-made animations and voices)
- **What is done (first step, the animal pack):** the nine animal stickers (🦊 🐯 🐼 🦅 🐸 🐺 🐻 🐰 🦎) have a recorded sound each (art/stk/*.mp3: AI sound-effect model, then trimmed / levelled / 22 kHz mono MP3, ~12 KB each) and **move with the loudness of their sound**: the tiger and bear shake and swell with every roar and send out rings of sound, the wolf throws its head back while it howls, the frog jumps with every croak, the eagle jitters, the fox nods, the rabbit bounces, the lizard sways, the panda squashes (`STK_VOX` / `STK_MOVE` / `stkMove` in index.html, drawn in the match bubble). It plays when the sticker shows up in a match (mine or the opponent's, `bubble()` -> `Snd.stk`): once, a new one cuts the old, a new one cuts the old, only a double trigger within 0.25 s is dropped (the owner: the opponent must hear every one), never when muted, not at all when the player chose "show only mine"; the sound goes through the game's master gain, so the portals' ad mute and the volume setting apply. A tap on a voiced sticker in the **world chat** plays it again (also inside the 1.2 s) and wiggles it. Every other sticker is drawn and played exactly as before. No new text, so no translation change.
- **Files:** `art/stk/manifest.json` (sticker -> sound + way to move + where the sound came from: model, job id, prompt), `art/stk/*.mp3` (the processed sounds), `tools/build_stk.py` (`--import <dir>` processes raw AI sounds `<slug>_raw.mp3`; with no argument it writes `const STK_VOX = {...}` into index.html; `--check` says whether it is up to date). `tools/build_web.py` now also moves `audio/mpeg` data into `a/<hash>.mp3` (the light build keeps only paths), and `server/server.js` serves `.mp3` as `audio/mpeg`. To add a sticker's voice: put `<slug>.mp3` into art/stk, add it to the manifest, run `python3 tools/build_stk.py`.
- **Test:** `node tools/test_sticker_voice.js` (19 checks, ~2 min; each of 6 undone pieces makes 1 to 3 of them fail).
- **What is NOT done, and why (the owner must decide):** real AI **video** of the stickers / premium cues / avatars / VIP items. The owner's Higgsfield account is on the **free plan**: video generation answers "Requires basic plan or higher" (Kling 3.0 and Grok Video both refused; a 3-second Kling clip would cost 4.5 credits, a sound effect 0.5), and one-time credit top-ups are not offered on the free plan. The free balance was 9.85 credits, 5.35 are left after the nine sounds. A paid plan (PLUS: 1 000 credits a month, $39, or $27 a month billed yearly) would pay for all 51 stickers' sounds (~25 credits) and short clips for them (~230-380 credits). Notes for whoever does it: the sandbox network must allow `upload.higgsfield.ai` (uploads: a presigned PUT, its token lives about an hour, send `Content-Type` and `If-None-Match: *`) and `d8j0ntlcm91z4.cloudfront.net` (results); both are now in the owner's environments (Default and `ajal-yog-dusi`); the free plan allows one sound job at a time (429 otherwise). A video sticker can reuse the same `STK_VOX` sound and `stkMove` fallback; the clip itself would be a sprite sheet (frames keyed out of a green background) drawn in the same bubble.
- **Licence:** the sounds were made on the free plan; check Higgsfield's terms for commercial use of free-plan results before relying on them in a paid game (each sound's job id is in the manifest).

## Sounds for the chat emoji (the owner, Nov 17: "only sounds, the 💬 window stays")
- The 😀 tab of the match chat window has 13 emoji now (🎉 out, 🤪 and 🤦 in): 😀 😂 😍 😎 😮 😢 😡 🤔 👍 👏 🔥 🤪 🤦, and each plays a sound when a **player** sends it (mine, or a real opponent's): `bubble()` -> `Snd.stk`, the same engine as the voiced stickers (a new one cuts the old, never when muted, "show only mine" hides the opponent's). Not for the bot's own chatter (an AI player's bubbles) and not for the spectator cheer keys (watch games). The emoji bubble itself looks as before (no motion).
- Sources: 5 clips from the owner's Icy Tower build (😂 laugh_1, 🔥 fire_1, 👏 clap_1, 😮 wow_1, 😡 angry_1; the other variants stay in art/emoji_sounds/; **their licence is not stated in that game's CREDITS.md: check before shipping**), 8 recordings by the owner (😀 😍 😎 😢 🤔 👍 🤪 🤦). The owner's recordings start after ~2 s of silence and hold several sounds, so `python3 tools/build_stk.py --import <dir> --cap 2.6` keeps the first sound(s) (the whole of a sound, up to 2.6 s) -> art/stk/emo_*.mp3 (manifest entries with move "emo"). To pick another part of a recording, cut it and re-import.

## INBOX: two dark bands beside the picture; watching: my name hidden behind the eye key (the owner's screenshots, Nov 18)
- **INBOX.** The window is a picture (the INBOX art) centred in `#inboxBody`; left and right of it two dark bands stood ("olib tashla, juda xunik turibdi"). Cause: the ach2 restyle `.screen>.panel{background:...!important}` (index.html, ~line 1341) paints every screen's `.panel`, and `#inboxBody` is a `.panel`; the plain `background:none` of `#inboxBody.panel` lost to that `!important`. Now it is `background:none!important` (with the higher specificity of `#inboxBody.panel`), so the room picture shows beside the INBOX window and nothing else about the screen changed.
- **Watching a match.** The corner chat feed (`#liveFeed`, rows `.lf` added by `Net.on("schat")`: "Ajalingman: 😮") started at `left:70px`, i.e. under the round eye key `#specEye` (and its counter badge) that a watcher has in the same corner, so the name was hidden. A watcher's feed now starts to the **right** of the eye key and its badge: `body:has(#specUi.on) #liveFeed{left:calc(max(2px, safe-area-left) + 104px)}` (`#specUi.on` exists only while `mode==="game" && G.net.spec`, so a player's own feed stays where it was).
- **Test:** `node tools/test_inbox_spec.js` (10 checks, ~1 min, no game server; 915x413 phone, 740x360 small phone, 1280x576 computer): `#inboxBody` has no background / shadow / border and paints nothing beside the picture; the feed's left edge is at least 4 px right of the eye key + badge. Without the `!important` 5 checks fail, without the feed rule 3 fail.

## Playgama: "no ad when the game opens" (the owner, Nov 18: "Playgamada kirganda reklama qo'ymayapti") - Bridge itself refused it, twice
- **Cause, read in the real SDK** (`https://bridge.playgama.com/v2/stable/playgama-bridge.js`, v2.4.3, `AdvertisementModule.show`): Bridge answers an interstitial with state `failed` (nothing is shown, and the game cannot tell why) when (1) **`game_ready` has not been sent yet** (`#ee === null`), (2) **fewer than `initialInterstitialDelay` seconds passed since `game_ready`** - the SDK's own default is **30 s** (`advertisement.initialInterstitialDelay` in `playgama-bridge-config.json` overrides it; our config did not say), (3) fewer than `minimumDelayBetweenInterstitial` s (ours: 60) passed since the last ad CLOSED. The game asks its entry ad (`y8Preroll`, placement `game_start`) ~1.2 s after the logo fades, so rule (2) refused it every time, and rule (1) would have refused it in roughly 6 starts of 10 on top of that (by the timers' arithmetic, not counted on a phone): the page sends `game_ready` 1.1-1.35 s after the logo fades (the check runs every 250 ms, +400 ms), i.e. often AFTER the ad request.
- **Now:** `playgama-bridge-config.json` has `"initialInterstitialDelay": 0` (tools/build_crazy.py `_cfg`), and the Playgama `showAd` holds an interstitial back until `Y8.gameReady` (set right after `P.sendMessage("game_ready")`; a safety cap of 9 s; rewarded video does not wait). The between-match ad and the lobby-button ad are unchanged: Bridge keeps 60 s between two ads (`minimumDelayBetweenInterstitial`), so a match that ends within a minute of the entry ad still shows none (Bridge's rule, not ours).
- **What is NOT proven:** that Playgama's own platform then FILLS the ad - Bridge now passes the request on, but whether the page answers `opened` is Playgama's call (ad fill, the player's region, ad blockers; their sandbox plays are mostly new players). Check the number in their dashboard after the new zip is up.
- Test: `node tools/test_playgama_ads.js` (23 checks, ~2 min; builds the Playgama zip): its mock Bridge keeps the three refusals above (the delay is read from the BUILT config file, as Bridge does), so an entry ad only counts when it was really shown (`int:`), and it checks the order (`game_ready` before `game_start`), a held-back ad (`Y8.gameReady = false`), and - as a check of the mock itself - that the old config (no `initialInterstitialDelay`) gets `fail:initial-delay`. With the config key removed 4 checks fail, with the wait removed 5 fail (`["req:game_start","fail:no-game-ready","msg:game_ready"]`: the real order of the old build).

## Playgama certification: "Did the game pause?" (the owner, Nov 22: step 8 "System Events" of Playgama's Guided / Extended Certification) - the pause message used to silence the game only
- **What Playgama does:** in the sandbox a step says "Start playing. The platform will send the pause signal 3 seconds after pressing" (before it, the same with the *mute* signal), then asks "Did the game pause?". It is the same `PAUSE_STATE_CHANGED` message Bridge sends when an ad is on, the tab is hidden or the platform's own menu is open.
- **What the build did before (and what I wrongly told the owner first):** the handler in `playgamaInit()` only silenced the sound (`window.__B8_MUTE`) and dropped the aim. The balls kept rolling, the turn clock kept running, the bot kept playing - the honest answer to Playgama's question was "No".
- **Now (Playgama build only; `index.html` and `web/` are untouched, the other builds too):** `tools/build_crazy.py` adds `b8Pause()` / `b8Frz()` (in `PG_JS`) and three patches in `frame()` (`update(); watchdog();`, `aiStep(game)` and the `FXS` line). While the platform says pause, a **solo / bot / practice match stands still**: the physics step, the bot's thinking and its shot, the turn clock (its number and its ring), the intro / banner / pill / bubble / venue clocks (`G.turnStart`, `G.intro.t0`, `G.banner.t0`, `G.pill.until`, `G.bubbles`, `G.venue.t0`, `G.movingSince`, `G.handUntil` are moved on by the time that passed, every frame and once more at "go on", so the player has exactly the time he had) and the effect animations (`FXS = 0`). The draw loop keeps running, so the picture is still there; the sound is silent as before.
- **An ONLINE match (`G.net`) is only silenced, not frozen:** the opponent's phone runs its own clock and one side cannot stop the table (`b8Frz()` answers false for it). A match that *starts* while the platform is paused (the ad before a match: Bridge's "closed" and "go on" messages may come in either order) stands still until "go on" and then has its whole turn time.
- **Safety valve:** a "go on" message that never came must not freeze the solo matches for good. A page that is **visible** and has been paused for `B8_PAUSE_MAX` (3 minutes; no ad lasts that long) goes on by itself (the sound too). A hidden tab stays frozen until the platform says "go on".
- **Compared with the zip the owner had uploaded (`bilyard8-playgama-v2.5-e67be63.zip`):** `index.html` did not change since, and the build of today's script *without* this change is byte-for-byte the v2.5 page; the new page differs by this pause code only.
- **What is NOT proven:** how Playgama's own sandbox judges it (it asks the *tester* to look: balls, clock, sound), and the real Bridge's message order on a phone - only a mock Bridge that sends `PAUSE_STATE_CHANGED` on demand was used. Chromium, not a real phone.
- **Test:** `node tools/test_playgama_pause.js` (26 checks, ~40 s with `PG_ZIP=<built zip>`, ~1 min when it builds the zip itself): the balls stand still during the pause and roll on after it; the turn clock holds (a control loses 2.5 s in 2.5 s, the paused one does not) and runs again; the bot's aiming stands still and goes on; a match started during a pause keeps its whole turn time; an online match keeps running and is silenced; the safety valve; silence on/off; no `localStorage`; no page errors. The same test on the *old* build fails 9 checks (the negative control).

## Playgama certification: "Leaderboard entries were not requested" (the owner, Nov 22: the only finding left at 100%) - the in-game leaderboard mode
- **What the QA tool tests (step 9 "Leaderboards"):** first the **In-Game** mode (`bridge.leaderboards.type === "in_game"`): "the game should submit scores, fetch entries from the platform and display them correctly" - the tester opens the game's leaderboard and Playgama's wrapper watches for `getEntries()`; then the "platform does not support leaderboards" mode (`not_available`), which already passed: nothing may break. Playgama's wiki (`wiki.playgama.com`, Leaderboards): `setScore(id, score)`; `getEntries(id)` **only in `in_game`**, entries `{id, name, photo, score, rank}`, drawn in the game's own UI; `showNativePopup(id)` only in `native_popup`. The leaderboard id (`main`) is in `playgama-bridge-config.json` (`leaderboards`), as before.
- **What the build did:** it sent the score (`saveLeaderboardScore` -> `setScore("main", profile.won)`, only when the type is not `not_available`) and its leaderboard button opened the game's own server table, which never asks the platform -> the finding.
- **Now (Playgama build only, `index.html` / `web/` untouched, CrazyGames / GameMonetize pages byte-identical):** `tools/build_crazy.py` adds `pgLbOn()` / `pgLbOpen()` / `pgLbHead()` / `pgLeader()` (in `PG_JS`) and three patches (the tab markup, `go("leader")`, `buildLeader()`). **Only when the type is `in_game`** the leaderboard screen has a **🎮 Playgama** tab (the other five tabs - My League / Friends / Country / World / Club Leagues - stay as they were), the screen **opens on it**, and opening it calls `getEntries("main")` (once per opening; choosing the tab asks again) and sends the player's total winnings with `setScore("main", ...)` (not held back: the entries are asked at once). The rows use the game's own leaderboard style (medals for 1-3, the platform's photo if it is an `https://` link - otherwise one of the game's avatars - name, winnings with the coin icon; the player's own row, `bridge.player.id`, is highlighted); names are escaped (a name with markup is shown as text). An empty answer shows "none yet", a failed request the usual "can't reach" line.
- **Other modes:** `not_available` (what the real Playgama platform answered when `bridge.js` v2.4.3 was read) and `native_popup`: no tab, no request - the screen is exactly the game's own (`native_popup` is not used: the game's own table with the weekly prizes stays the leaderboard there).
- **What is NOT proven:** what Playgama's tester accepts as "display correctly" and that a real Playgama sandbox in `in_game` mode answers `getEntries` the way the mock does (Chromium + a mock Bridge only; this section's build used no Bridge SaaS token: the real Playgama platform has no leaderboard of its own - `in_game` there comes from **Bridge SaaS**, see "Playgama: Bridge SaaS" below).
- **Test:** `node tools/test_playgama_leaderboard.js` (27 checks, ~1 min with `PG_ZIP=<built zip>`): the tab and the opening request, the rows (rank, name, score, medals, own row, escaping, `javascript:` photo ignored), the game's own tabs still work (and do not ask the platform), the Playgama tab asks again when chosen and on the next opening, the total winnings go with the visit and after a match, an empty table, a failed request, a table inside a wrapper and an error object (what Bridge SaaS can hand over), `not_available` / `native_popup` unchanged (no tab, no request, no score). The same test on the previous build fails.

## Playgama: achievements for all 71 (the owner, Nov 22, after Playgama's "Achievements" video: "Achievementsniyam qo'shaylik")
- **What Playgama's Bridge docs say (wiki.playgama.com > Achievements, the video):** declare the achievements in `playgama-bridge-config.json` (`achievements`: `id`, `name` and `description` - the last two optional, plus per-platform ids), call `bridge.achievements.unlock(id)` **once, when it is earned**; Bridge keeps them (natively on Y8 / Lagged, locally on the other platforms); `getAchievements()` returns `{id, name, description, unlocked}` for a game's own achievements screen.
- **What the build did before:** the config declared only the **9 main** achievements (`ACHS`, the Y8 names) and `unlock()` -> `y8Achievement()` -> `awardAchievement` sent those, once each. The game has **71**: the 9 main ones and the **62 of the second set** (`ACH2`: wins, potted balls, win streaks, levels, tournaments, cue collection, the 30 city rings, the 9 leagues, club, VIP; `profile.ach2`, awarded in `checkAch()`), which never reached the platform.
- **Now (Playgama build only; `index.html`, `web/`, the CrazyGames and GameMonetize pages are byte-identical):** (1) `playgama-bridge-config.json` declares all 71 with the **English name and description exactly as the game shows them** (`tools/pg_achievements.json`, written by `node tools/pg_achievements.js` from the game's own `T()`; run it again after `ACHS` / `ACH2` change; the first nine keep their Y8 mapping, the others have none: Y8 has no keys for them). (2) `checkAch()` is patched (`pgAch(id)` in `PG_JS`) so each second-set achievement is unlocked on the platform when it is earned; the main nine already were. (3) What a player earned **before** this build (`profile.ach` / `profile.ach2`) is sent **once** ~2 s after `game_ready` (`pgAchSync()`, remembered in `profile.pgAchSync`). A duplicate is never sent in a visit (`achSent`).
- **What is deliberately not done:** `getAchievements()` is not called and there is no second achievements screen: the game's own window ("Yutuqlar", `achModal()`) already lists all 71 from its own saved data, which is the same data Bridge got; asking the platform back would only be a request nobody uses.
- **What is NOT proven:** how Playgama's QA tool (or reviewers) check achievements - Chromium and a mock Bridge only (`unlock(id)` calls are logged); that Playgama accepts 71 declared ids (the docs show a short list; nothing there says a limit).
- **Test:** `node tools/test_playgama_achievements.js` (17 checks, ~1 min with `PG_ZIP=<built zip>`): the config has exactly the game's 71 ids in its order with the names / descriptions the game shows (so the JSON is current), the Y8 mapping of the first nine only, a main achievement is unlocked once, a second-set one once, two at the same moment both, every id sent is declared, the old achievements are sent once after `game_ready` and not on the next visit, no `localStorage`, no page errors. The same test on the previous build fails.

## Playgama: Bridge SaaS - Playgama's own leaderboard service (the owner, Nov 22: "Bridge SaaS buniyam burulla qoshaylik")
- **What the docs and the SDK say (wiki.playgama.com > Bridge SDK > SaaS and SaaS > Leaderboards; Bridge 2.4.4 read in full):** the leaderboards page lists `playgama` under "does not support" (its native type is `not_available`: that is why the real Playgama platform has no leaderboard at all). SaaS ("beta": leaderboards and multiplayer today) fills the gap. The game's card in the developer dashboard has a **Bridge SaaS** section (Leaderboards, Multiplayer) with the game's **public token** at the top of every page; it goes into `playgama-bridge-config.json` as `saas.publicToken`, and the leaderboard itself is created in the dashboard (Bridge SaaS -> Leaderboards). In Bridge's code (`_isSaas`): SaaS leaderboards are on when the platform is `playgama`, `playgama_sandbox` or `standalone` **and** a public token exists; on any other platform only if `saas.leaderboards.platforms` lists it. Then `bridge.leaderboards.type` is `in_game`, `setScore(id, score)` POSTs `{score}` to `https://api.playgama.com/api/bridge/v1/leaderboards/<id>` and `getEntries(id)` GETs it, with the headers `x-public-token`, `x-platform-id` and `x-player-id` (a player id always exists: the platform's user id, or Bridge's own guest id). The client hands over the backend's JSON as it is: it never looks at the HTTP status.
- **What the build does now (Playgama only; `index.html`, `web/`, the CrazyGames and GameMonetize pages untouched):** with a token, `tools/build_crazy.py` writes `"saas": {"publicToken": "..."}` into `playgama-bridge-config.json`. The token comes from `PG_SAAS_TOKEN=<token>` in the environment or from `tools/pg_saas_token.txt` (one line; commit it - it is public by design and sits in every downloaded copy of the game anyway; **never put a secret / private / server key there**). No token = no `saas` block = the build of before (checked: the config and every file except the two lines below are byte-identical to v2.8). A value that does not look like a token (8-200 of `A-Za-z0-9_.-`, no spaces) stops the build. The game code of the leaderboard section (above) needed no change: with `in_game` the 🎮 Playgama tab, `getEntries("main")` and `setScore("main", ...)` just work. One hardening: `pgLeader()` now also accepts a table inside a wrapper (`entries` / `data` / `items` / `results` / `leaderboard` / `rows`), and an object without a table (an error answer, e.g. the leaderboard is not created yet) shows the calm "can't reach" line instead of "none yet".
- **`qa_tool` is deliberately NOT listed** (the wiki's example lists `["playgama", "qa_tool"]`): the QA tool plays the leaderboard step through its own platform bridge (`platform-bridges/qatool.js`: the tool sets the type - `in_game`, then "not supported" - and receives the `getEntries` / `setScore` messages); with SaaS switched on for `qa_tool` those calls would go to Playgama's backend instead and the tool would never see them (the old finding "entries were not requested" again). `qa_tool` is not in Bridge's own list, so the token alone leaves it on the native path. `PG_SAAS_QA=1` adds `"leaderboards": {"platforms": ["playgama", "qa_tool"]}` if Playgama ever says it is needed.
- **What the owner does in the dashboard** (not possible from here): the game's card -> **Bridge SaaS -> Leaderboards**: create a leaderboard with the id `main` (highest score first), and copy the public token from the top of that page. Without `main` the backend answers an error object and the tab shows the "can't reach" line. The SaaS terms / price are the owner's to read there (unknown to me).
- **What is NOT proven:** anything about the real backend - there is no token here, so nothing was ever sent to `api.playgama.com`: that `getEntries` answers a table `{id, name, photo, score, rank}` (a wrapper and an error object are handled), that its CORS lets the game's origin in (the SDK sends `credentials: "include"`), how the dashboard leaderboard is set up (sort order, reset period), whether a guest's score counts, and how the QA tool treats a game that carries a token (expected: the native path, the same as without a token). Chromium + the real SDK + a mock backend only.
- **Test:** `node tools/test_playgama_saas.js` (29 checks, ~1 min; it builds the zip with a fake token unless `PG_ZIP` is given, fetches the real SDK from `bridge.playgama.com` with curl - or reads `B8_BRIDGE_DIR` - and says SKIP without network). The **real Bridge 2.4.4 with its real `playgama` platform bridge** runs on the real page of the build; only the platform's own SDK (`playgama.com/platform-sdk/v1.js`) is a stub and `api.playgama.com` a mock that logs every request. Without the `saas` block: `not_available`, no tab, no request, no score. With it: `in_game`; the screen opens on the Playgama tab; `GET /api/bridge/v1/leaderboards/main` once with the three headers; the rows and the player's own row; `POST {score}` with the visit and after a match; a wrapper, an empty table, an error object, a non-JSON answer and a dead network; a platform outside Playgama's list (`mock`, the same rule as `qa_tool`) is not switched on by the token alone, only by `saas.leaderboards.platforms`; a bad token stops the build. On the v2.8 zip (no `saas` block) 14 checks fail (the negative control: the token, `in_game`, the tab, every request and every answer case).

## Playgama: Extended Certification passed, the game is submitted for review (the owner, Nov 22, 16:14 on his phone)
- **The submitted build is `bilyard8-playgama-v2.8-e874be7`** (its name is in the QA tool's header). The QA tool went through all its steps on it (Build Startup, Game Saves, Rewarded / Interstitial Ads, Categorization, Authorization, System Events = the pause, Leaderboards, Localization, Scale test, Game Quality, Mobile) and ended with "Great job! You've completed the Extended Certification"; the owner pressed **Submit Game**: "Your Game Is Under Review ... usually takes 1-2 business days". Playgama answers in the developer dashboard (published, or the issues it found).
- **The Leaderboards step on the real QA tool (the first real confirmation of the in-game tab; the sections above only had a mock Bridge):** in the tool's in-game mode it waited for "the leaderboard button in the game"; the owner pressed the Reyting (trophy) button, the screen opened on the 🎮 Playgama tab and showed the tool's one entry (`guest_3187`, 100); "Did the in-game leaderboard window open and display data correctly?" - **Yes**. (The tool's own `getEntries` handler answered through the platform bridge, i.e. the native path: the reason `qa_tool` is not listed in the SaaS config.)
- **While the review runs: do not upload another Playgama build** (a new upload may need a new certification). Bridge SaaS (commit `fa52a07`, section above) is ready but **not shipped**: it needs the owner's public token and a leaderboard with the id `main` created in the dashboard (Bridge SaaS -> Leaderboards); build it as v2.9 only after Playgama's answer, and say so to the owner.

## Online match: "the turn ran out, 'opponent did not respond', then he shoots and the balls change places" (the owner, Nov 18) - a take-over that crossed a late shot
- **How an online match stays in step:** every phone plays every shot itself (the shooter sends the exact numbers, `shot`, with its number `no`), and when the balls stop the shooter sends the final places (`state`); the other phone takes those places over. Each phone also keeps a clock for the OTHER player: when it has heard nothing from him for `turnLimit + 8` s it **takes the turn** ("Raqib javob bermadi", `watchdog()`), gives him a miss (3 in a row lose the match) and sends its own `state` with the turn.
- **The bug (reproduced with two real browsers on the real server, tools/test_turn_clash.js):** if the silent player had only been *late* (his messages held up by a stalled mobile connection, his clock frozen, a phone in the background), his shot arrived after the other phone had taken the turn, and often after that phone had shot too. Two shots with the same number from the same table, each phone accepted the other's `state`, and the tables were **swapped for good** (about 270 units apart; the phones also disagreed about whose turn it was). The take-over's own `state` was worse: it describes the table *before* the late shot, so it could put the balls of the other phone's finished shot back. The same stale-table problem exists without a take-over (a late `state` of an earlier shot arriving after the next shot).
- **The rules now (`turnClash()` in index.html, nothing happens unless a take-over is involved, except the last rule):** a take-over is marked (`G.tk` = the number of the shot it takes; every `shot` / `state` it sends carries `tk`, and every `state` carries `no` = the number of shots the table comes after). (1) The phone that did NOT take over is right: it **ignores the take-over's messages** when its own last shot (`G.lastLocal`) is not older than the take-over (and it had not accepted the take-over before, `G.accTk`). (2) The phone that took over, if it has **not shot yet**, plays the late shot as the next one (the take-over is over; his miss is cleared); if it **has shot**, it ignores the late shot and takes his next `state` (the final places) instead - rolling balls stop, the table becomes his (`G.adopt`). (3) A `state` that comes after fewer shots than the table the phone is on (also a pending one, when the balls stop) is dropped. Spectators are not touched.
- **A second way to the same symptom (found because `tools/test_spec_sync.js` failed in about one run of six, on the old page too):** a tap on the cue becomes a shot 5 frames later (`G.strike`). If the opponent's `state` takes the turn away inside those 5 frames, the shot was still played on this phone - with the OPPONENT's cue, as his shot - and never sent: one phone had a shot the other never saw (tables 250-600 units apart, the watchers one shot short) until the next `state` of a shot overwrote it. Now `update()` drops a local shot whose turn has gone (`G.cur !== 0`, online, not a spectator): it simply never happened (tools/test_turn_clash.js scenario 5: tables 253 apart and 0 vs 1 shots counted on the old page, equal now).
- **What it does not do:** it does not stop a phone from taking the turn too early (the clock is still `turnLimit + 8` s) - the owner may still see the banner once, but the late shot now counts and the tables stay the same; it needs the **new page on both phones** (the website / Telegram load it on the next start; an old Android app keeps the old behaviour until a new .aab is installed); spectators of a clashed turn may see the annulled shot for a moment.
- **Test:** `node tools/test_turn_clash.js` (~8 min, 29 checks, two real browsers + the real server): a message hold (`window.__hold`, in order, like TCP) stands for a stalled connection. Normal shots; P shoots and is cut off for 14 s, Q takes the turn and (1) shoots too / (2) does not shoot: after P's messages arrive both tables are the same and both phones agree on the turn; a right take-over (P did not shoot at all) and the shots after it; P's own time-out table arriving 24 s late after Q's shot; six normal shots with every message 0.6 s late. On the old page 3-4 of these fail (the tables are 250-260 units apart, or a ball is in a pocket on one phone only; the phones disagree on the turn); without rule (3) the time-out scenario fails too (tables 430 units apart). **For a negative control the page AND the server must be a real copy** (`server/server.js` takes its root from its real path, so a symlinked `server/` would serve the repo's page).

## Cups / championships: a waiting room where real players gather, then the knockout (the owner, Nov 18: "kubokni bossa 3 minut kutiladi, kimlar qo'shilganini ko'radi, PLAY bosilmaydi, vaqt tugagach hammani o'yinga olib kiradi; Jahon Kubogi 5 minut, 64 ta odam")
- **Before:** pressing a cup took the fee and made a bracket of 100% bots at once (`joinTour` -> the bracket on the phone). **Now** (the owner chose "a real online cup" and "the prize as it is now"): pressing a cup takes the fee as before and puts the player into a **waiting room** on the server (`cups` in server/server.js, section "public cups"). Everybody who presses the *same* cup before the room closes is in the same session. **3 minutes** (`CUP_WAIT_MS`), a 64-player cup (the World Cup) **5 minutes** (`CUP_WAIT_BIG_MS`). The bracket screen is the waiting room: "Players are gathering", the names of those who joined (empty places "..."), one countdown on every phone (the server sends its own clock, `now`, so the phones agree), **PLAY is locked** and shows the countdown, and a leave button (the fee goes back while the room waits).
- **When the time is up** the server fills the bracket: the real players that came + "bots" for the empty places. The bots are drawn **on each phone** from the session's seed (`cupBotAt`, `Math.random` is swapped for a seeded generator while `botPlayer` runs), so every phone shows the same names. Round 0 is built like the old bracket (the byes spread evenly: `cupRound0` in the page and the server are the same code). Then, for every match: **two real players -> a real online match** (the server makes a room like a lobby tournament's, `startRoom(..., cup)`, and sends `match` with `cup:{sid,round,n}`; both phones start it by themselves after the VS screen, `cupMatch` / `cupPlayOnline`; a match that arrives while the phone shows a result is kept and started as soon as the menu is free, `cupPump`); **a real player against a bot** -> played on his phone a few seconds after the round began (`cupAuto`), the result goes to the server (`cupres`); **two bots** -> decided by chance on the server. A round ends the moment its last match is decided; the next one starts at once.
- **Nobody else comes** -> the player is alone with 31 / 63 bots, as before (the wait is the only difference). **No server answers** (offline, a sleeping server, an old server without cups): after 15 s the old cup against bots starts at once (`joinTourLocal`), the fee stays paid. **The server forgot the cup** (it restarted): the phone asks every 15 s (`cupstatus`) and hears `cupnone`: in the waiting room the fee is given back, in the knockout the cup is simply over.
- **Prize:** unchanged - the advertised prize is paid by the champion's phone (`tourChampion`, client-trusted like every cup before); the server never pays a cup match (`finish()` skips the table prize for `room.cup`).
- **Leaving / dropping:** a phone gone more than 3 minutes (`CUP_AWAY_MS`) leaves the cup (he forfeits what he has not played); a match against a bot not played in 20 minutes (`CUP_LOCAL_MS`) goes to the bot; quitting a cup match from the pause window leaves the cup. The sessions a phone has left are remembered (`cupMark` / `cupGone`): a late message of one never brings the cup back, and the entry fee is never given back twice (a waiting room can be entered again: its mark ends when the server's `cupleft` arrives).
- **Two bugs the browser test found while this was built (both fixed):** (1) `Net.on(...)` was registered before `const Net` exists (the whole page stopped at start) - the handlers are now below `Net`; (2) the result of a match is handled 1.6 s after it ends, and when the *other* match of the round had ended earlier the server's next-round bracket was already on the phone: the winner was then crowned champion without playing the final. A match now remembers its round (`G.cupRound`) and the result belongs to that round, whatever has arrived since.
- **What it does not do (v1):** a phone that is closed and opened again during a cup does not find it again (the bracket comes back only to a phone that asks, and the page does not keep the cup); a server restart ends the running cups (the waiting rooms are refunded); the old Android app (before the new aab) has no waiting room and plays the old cup against bots; bots in the bracket are drawn by the phones (cosmetic, the server only needs to know *that* a place is a bot).
- **Tests:** `node server/test_cups.js` (27 checks, the server alone with `ws` clients: rooms, waiting time 3 / 5 min, bracket, byes, bots, real-vs-real rooms, `cupres`, leave / drop / sweep, champion) and `node tools/test_cups_ui.js` (about 3 minutes, three real browsers + the real server with a 25 s waiting room and a 4-place cup: the gather screen and its countdown on every phone, PLAY locked, the names, leave + refund + join again, the knockout, a real online match with a shot relayed, the player against the bot, the final, the champion's prize paid once, a second cup where the bot wins, no server -> the old cup, `cupnone` -> refund).

## The shop's tables list freezes while it scrolls (the owner, Nov 19, Telegram, the Premium tables row: "after I moved to this tables row, when I try to go down it freezes and goes down in jerks")
- **Why it jerked (measured, not guessed: a 4x slower CPU through the browser's own throttle, a finger swipe sent as real touch events on the turned page).** On a phone held upright the page is turned 90 deg (`html.rot`) and **the swipe is handled by the page's own JS**, not by the browser ("A phone held upright shows the page turned 90°", `touchmove` -> `scrollTop`): every moment the main thread is busy stops the list. Old page, 4x CPU: touch events wait **240 ms** (median) and up to **5.9 s**, 4.4 frames a second, one task of 1.6 s, **20 table pictures drawn while the finger was still on the list**, and 22 big canvases kept. Four causes:
  1. **A table's picture costs 0.35 s on this computer (1.3 s at 4x), 85% of it the colouring of the big table photo** (`tintedTable`: a per-pixel pass + four box blurs over 1620x912 pixels). The list drew four of them in one go each time a new row came into view, inside the scroll (IntersectionObserver -> `tableThumb`).
  2. **The colouring canvases (6 MB each, one per table) were all kept** (`TINT_TBL` had no limit): 150 tables = 900 MB on a phone.
  3. **A demo match plays behind every menu** (`startDemo`, `game.demo`) and was simulated and drawn although a full-screen menu hides it: 3/4 of the work of every frame of the shop at 4x.
  4. **112 shine animations** (`.tblcard.lux .tprev::after`, `luxsh`) ran at once.
- **Now.** (1) `tintedTable()` is the old code as a generator (`tintedTableGen`, with `sharpFeltGen`; `drainGen` runs one to the end, so the game itself gets exactly what it got) that gives way every ~8 ms; the shop runs it in slices (`tintSlices`) **only while nothing touches the list** (`tblHot()`: a touch / scroll within 160 ms, or the page in the background), the cards on the screen first, then those just outside it (`TBLQ`, `tblPump`, `tblNext`, two IntersectionObservers; a card scrolled past is never drawn). (2) The colouring canvas a shop picture made is dropped when the picture is done (`TBLQ.own`; the selected table's stays). (3) `DEMO_COVER` lists the screens that hide the canvas completely; behind them `frame()` neither simulates nor draws the demo (the home screen, the VS / result screens and modals are not in the list and keep it). (4) The shine runs only on the cards on the screen (`.shn`, set by the same observer) and stands still while the list is dragged (`#tblGrid.mv`). New numbers, same test: median **62-70 ms**, max 0.23-0.27 s, 23-25 frames a second, longest task 0.5-0.6 s (native canvas calls), 0 pictures drawn under the finger, at most 3 colouring canvases kept.
- **The pictures are the same.** One-off check against the old page for all 156 tables: the coloured photo of every table (155 with one) and its shop picture were identical byte for byte (a control run old-vs-old showed the noise level: one table whose lazy art had not loaded); `tools/test_table_scroll.js` repeats it for six tables (sliced = at once) and checks the pictures made the sliced way against the old way.
- **What it does not do.** It was measured on an emulated slow CPU, not on the owner's phone. On a slow phone the pictures now arrive one at a time after the list rests (first one ~5 s at 4x, then every 3-4 s) instead of freezing the list. The first-row pictures are not stored between visits (an IndexedDB cache would be the next step). The other lists (cities, cues, frames) were not touched; the demo behind the home screen still runs.
- **Test:** `node tools/test_table_scroll.js` (about 4 minutes, 27 checks, one browser, no game server): the screens in `DEMO_COVER` hide a magenta canvas (portrait turned page and landscape); the demo's frame counter stands behind the shop and runs again on home; sliced colouring = colouring at once, pixel for pixel; the finger test at 4x CPU (touch delay, frames a second, longest task, no picture drawn under the finger, shine paused while dragging); at rest the pictures come, at most 3 colouring canvases are kept, only the cards on the screen shine. The old page fails 17 of its 22 checks (touch events wait 228 ms median and up to 5.8 s, 4.3 frames a second, 20 pictures drawn under the finger, 22 colouring canvases kept).

## Play Age Signals API in the Android app (the owner, Nov 19-20: Google's e-mail about the US state age laws and Brazil; "Birinchi yo'lni qil, qolsin" = keep the game in those countries and integrate the API)
- **Why.** From 2027 the app stores of some US states and of Brazil must pass a user's age RANGE to apps (Google: *Play Age Signals API*, beta). The owner chose to keep the game available there and integrate the API instead of switching the countries off in Play Console.
- **What was done** (details, tests and the honest list in `android/README.md`, section *Play Age Signals API*): a native Capacitor plugin `android/native/RbAgePlugin.java` (name `RbAge`; Google's flow `requestAgeSignalsAccess(activity)` → `checkAgeSignals()`), registered and given the Gradle dependency `com.google.android.play:age-signals:0.0.6` by `.github/workflows/android.yml`; page side `ageCheck()` / `ageBand()` in `index.html` (Android app only). The answer stays in `window.__AGE` on the phone (`band` = child / teen / adult / unknown): **not sent to the server, not saved in the profile, not used for ads, offers or statistics** (Play's policy), and **nothing in the game changes because of it yet** - a rule such as "no 💎 for `child`" is a legal / policy decision for the owner.
- **Written with reflection on purpose.** Google's pages give only short class names, and the library cannot be downloaded in the coding sandbox, so the plugin finds the classes by name and `tools/age_pkg.sh` (run by the workflow) reads the real package out of the library and prints its API into the build log. A wrong name can therefore never fail the Gradle build; at worst the plugin answers `unavailable`. The first real build (Nov 20) showed the library's real layout - package `com.google.android.play.agesignals`, the status constants in its `.model` sub-package (the first plugin version looked in the wrong place and would have answered `unknown` every time: fixed, tested, see `android/README.md`).
- **Tests.** `bash tools/test_age_plugin.sh` (the real Java file compiled with `javac` against stand-ins and a fake library of the documented shape, 16 checks, plus two negative controls: without `setAccessible` or without the `.model` lookup it fails) and `node tools/test_age_app.js` (page side, 21 checks). `web/` rebuilt (`web/.src` = sha1 of `index.html`), `node tools/test_html_lang.js` 22/0, `node tools/test_ads_app.js` 23/0.
- **Not proven:** a run on a phone, and what Play answers in the US / Brazil. The real GitHub build compiled and packaged it (runs 27 and 28) and every class / method / constant the plugin uses is confirmed from the library's own API in that build log. A new `.aab` (Actions → "Android app (Play Market)") is needed; the website, Telegram and the portals are untouched by this.

## 29 new glowing cues in the shop (the owner, Nov 20: a picture of 29 cues, "buni pulga qilib qo'yaylik, shu pulga eng qimmati 3000 bo'lsin")
- **What.** The owner's picture (`art/cue_sheet_nov20.png`, 29 cues standing on black) became 29 cues `nc_01` .. `nc_29`, listed on the shop's **Premium** page (gold-frame card, picture, four stat bars, Try / Watch) and sold for **💵 cash only** - the paper money, the green banknote chip (the owner, Nov 20, with an arrow on that chip: *"qog'ozli pulga degandim"*; the first version read "pul" as 💎 and was wrong). **Why 💵:** the money of the weekly rating is 💵 (world top 3 = **1500 / 750 / 400**, country top 3 = 400 / 200 / 100), and the owner wants a player to be able to collect a cue with those prizes; 3 000 💵 = two weeks of a world #1. They have **no 💎 price, no card price and no server SKU** (`server/prices.json` is unchanged: 360 prices).
- **Prices (💵):** **200** (1: Moviy Chaqin) · **400** (4) · **600** (8) · **1 000** (6) · **1 500** (5) · **2 250** (3) · **3 000** (2: Qirol Sakkizligi, Safir Toji = the dearest, as asked). The ladder numbers are mine (the owner gave only the top): to change one, edit its number in `NEW_CUES` (`index.html`); the stat total follows the price (`NC_STAT_TOTAL`: same price = same strength). The new cues are skipped by the shared dollar / 💎 price ladder (`CUE_TIERS` is untouched, every older cue keeps its price: the test compares the whole old catalog and `server/prices.json` with commit 919cd9c). Buying = `buyCue(c, "cash", price)`; with too little 💵 the game says "Not enough money" and takes nothing.
- **Pictures.** `tools/make_new_cues.py` (needs numpy, Pillow, opencv: `/usr/bin/python3 tools/make_new_cues.py --apply`) cuts the sheet so that every cue is **as it is in the owner's picture**: thick butt with its metal rings, the body tapering to the thin tip with ferrule and leather tip, and everything that sticks out (8 ball, crown, lion's head, dragon coils). The outline = a **flood fill from outside** (the cue's crisp rim and everything bright is a wall; what the flood cannot reach is the cue; neighbours' ornaments that cross the +-33 px line and sparks hanging on 1-2 px bridges are dropped) **united with a common cone** (the 29 cues are one design: ~14 px at the tip, ~36 px from the upper third down to the butt; dark cues have dim rims the flood leaks through). Then rotated 90 degrees clockwise (tip right, butt left, like the game's other cue pictures), 1160 px long (the same scale for all), lossy WebP with alpha, written to `const CUE_ART3` in `index.html` (~0.6 MB; the build moves them to files like all other pictures). The four cues with an 8 ball show the 8 sideways, because the cue lies on its side (in the game it turns with the aim anyway).
- **Lesson (the owner, Nov 20: "ushlaydigan tomonini ham uraydigan tomonini ham bir xil qilib qo'ygansan"):** the first version came out as a uniform stick (28 px everywhere): a "width never shrinks towards the butt" clamp in the common-cone code carried the narrow rounded end of the butt cap over the whole cue, and I had only looked at small contact sheets. Now `test_new_cues.js` measures the picture itself: >= 40 px at the butt and in the body, <= 30 px at the tip, the tip at most 2/3 of the butt; the old pictures fail it (negative control).
- **Names** are Uzbek and (unlike the older cues) not in the translation table: they show as written in every language (some, like "Muz", are common words that other cues already use; none of the 29 clashes with another cue). **No HD copies** were made (`tools/build_hd.py` would make them; the normal 1160 px picture is used).
- **Server / builds.** Nothing changes on the server (cash is spent on the client and synced like every other 💵 spend). The website / Telegram need Render's Manual Deploy; the Android app a new `.aab`; the portals a new zip.
- **Test:** `node tools/test_new_cues.js` (27 checks, ~2 minutes; `NC_SHOTS=<folder>` saves screenshots): catalog and 💵 prices (200 … 3000, two at 3000, no 💎 / $ price), every older cue and `prices.json` unchanged, the pictures decode and keep the cue's shape (thick butt, thin tip), the Premium page shows a 💵 banknote button and no 💎 / $ button, 1 💵 too little buys nothing and takes nothing, 3 000 💵 buys exactly the 3 000 cue (💎 untouched) and equips it, it is used in a match and listed in the in-match picker, Try works, no page errors.

## New modes: trick shots, lessons, bot levels, 9-ball, 2v2, shot analysis, replay + clip (the owner's list, Nov 22: "buni ham qoshamiz")
- **Where (Nov 23, the owner: the picture goes UNDER the mini-game cards).** On the *Minigames* screen the banner itself (`#miniStrip`, built by `proMiniStrip()` from the same `proFillBanner()` as the full screen) lies **below the game cards**, its five keys working in place (no header key any more); the *Play with friends* screen keeps its header key **"🎱 Yangi rejimlar"**, which opens `#proScr` (built in JS by `buildPro()`). **The screen is the owner's own picture** (Nov 23: *"put the 3rd picture in place of the 4th, exactly like that, it will look beautiful"*): a 2022 x 778 WebP, five illustrated panels in a gold frame (a crowned 8 ball, a rack of balls, a robot with a cue, a glowing 9 ball, two couples with a trophy), kept in `PRO_BAN` (`pro_0_banner.js` in the source parts, a data URI of 270 KB; `tools/build_web.py` moves it out into a file like every other picture), with **five keys lying over its panels** (`.pro-pan`, their place in % of the picture is `PRO_PANELS`): trick shots, lessons, against a bot, 9-ball, 2v2 - in the order of the picture, whatever the language's direction. Each key carries the name of its mode and a line under it (the stars of today's tricks, "3/6 lessons", "easy / medium / hard" ...) in the picture's own units (`cqw`, so the words grow and shrink with it) on a soft dark halo, a glow when pressed, and the red dot `!` until the mode was opened. A tap opens the list of that mode: rows of the leaderboard's kind (`.lbr`) with a small picture each (`proGlyph()`, painted in code from the game's own balls), the analysis is a row of the profile's stat tiles (`.pst`) plus two rows, the replay window has the modal's glossy keys and a gold frame. (Before the picture the screen was five Minigames-style cards with pictures painted in code; they and their painters are gone.) The style sheet uses `html.mh460` / `.mh520` / `.mh560` and `var(--u-vw / --u-vh)` instead of `@media` and `vw / vh` (the page may be turned 90 degrees on a phone held upright), and `#proRep` is zoomed like a `.screen` (`zoom: var(--uiz)`). Everything is in ONE block of `index.html`, between `/*PRO_BEGIN*/` and `/*PRO_END*/` (before the `?perf=1` monitor), plus **two one-line edits** elsewhere: the pause window's "Exit" goes back to `proScr` after a trick shot / a pro match, and the "Tushirilgan N / 15" text says `/ 9` in 9-ball. The block creates its own screen, its own `<style>` and the replay window with JS, so **no markup was added to the page's HTML** (nothing new for the ad network to read; the English-HTML rule is untouched).
- **How it is wired (nothing old was rewritten).** The old functions are *wrapped*: `baseGame`, `enterGameUi`, `nextTurn`, `doShoot`, `resolveShot`, `endGame`, `physicsFrame`, `onRail`, `scoreSim`, `legalTargets`, `drawHUD`, `drawFx`, `go`, `buildMini`, `buildWithFr` (see `proWrap()`); each wrapper catches its own errors and calls the old function when the game is an ordinary one. A pro match is an ordinary `startMatch("pvp", city)` whose game object gets `G.pro` (+ `G.rule = "nine"` / `G.team`) from `PRO_NEXT` inside the `baseGame` wrapper (`PRO_NEXT` expires after 2 minutes, so a stale one never touches a normal match). Trick shots and lessons are a new game type `"trick"` (like `"lucky"`, but with pockets). To take everything out: delete the block and the two edits (and the 77 lines at the end of `tools/xt.txt`, then `python3 tools/build_i18n.py`).
- **Trick shots (5 a day).** The same five for everybody, the day of Tashkent (`dshotDayKey()`, no server): straight, cut, bank, combination, hard combination (`TR_SET`). A layout is **built around the winning shot** (the pocket, the ghost ball, the bounce point of a bank, the helper ball of a combination), the other balls are put where they touch none of those lines, and a layout is kept only when **a band of aims pots in the real physics** (`trRobust`: some aim a with a - 0.25, a, a + 0.25 degrees all potting at power 0.6). It is made in small slices between frames (`trickLayoutG` + `trickGet`: a bank can take 0.2 - 0.7 s on a PC) and cached; the menu asks for the day's five ahead of time. The 8 ball (ringed in gold) must be potted in one shot without scratching; a miss resets the table after 2 s and counts an attempt; **stars 3 / 2 / 1 for the 1st / 2nd / later attempt**; coins `150 / 200 / 300 / 400 / 600` x stars, **paid once per puzzle per day** (replaying only raises the stars); all five done: **+1 💵** (cash is earned, never 💎). State: `profile.pro.trick = {day, st[5], paid[5], bonus}`. `tools/test_pro_modes.js` plays every day of the next year (`DAYS=365`) and fails if one layout cannot be solved.
- **Lessons (6).** Aim, Power (a far pocket: too weak does not get there, too hard bounces out of the jaws), Follow (top spin: the cue ball must pass a line; a plain shot does not), Draw (back spin: the cue ball must come back over a line), Cut, Bank. Fixed layouts (`LESSONS`, `proFixed`), their rule is checked after the shot (`L.goal`), the first pass of each pays 300 coins, all six +1 500. `profile.pro.lessons`. The test checks that follow / draw are NOT solved by a plain shot and ARE solved with spin.
- **Bot levels.** `BOT_LV`: easy / medium / hard = aim noise `G.skill` 0.034 / 0.0115 / 0.0025 (the old `aiTier()` turns it into plain shots / draw + follow / english + planning, so the harder bots also *play* differently). A level match pays **nothing** (no entry fee, no coins, no trophies, no XP, no wins counted) except a **one-time first win of each level** (500 / 2 000 / 5 000 coins); the record (W-L per level) is in `profile.pro.rec`. It ends in our own result window (Play again / Harder / Analysis / Menu), the old result screen is not used.
- **9-ball.** WPA rules, simplified (`resolveNine`): the cue ball must touch the **lowest** ball first, then a ball must be pocketed or **reach a rail after the contact** (`G.shot.rA`, counted by the `onRail` wrapper), the 9 on a legal shot wins (also as a combination), the 9 on a foul is spotted again, a foul gives the opponent the ball in hand; no push-out, no 3-foul rule. The bot plays it (`scoreNine`, `leaveNine`; `legalTargets` returns only the lowest ball). Offline only: against a bot (3 levels) or two people on one phone.
- **2v2.** A side is two players that shoot in turn (`G.team`, `G.mi`; `proTeamTurn` runs in the `nextTurn` wrapper): the order is A1, B1, A2, B2, A1 ... and a side that keeps the table (a pot) keeps its shooter. Three modes: me + a bot partner vs two bots / me + a friend on this phone vs two bots / four people on one phone. **Online 2v2 is done, see the next section** (the room type the plan below talked about is `trooms` in `server/server.js`).
- **Shot analysis.** Every shot of every match (also the old city / online matches) is logged (`proShotStart` after `doShoot`, `proPost` after `resolveShot`): pots, foul, combination / bank, break, a missed easy shot (`bestShotFrom` before the shot), run. After the match a **📊 key** (beside the old result screen's keys, or inside our result window) opens: a row per human player (shots, pots, accuracy, fouls, best run), the **best shot** (score = 100 per ball + 80 combination + 60 bank + 40 break + 150 winning shot) and the **biggest mistake** (lost the match 300 > scratch 120 > foul 80 > easy miss 50), both with a replay key.
- **Replay + 10 s clip.** While a shot rolls, `proRecFrame` stores the ball positions of every physics step (`G.rec`, at most 25 s); only the best and the worst shot keep theirs. The replay window (`#proRep`) paints the table with the game's own `drawTable` into a 960 x 540 canvas (balls, the cue ball's trail, rings where a ball dropped, the player's name, the caption, the game's link), at most 10 s (a longer shot is time-lapsed), with *Again / Slow / Share / Close*. The picture lives in `.pr-view` (a size container: the canvas is the largest 16:9 that fits between the top and the caption + keys), so **nothing can push the table off the screen** (the owner's upright phone, Nov 23: the video preview that used to appear under the keys did). **Share** is described in the section "Shared shots" below: in Telegram a link to the shot goes to the chat picker; a phone's browser that can share files also gets the 10 s clip itself (`proRepRecord`: `MediaRecorder` on `captureStream`, webm vp9 / vp8 or mp4, no sound). The share key and the link on the picture are **not shown on Y8 and the other portals** (`NO_SOCIAL`).
- **Texts.** 77 new lines at the end of `tools/xt.txt` (en, ru and the 13 other languages), `python3 tools/build_i18n.py` rebuilds the table; `tools/test_pro_modes.js` checks that every text of the block has all 15 translations.
- **Scale (the owner, Nov 23: *"pay attention to the scale too"*).** The screens of the new modes were measured on 17 phone / tablet sizes (568x320 ... 1024x768 landscape, and the page turned on an upright phone) and on desktops up to 2560x1440. The Minigames screen is as tall as its cards want to be, so on pages 340-385 px tall (a 360 dp phone held sideways: 800x360) and 415-505 px tall (the big iPhones and Pixel Pro: 932x430, 1004x489) its second row is cut off (the old CSS only has compact cards for <= 385 and for 386-414). **It was not changed** (an old screen; the owner's own phone is 393 px tall, in the band that has its compact cards). The screen of the new modes is never cut off: the picture is the largest one that fits under the title, `width: min(100%, 1100px, (100 * var(--u-vh) - var(--pro-chrome)) * 2.5993)` with the proportions kept by `aspect-ratio` (`--pro-chrome` = what the title row and the paddings take of the page's own height: 142 px, 132 px on phones; 2.5993 = 2022 / 778), so on a 392 px phone it is 765 px wide and nothing scrolls. The lists and the analysis shrink the header of their window with the page (`#modal .panel:has(.pro-list)` / `:has(.pro-an)`: icon, title, text, rows, `clamp(.., calc(k * var(--u-vh) +- c), ..)`), so five or six rows fit without a scroll bar; the analysis rows have the label and the keys on one line and the text under them over the full width (in a narrow window it had been five lines). In the game: the trick-shot plate sits above the rail (it covered the middle pocket), the 9-ball tracker is in the free top-left corner in a dark plate with bigger balls (it had sat on the rail and the pocket), the left team of 2v2 moved off the potted-ball dots. `tools/test_pro_modes.js` section 11 checks all this (17 sizes; the trick-shot and the bot-match result windows too) and fails on the old layout.
- **Test:** `node tools/test_pro_modes.js` (the "miss first" shot of the UI part takes the first aim offset that really misses on THAT day's layout: on 2026-11-28 the fixed +0.35 rad pots the 8 on layout #0 and the test failed (65 / 4) that day, the game was fine; `DAYS=365` for the whole year; 68 checks, ~2 minutes with 10 days; the clip is recorded by `proRepRecord` there, what Share does is `tools/test_replay_share.js`): the daily layouts, the lessons, a trick shot through the UI (miss, reset, stars, coins once, replay, a real webm clip), the bot levels and that an old table match is untouched, 9-ball rules (12 situations) and a whole match, 2v2 order and all three modes, the analysis, the translations, the portal frame (no share key), that the new screen is the owner's picture with five keys over its panels (order, place, words, red dots, a tap opens its list, rows in the lists), and the scale on 17 sizes (the whole picture large and nothing cut off, no scroll bar inside the windows).

## Online 2v2: four phones, one room (the owner, Nov 23: "Online 2v2 ni boshla")
- **Where.** The 2v2 list (New modes -> 2v2 teams) has a fourth row **"🌐 Online: do'stlar bilan"** (`tnOpen()`, window `#tnRoom`, all of it in `pro_6_online.js` inside the PRO block). The window creates a room (a 4-letter code) or joins with a typed code; the room shows two teams of two seats (**Team 1 = seats 0 + 1, Team 2 = seats 2 + 3**; a free seat can be tapped to sit there), the code, **Copy** and **Invite** (Telegram's chat picker, or the share sheet / clipboard on the web; hidden in builds with `NO_SOCIAL`). Invite links: `startapp=t_<code>` in Telegram, `?t=<code>` on the web (`tnInvite()` opens the window once the menu is on screen). The host presses **Start** with at least two phones in the room; **every empty seat is a bot**. After a match "Play again" brings the same people back to the room window (the seat a bot played is free again), "Menu" leaves the room. No coins, no trophies, no ranking: friendly matches (the 2v2 record of `proStore().rec.team` counts them).
- **Server (`server/server.js`, "online 2v2", `trooms`).** Messages: `tcreate`, `tjoin` (first free seat, the host's partner first), `tmove`, `tleave`, `tstart` (host; at least two connected phones; carries the bots' profiles for the empty seats, cleaned), `tresume`, and the relay `taim / tshot / tstate / tchat / tneed / tend` to the **other three** seats (with `from` = the sender's seat and `room` = the match id; chat is cleaned and rate limited). The server knows nothing about billiards. Everything it sends about the room is `troom` (state, host, driver, seats with `away` / `gone`) and `tmatch` (room id, seed, first side, bots, driver). Phones that drop: in the waiting room the seat is kept `TEAM_OPEN_GRACE_MS` (3 min: the share sheet drops the socket), in a match `TEAM_GRACE_MS` (25 s), then the seat is **gone** and a bot plays it; `tresume` takes the seat back (and the match again) inside the grace. The **driver** (the lowest connected human seat, chosen by the server so that every phone agrees) plays the bots' shots. `tend` (the first report) turns the room back into a waiting room. Rooms with nobody connected are removed after 5 minutes.
- **Client.** A match has `G.tnet` (**not** `G.net`: nothing of the 1v1 online code (`netGate`, `turnClash`, results, AFK, ratings) is touched; a plain match, the 1v1 online, the cups and the lobbies run exactly as before). Every phone simulates the same table from the same seed (`rack(seed)`, `simSeed0`); `G.cur`, `G.mi`, `G.groups` are in THIS phone's view (side 0 = my team), everything on the wire is in seats / global sides (`tnGs / tnLs / tnSeat`). The shooter's phone sends the numbers of his shot (`tshot`: the cue ball's position and velocity, spin, `by` = the shooter's seat, `no` = the shot number) and, when his balls have stopped, the table (`tstate`: positions, turn, `mi`, groups, ball in hand, `over / win`); the others play the shot from those numbers (`requestShot(power, remote)`, the same path as the 1v1 opponent's shot) and take the table over when it comes (a table older than their own shot is dropped; **two tables of the same shot number: the lower seat's stands**). Messages are queued and played / applied in order when the balls are at rest (`tnTick`, after `update()`); a message that comes before this phone's `tmatch` is kept (`TN.early`). A bot is a player with `isAI` on the driver's phone and `isAI + remote` on the others (so only one phone thinks); when a seat is gone its player turns into a bot on every phone, and when the driver changes the flags are swapped. A phone that comes back (`tresume`) gets the match again and asks for the table (`tneed`): every phone answers when its balls rest. The turn clock of a human is his own phone's (`timeoutFoul` passes the table and tells it with a `tstate`); a connected phone that freezes without dropping its socket is not replaced until the socket dies (the server pings every 30 s).
- **Tests.** `node server/test_team.js` (36 checks: create / join / full / moves / host change / start / relay / chat cleaning / away / back / gone / end / second match / two phones + two bots / a lone host), `node tools/test_team_online.js` (about 6 minutes, the real server and 4 Chromium phones: the Online row, the window, a typed code and an invite link, four phones at the same table with the same seed and the same seat holding the cue, 8 shots with the table / turn / groups equal on all four after each, a phone closed in the middle: after the grace a bot plays its seat and the driver's phone plays the bot's shot, the end seen in each phone's own view, "Play again", a second match with two phones + two bots; `SHOT=dir` saves pictures), and `tools/test_pro_modes.js` checks the list (4 rows) and the translations (28 new lines in `tools/xt.txt`).
- **Not done / limits.** Spectators of a team match; a voice room; counting three idle turns as "away" (a human who does nothing only loses his clock every turn); replacing a phone that is connected but frozen before its socket dies. **After this change the server must be redeployed** (Render: Manual Deploy); an old page cannot join a room (the page and the server go together).

## Board games: shashka with voice (the owner, Nov 23: "Mini o'yinlarning ichiga shashka, mafiya, shaxmat ... hammasida ovoz bilan gaplashib o'ynaladi")
- **Plan (agreed with the owner).** 1) **Shashka** (done, below) - it also builds the shared parts: the *board game room* (server), the voice room, the "Board games" row; 2) **Magic Chess** (done, below: the 3D Wizard Chess page, with the flat board as fallback; the owner, Nov 23: *only ONE chess, the magic one, no plain chess*): the rules of chess with our own pieces and a spell when a piece is taken (**never the name or the pictures of "Harry Potter"**: a trademark of Warner Bros; a store or an ad network can take the whole game down for it); 3) **mafia** (6-8 players, hidden roles kept on the server, night / day, voting; the voice room is the point of it). First versions have no coin stakes (a stake would need the server to check every move). Voice with strangers needs mute / report: for now rooms are private (a code).
- **Where.** Mini-games screen -> the row **"Stol o'yinlari" / Board games** under the new-modes picture (`shMiniRow()`, `#miniBoards`; more cards go into the same row) -> the Checkers card opens a list: against the bot (easy / medium / hard), two people on one phone, online with a friend (voice). The card's picture is painted in code (`shArt()`), nothing is added to the page's size.
- **Rules (shashka = Russian draughts).** Core in `pro_7_shashka.js`, between `/*SH_CORE_BEGIN*/` and `/*SH_CORE_END*/`, without any page code (`tools/test_shashka.js` loads it on its own): 8x8, the dark cells, a man steps forward and captures **in all four directions**, a king flies and captures from a distance, **capturing is compulsory** (any route, but a route is played to its end), the captured pieces stay until the route is over and are not jumped twice (Turkish strike), a man that reaches the far row in the middle of a capture goes on as a king, a king must land where it can go on; the side that cannot move loses; 3 repetitions or 30 half-moves of kings only without a capture are a draw. A move is `{path: [from, ...landings], caps: [...]}`; over the wire a move is its path, the other phone looks it up among the legal moves (`shFind`).
- **The bot.** Negamax with alpha-beta and iterative deepening (man 100 / king 320, a man's advance, the back row, the centre; captures are compulsory so the tree is thin): easy = depth 2 + noise + 18% random moves, medium = depth 4, hard = up to depth 12 inside 0.9 s. It accepts a draw when it is not clearly better. Colours are random, a rematch swaps them. "Undo" takes back my move and its answer (the game is replayed from the move list).
- **The screen (`pro_8_shashka_ui.js`, `#shScr`).** A DOM overlay: 64 cells that take the taps and pieces drawn over them; my colour is at the bottom (the board turns round for Black); tap a piece, then its landing cells: the moment only one route is left it is played whole (a forced continuation needs no more taps), a compulsory capture is announced and the pieces that have to capture glow, a piece that cannot capture is refused; the move slides along its route and the taken pieces fade. Keys: mic / speaker (online), undo (not online), draw, resign, exit. Sizes use `--u-vh` / `--u-vw` like the other PRO windows (an upright phone turns the page). Toasts and windows (`#modal`, `#toasts`) are raised above these screens (`body:has(#shScr.on,#tnRoom.on,#brRoom.on)`): before, a toast of the 2v2 room window was hidden behind it.
