- **Board game rooms (`server/server.js`, "board game rooms", `brooms`).** `bcreate {game}` (a code of 4 letters, 2 seats for a board game; `BG_MAX` says how many seats a game has: mafia will use up to 8), `bjoin`, `bleave`, `bstart` (host), `bmove {path}`, `bover {winner, why}`, `boffer {draw | yes | no}`, `brematch`, `bresume`, `bvoice`. The server keeps the **move list** of the match and lets only the seat whose turn it is move; it does not know the rules (a phone that receives an illegal move asks for the list again). A phone that was away gets `bmatch` + `bsync {moves}` and **rebuilds its game from the server's list** when it differs (so a reloaded page: Online -> the room window -> the same position). Grace: a phone that drops keeps its seat 3 min in the waiting room, `BROOM_GRACE_MS` (30 s) in a match, then it **forfeits** (`bend`, reason `left`); leaving in a match forfeits at once. A rematch (`brematch` from both) starts at once with the colours swapped. Invite links: `startapp=b_<code>` / `?b=<code>`.
- **Voice (`RoomVoice`, `RV` in `pro_9_room.js`).** A mesh of WebRTC calls between the seats of the room (2 for a board game, up to 8 later), the server only relays the signalling (`bvoice`: hello / offer / answer / ice / bye / mute, the same kinds as the lobby's voice room, whose code it follows: the smaller user id offers, one `<audio>` per friend unlocked inside the tap, the app's one microphone `MicHub`, a refused microphone -> listen-only + the same explanation windows `Voice.micFail`). When one of the two taps 🎤 the other, with the speaker on, **listens at once without a tap** and can then tap 🎤 himself. The keys are in the room window and on the board screen. Telegram's iOS browser has no microphone: listen-only there (as in the lobby).
- **Tests.** `node tools/test_shashka.js` (27 checks: the start, perft 7 / 49 / 302 / 1469, compulsory capture, backward capture, routes, no piece jumped twice, flying kings, promotion by a step / by a capture / in the middle of a capture, end of the game, draws, the bot's legal moves in ~300 positions and a win in one, mid beats easy, hard is not worse than mid; ~80 s), `node tools/test_shashka_ui.js` (38 checks: the row and the list, the board on 5 screens, a finger, compulsory capture, routes, the end / resign / draw windows, the bot on 3 levels, undo, the colours swap), `node server/test_board.js` (31 checks), `node tools/test_board_online.js` (33 checks, 2 Chromium phones with a fake microphone: room, code, voice **audio bytes both ways**, mute, the colours, 12 moves with equal boards, a draw offer no / yes, rematch with swapped colours, a reload in the middle of a match, resignation, leaving; `SHOT=dir` saves pictures).
- **Not done yet.** Mafia (see the plan); a move clock; spectators; a voice room for strangers (mute / report); chat text. **The server must be redeployed** (Render: Manual Deploy) for the rooms.

## Magic Chess ("Sehrli shaxmat", Nov 23): the only chess of the game
- **What it is.** Chess with a magic look; there is **no plain chess** (the owner's decision). The Board games row under the mini-games has two cards: Checkers and Magic Chess (`BGLIST`); the card opens the same list as shashka: three bot levels, two on one phone, online with a friend (voice).
- **Rules (`pro_a_magic.js`, between `/*MC_CORE_BEGIN*/` and `/*MC_CORE_END*/`, no page code).** All of chess: castling (not out of / through / into check, the rights kept in `cas`), en passant (only right after the double step), promotion to a queen / rook / bishop / knight, check / checkmate / stalemate, draws by threefold repetition, the 50-move rule and no mating material (K v K, K + a minor piece v K, bishops of one colour). A square is `row * 8 + col` (a8 = 0), pieces 1 pawn ... 6 king (White positive). A move is `{f, t, p, k, c}`; over the wire it is `[from, to]` (+ the promotion's index in `MC_PROMO` = queen, rook, bishop, knight as a third number: the server's path check allows it). `mcFindIn` looks a received path up among the legal moves.
- **Tested against the known numbers.** `tools/test_magic.js` runs **perft** (every legal move sequence counted) on five positions of the chess-programming suite: the start (20 / 400 / 8902 / 197281), Kiwipete (48 / 2039 / 97862), the end-game with pins (14 / 191 / 2812 / 43238), the promotion position (6 / 264 / 9467) and position 5 (44 / 1486 / 62379): castling, en passant, promotions, pins and checks are all in them. Then scholar's / fool's mate, stalemate, the castling refusals, en passant, the four promotions, the draws, and the bot.
- **The bot.** Negamax with alpha-beta, a quiet-position search on captures, captures tried first, material + piece-square tables (the well-known "simplified evaluation"), iterative deepening inside a time budget: easy = depth 1 + noise + 20% random moves, medium = depth 3 (0.7 s), hard = up to depth 6 (1.4 s). It finds mate in one (also the back-rank mate and a rook mate), takes a free queen, accepts a draw when it is not clearly better.
- **The look (`pro_b_magic_ui.js`, `#mcScr`).** The pieces are drawn in code as SVG (`MC_LAYERS`, one list of paths per kind, gradients ivory-gold for White and violet for Black): **pawn = an apprentice in a pointed hat, knight = a unicorn, bishop = a wizard with a star on his hat, rook = a tower, queen = a sorceress's crown, king = a sun king with a cross**. They have a soft glow (gold / violet), the selected one floats. The board is enchanted (violet and lavender, labels a-h / 1-8 in the corners, my colour at the bottom). Cells: the last move is gold, the selected piece's cell is outlined, a free cell it can go to is a cyan dot, an enemy piece it can take has a red ring, a king in check glows red and the message says *Check!*. **A capture is a spell:** the piece glides there and lunges, a ring of light spreads from the cell, 14 sparks fly, the taken piece bursts (blurs, grows and fades) and the board shakes; castling moves the rook with the king; a promotion sparkles and a window asks what the pawn becomes (four pieces drawn like the board's); a mate is sparks, a flash of lightning and the result window with the reason. Under each player: the pieces he has taken. Keys: mic / speaker (online), undo (not online), draw, resign, exit. The same sizes as shashka (`--u-vh` / `--u-vw`: an upright phone turns the page).
- **Online.** The same board game room (`BG_MAX = {shashka: 2, magic: 2}` on the server), move list, resume and voice as shashka; since there are now two games the client's wire code is generic: `BGS[game]` (state `U`, `open`, `remote`, `rules {new, find, play}`, `endNow`, `render`, `after`), `brGame()` = the game on screen, `bgSend / bgOver / bgRematch`. A new board game = its rules + a screen + `BGS.<id>` + a `BGLIST` card (+ `BG_MAX` on the server). Invite text: *Sehrli shaxmat o'ynaymiz! Xona kodi: {0}*.
- **Tests.** `node tools/test_magic.js` (31 checks, ~2 min), `node tools/test_magic_ui.js` (42 checks: the row, the list, 32 pieces of six kinds, a finger, check, the spell, castling, promotion with its picker and cancel, mate with the lightning, stalemate, a draw, a resignation, the bot on 3 levels, undo, the colours swap, 5 screen sizes), `node tools/test_magic_online.js` (2 phones with a fake microphone: the room titled Magic Chess, the voice, 24 plies of random moves with a taste for captures / castles / promotions with equal boards, a promotion over the wire, a draw, a rematch with swapped colours, a resignation, leaving; `SHOT=dir` saves pictures). Also changed: `tools/test_shashka_ui.js` / `test_board_online.js` still pass through the generic wire code.

## The 3D Wizard Chess in the game (the owner, Nov 23: "Mana tayyor": the finished 3D chess is the chess of the Board games row)
- **What it is.** `wizard-chess/index.html` (made on Oct 29-30 in an earlier session, branch `ccr-0d4fca54-c8q2b8`: a candle-lit / sunlit hall, pieces that carry their own weapons and fight, its own alpha-beta engine in a worker, camera presets, sound) now ships with the game: **Mini-games -> Board games -> Magic Chess** opens it in a full-screen frame (`#wzScr`, `pro_c_wizard.js`). Never the name or the pictures of "Harry Potter" (a trademark): the page's own pieces / hall are ours. The flat Magic Chess board of `pro_b` stays as the **fallback** (same card, same room, same rules, same voice) where the page is not there (the Android app, the portals' zips) or WebGL is off: `WZ.ok` is decided once in the background (a canvas with WebGL + `GET wizard-chess/ok.json`); `window.__WZ_OFF = true` forces the flat board (the tests of the flat board set it).
- **Changes made to the page (marked in the file).** `<html lang="en">` and an English loading text (the rule of CLAUDE.md: the HTML a page ships is English; the page sets the live language itself: uz / en / ru, the game passes `lang=`); **three.js r159 (MIT) is kept next to it** (`wizard-chess/three.min.js`, taken from the npm package, no CDN: it works offline and in the app); `[hidden]{display:none!important}` (`.modal` set `display:grid`, so the promotion and the result windows showed at once); the **embed mode** `?embed=1&mode=ai|2p|net&human=1|-1&level=0..3&lang=&quick=1` (no saved game, `quick` = the camera sweep and the pieces' arrival are skipped for the next game of a session) and the **bridge**: `{wc:1, t:"move", uci}` out when I move in `net` mode, `{t:"apply", uci}` / `{t:"load", moves}` / `{t:"lock"}` in, `{t:"ready"}` out when the hall is ready, `{t:"fail"}` out when the frame cannot draw it (WebGL refused in the frame, or its context was lost: `wzFail()` hides the frame and opens the flat board with the same game, an online one keeps its moves; `WZ.ok = false` so the next games open the flat board at once), `sound=0` in the frame's address when the game is muted (`profile.muted`: the hall starts silent, its own sound key still works); in `net` mode the page hides its computer / take-back / new-game keys and does not show its own result window.
- **How the three modes work.** Against the computer (its own engine: easy / medium / hard = its levels 0 / 1 / 2) and two on one phone the page plays by itself. **Online with a friend** (`net`): the page only draws and takes MY moves; **the game keeps the rules** (`MCU.G` = the core of `pro_a`: `mcFind` / `mcPlay` check every move that comes either way, so the two engines must agree: a test compares them), the room, the move list (resume: the page is told `load`), the draw offers, the resignation, the result window (after the page's own mate banner) and the **voice** (RoomVoice keys lie over the hall). A move's text `e7e8q` <-> the game's path `[from, to, choice]` (`mcUciOfPath` / `mcPathOfUci`).
- **Served by** `server/server.js` (`STATIC`: `/wizard-chess/`, `index.html`, `three.min.js`, `ok.json`) **and by the static build**: the Telegram Mini App's page is NOT served by Render but by Cloudflare Workers static assets, which are `python3 tools/build_web.py web_site` (README "Hosting layout"), so `tools/build_web.py` copies `wizard-chess/` into every folder it builds (and `web/_headers` says `ok.json` is `no-store`). **The owner's screenshot of Nov 24** (the flat board in Telegram after the deploy, the frame fix already there = the new page): the game looks for `wizard-chess/ok.json` on the host that served the page, and Cloudflare's build had no such folder, so `WZ.ok` was false. `tools/test_web_serve.js` checks that `web/wizard-chess` equals `wizard-chess` byte for byte (rebuild `web/` after changing the page). The Android build and the portals do not carry the folder: they use the flat board.
- **Why the flat board opened (the reason is kept).** `WZ.why` = `nogl` (no WebGL in the page), `portal` / `file` (a portal or the app: by design, no note), `http404` (the host has no 3D folder), `json` (ok.json is not the file we expect), `net` (the request failed), `fail-webgl` / `fail-lost` (the frame could not draw the hall / lost its context). Where a 3D hall was expected the flat board shows **one small note per session: `3D: <reason>`** (`wzTell()`), so a phone that does not get the hall can say why.
- **Tests.** `node tools/test_wizard_chess.js` (about 12 minutes: the browser draws the hall in software, ~20 s to open it): the page is found, WebGL, the two engines have the same legal moves in every position of 6 random games (~900 positions, promotions included), a finger plays in the hall (the test finds the point where the page's own ray picks the piece / the cell), the computer answers, the close key frees the hall, the flat fallback with the page off / missing, then 2 phones on the real server: the frames asked for `mode=net`, one White one Black, a move made in one hall is played in the other and both rules cores have the same list, voice over the hall (audio bytes), a draw offer, a rematch (colours swap, `quick=1`), a reload of one page (the hall comes back from the server's move list), a resignation, leaving. (`__wcMaxDt` lets a frame count as half a second in this slow software rendering, `__wcPR` draws it at half the pixel ratio; `PART=A` runs only the one-phone part, about 5 minutes.) Also: a muted game opens the hall with `sound=0` and its sound key says off; a `{t:"fail"}` from the frame puts the flat board in its place; a 412 x 915 window (the page turned) plays `e2e4` with a real touch.

- **The owner's phone: a portrait window with the page turned (Nov 23, "Bomadiyu" + a screenshot of the flat Magic Chess lying on its side).** Every screenshot he sends of the game (the Mini-games screen, the 2v2 room, the chess) is a **portrait** window (412 x 915 css px) in which the page is turned 90 degrees (`html.rot`, `resize()`: `rotated = touch && innerHeight > innerWidth`): he holds the phone **sideways** and reads the turned page, as for the rest of the game ("the game always plays in landscape"). So the board screens are **not** made upright: they stay in the game's turned frame, like every other screen (an upright board would have been lying on its side for him). What the screenshot's "Bomadiyu" was about: the Render site did not have the 3D page yet (it showed the flat fallback). To see what he sees: open the game in a **412 x 915 touch window** (`isMobile: true, hasTouch: true`) and turn the screenshot 90 degrees counter-clockwise (the page's top is the screen's right edge, its left the screen's top). **The 3D page works in the turned page** (checked on the real page with a touch tap through the turn: the finger's screen point (167, 475) arrives in the frame as (475, 245), the page's ray picks the pawn, `e2e4` is played and the computer answers `b8c6`); the hall is drawn in the page's landscape shape (915 x 412) with the keys at its bottom-right.
- **A framed avatar no longer sticks out of the player card (the owner's screenshot: the opponent's gold frame stood out of its card, and the card was taller than mine).** The slot `.sh-av` of both board screens is fixed (60 x 52), a framed avatar gets `margin: 0` and is scaled (`.62`; the small `pp_*` frames `.8`) so the **painted** part of every frame lies inside it. The painted parts were measured on all 128 `FRAMES` at a 44 px avatar (a flat background, the picture's opaque bounds): the widest frame (`fp_7`) is 2.14 x the picture, the highest (`fs_1`, `vf_*`) 1.7 x; the image boxes (139% / 272%) are much bigger than what is painted, so the audit's `.36` of the compact rows would have made the picture a dot here. Checked: all 128 frames in both cards at 915 x 412, 412 x 915 (turned) and 360 x 640: nothing leaves the slot or the card, both cards keep the same height.

## Shashka looks like real shashka (the owner, Nov 24: "make the shashka look like shashka too, then you move on to mafia")
- **The board** is a walnut frame round maple (light) and walnut (dark) cells with wood grain and thin cell edges; the labels a-h / 1-8 sit in the cells' corners and follow the board when it turns for Black. A **piece** is a disc with a thick edge (`--side`), a shadow under it, a shine (`::before`) and two grooves (`::after`): ivory for White, ebony for Black; a king keeps the gold crown and gets a gold ring; the piece that is picked up is lifted (`.pc.up`). The card of the Board games row is painted the same way (`shArt`). Rules, taps and layout are untouched (`pro_8_shashka_ui.js`). Checked on the owner's view (a 412 x 915 window, the picture turned back).

## Mafia ("Mafiya", Nov 24): hidden roles, night and day, with voice
- **What it is.** The third card of the Board games row. 5-8 players in a room (the same room as shashka: a 4-letter code, the invite link, voice); the host starts, **bots top the table up to 5**; "with bots (alone)" makes a room and starts it at once. Roles for 5 / 6-7 / 8 seats: 1 / 2 / 3 mafia, a doctor, a sheriff (*komissar*), the rest citizens. Phases: reveal (the role) -> **night** (each mafia names a victim, the doctor a person to save, the sheriff a person to check) -> **day** (talk, voice open; everybody who is alive can press "ready") -> **vote** (one person or nobody) -> night ... The victim is the one most of the mafia named (a tie: at random); a victim the doctor saved, or no victim at all, is "nobody died in the night" (nobody is told why); the doctor cannot save the same person two nights in a row; the sheriff cannot check the same person twice; a tied vote sends nobody out; a person who dies is shown with his role. **Town wins when no mafia is left, the mafia win when they are as many as the others.** A phase never ends sooner than its minimum even when everybody has acted (a fast night would tell the town that nobody special is left). Times: reveal 8 s, night 35 s, day 60 s, vote 30 s (`MAFIA_SCALE` shortens them for the tests).
- **Secrecy.** The roles live **only on the server** (`server/mafia.js`, a pure state machine: `create / act / tick / bot / view`). A phone is sent only `view(g, seat)` (`mview`): its own role, its partners if it is mafia (and what they chose at night), the sheriff's own findings (a person and "mafia / not", never a role), who is dead and what they were, every role only at the end. `server/test_mafia_rules.js` scans 40 whole games' views: only the allowed fields, no other living seat's role anywhere.
- **Server** (`server/server.js`, "MAFIA in a board game room"): `BG_MAX.mafia = 8`; `bstart` (the host, one person is enough) deals the seats (the people first, then the bots `bot:<seat>`), `mact {k: kill | save | check | vote | ready, to}` is one seat's action (rate-limited), the phases run on timers, the bots decide at their own moments of the night / the vote, a phone that drops can come back (`bresume`: `bmatch` + `mview`), **a phone that leaves or does not come back is replaced by a bot** and the match goes on (no human left: the room closes), the end sends the last view with every role and the room is a waiting room again (the bots are gone, the host starts the next one). `mview` carries the server's clock (`now`) so a phone can count the phase down.
- **Phone** (`pro_d_mafia.js`; the game's own `MF = 12` exists, so the state is `MFX`): the screen `#mfScr` (the seats as tiles with the avatar and its frame, a badge line: dead + role, my findings, the votes against a seat, a bot; the phase with its clock bar; my role card; what to do now; a short log; keys: ready / nobody, mic, speaker, exit), a **tap on a seat is the action of the phase** (night: a mafia names a victim, the doctor a person to save, the sheriff a person to check; the evening: the vote, tap again to take it back); the night is dark blue, the day warm, the result window lists every role. The room window of a Mafia room has 8 seats (`brRender` is wrapped for it). Never a role the phone may not know is drawn.
- **Voice** (`RV.setGate`): the **night is silent except between the mafia** (a mafia's voice goes only to the other mafia and he hears only them), a dead player only listens, the day and the vote are for everybody who is alive, the reveal is silent, the end is for everybody. It is enforced at the sender (the track is taken off the call to a phone that may not hear) and at the receiver (its audio element is muted); `RV.state().peers[].to / from` show it to the tests.
- **Texts:** 36 new texts in 15 languages (`tools/xt.txt`; the page's own `T()` for the role names, the prompts, the log lines, the result).
- **Tests.** `node server/test_mafia_rules.js` (49 checks: the roles, the night, the vote, the two wins, refused actions, no leak in 40 games of views, 600 whole games of bots), `node server/test_mafia.js` (24 checks on the real server: the room, the match with bots, only the own view, a phone that comes back, a leaver replaced by a bot, alone with bots, 8 people), `node tools/test_mafia_ui.js` (31 checks, about 3 minutes: the card and its list, a whole match through the screen against bots, no role icon on a seat that may not show one, the result window, "Play again" -> the room with 8 seats, the exit key, five screen sizes incl. the turned page, two phones with voice: the gate at night and by day, the same result on both). The two board UI tests know the third card.

## Board game rooms in the world chat (the owner, Nov 24: "make it so the lobby of shashka / chess / mafia can be posted to the world chat, people gather faster; friends can be invited")
- **Posting.** The host of an open board room (shashka, Magic Chess, Mafia) has a **🌐 key** in the room window next to the invite key (not in the portals' builds: no world chat there). `POST /api/world/lobby {board: 1}` (the same limits as a billiards lobby: once per 45 s for a room, a burst of 3 then one per 2 minutes; the room must be open, its host the caller, a seat free). The card lives while the room is open (at most 30 minutes) and leaves the list when the room starts, closes or its host leaves.
- **The list.** `GET /api/world` has the posts as cards `{kind: "board", game, n, max, open, host...}`; the world chat window draws a board card with the game (`♟️ Checkers / ✨ Magic Chess / 🕵️ Mafia`), the seats `n/max`, the host with his league and the Join key (no entry fee). **The room's code is never in the list** (an opaque `wid` only).
- **Join = a question to the host** (the billiards lobby's flow, shared): `POST /api/world/join {wid}` -> the host gets the ✓ / ✗ banner wherever he is -> only a ✓ makes `GET /api/world/req` return the code and the game; the asking phone opens the room window (`shRoomOpen(game, code)`) and takes a seat. Full / gone / declined / no answer are told as before.
- **Friends** are invited with the 📤 key (the Telegram share link / invite text of every game, `startapp=b_<code>`) as before; the world chat is for strangers.
- Test: `node tools/test_world_board.js` (28 checks, about 2 minutes: 3 phones on the real server: the 🌐 key, the card (game, 1/2, host, Join), the code is not in the list, the flood limit, the banner, a yes puts Bob in the room, a full room's card is closed, the card leaves when the match starts, a Mafia card 1/8, a no, the host leaves). The old `tools/test_world_chat.js` (64 checks) still passes.

## Nov 26: Mafia has sounds, shashka in the owner's reference look + 3D, air hockey, room codes in the chat
**The owner's message (Nov 25-26).** (1) *"In shashka there are the sounds of play, doesn't Mafia have any?"* and the Mafia screen just stood there after the end. (2) A picture of a shashka he likes: *"make it like this, 3D"*. (3) *"Then table tennis; the lobby code of 9-ball goes into the chat, that needs fixing too; quality play in shashka, chess, Mafia"*. (4) A video of a 3D air hockey (Japan vs South Korea, first goal wins): *"there is air hockey, add that one too"*. Then: *"flawless, do not change the other places, prepare it carefully"* - so only these screens were touched.
- **Why the board games were silent (found while doing 1): `Snd.clack / tick / foul / rail / pocket ...` are switched OFF on the menu screens** (`mode === "menu"`: they are the sounds of the background demo match behind the menu), and shashka, Magic Chess and Mafia run on menu-mode screens. So the piece sound of shashka (`Snd.clack`) and the warning (`Snd.foul`) never played. New sounds that are NOT switched off (`Snd` in index.html): `piece(cap)` (a wooden tap, a double one for a capture), `warn()`, `puck(v)`, `wallHit(v)`, `horn()` and, for Mafia, `night()`, `day()`, `knife()`, `heal()`, `bell()`, `knock()`, `reveal()`. Shashka and flat Magic Chess now use `piece` / `warn` (the 3D chess has its own sounds).
- **Mafia sounds** (`mfSfx` in `pro_d_mafia.js`, called when the phase changes): the roles are dealt (`reveal`), the night falls (`night`; after a vote the `bell` of the expelled one or a `knock` for a tie comes first), the morning (a `knife` when somebody was killed, with a `lose` sound for the victim; a `heal` chime when nobody died, then `day`), the vote (`bell`), the end (`win` / `lose` for my team), a knock when I pick a seat, a tick in the last 5 seconds of the day / vote. **The end:** the result window comes 0.9 s after the last view (it was 1.4 s) and the screen has its own **"Play again" key** (the green `go` key, which is "to the vote" in the day) so it never just stands there. Test (`tools/test_mafia_ui.js`): the sounds of a whole match are counted (one `reveal`, the night, the day, the bell, one win or lose, a knife or a rescue every morning) and the end key is there.
- **Shashka in the reference look (`#shScr`, `pro_8_shashka_ui.js`: only shashka; Magic Chess / Mafia / the rest keep their looks):** a table of dark wood, an ebony frame with a bevel, **light squares of pale veined stone and dark squares of black stone** (painted by the browser from an SVG noise: `SH_LT` / `SH_DK`, no pictures to download), thick ivory and black discs with two grooves on the top, gold marks for the turn / the last move / the landing cells, and the opponent's turn is a **pill with three waiting dots**. **The 3D key** (`data-a="tilt"`, remembered in `profile.sh3d`): the board stands up in a tilted view from my side (`.tilt3d`: `perspective` + `rotateX(41deg)`), the taps still hit the right cell (the browser maps them through the tilt: tested with real clicks), the discs' thickness grows. **The hint key** (💡, bot / two on one phone only, never against a person): the best move of the hard bot lights the piece and its cell for 2 s. The default view is the one from above (like the picture); the 3D key is the real 3D. (A real three.js scene with an orbiting camera, like the 3D chess, was not made: say so if it is wanted.)
- **Air hockey** (`pro_f_hockey.js`, the "Arcade games" row under the Board games picture of the mini-games screen: since Nov 27 the owner's picture with a key over each card, see "The Arcade row is the owner's picture" below): a table in perspective (a pinhole camera over a plane of 200 x 100 cm, drawn on ONE 1280 x 720 canvas that is scaled to the screen, so the same code works on a phone turned 90 degrees and on a monitor), dark blue with dots, red centre line and circle, blue lines, the goal slots with the lamp over each (it lights after a goal), the light at my goal; a puck; two mallets with the **flag of their player** on the knob (my country and a random one for the bot, as in the video: the HUD has both flags, `0 : 0`, "AIR HOCKEY", "country · country - First goal wins"); **after a goal the camera zooms to the scorer's mallet and "Japan SCORES!"** comes up, the lamp lights, a horn; the match is the **first goal** (as in the video), **3** or **7**; against a **bot** (easy / mid / hard: the speed of its mallet 170 / 290 / 430 cm/s, how often it thinks, how far it misses; it defends on a line before its goal using the predicted puck path with the walls folded back, and when the puck is in its half and slow it goes round it, gets behind it and hits it at the other goal) or **two on one phone** (each finger has its own half). The physics (`hkStep`): 4 sub-steps, rails 0.94, the goal only through the slot, a mallet hits with the speed it has (`e = 0.9`), a finger moves the mallet at most 560 cm/s (it cannot teleport) and it cannot cross the centre line, a puck that has stopped for 4.5 s is pushed back into play. **The finger is mapped to the table through three marks at the canvas' corners** (`hkPoint` idea: `.hk-m`), so it works under any CSS turn / zoom of the page (tested on a 915 x 412 phone and on the 412 x 915 turned page). No coins are paid against the bot. Results are kept (`proStore().rec["hk_<level>"]`). Table tennis is the second card of the row (see "Table tennis" below).
- **A room code written into the world chat** (the owner's 9-ball / "lobby code in the chat" line; 9-ball itself is still bot / two on one phone only, its online lobby does not exist): a chat message that is only a code (4 letters = a board room, 6 = a billiards lobby, also with "KOD:" / "CODE:") now gets a green **Join** key under it (`codeOf` / `codeJoin` in the WorldChat code); it closes the chat and joins (`lobbyJoin` / `shRoomOpen(game, code)`), and says "leave the current lobby first" when the player is in one. An ordinary message has no key. Test: `tools/test_world_board.js` (+4 checks).
- **Tests:** `node tools/test_hockey.js` (29 checks, ~1.5 min: the way in, the camera round trip and the perspective, the finger on the table (also on the turned page), the centre line, the speed limit, rails, goal slot, mallet hit, the sounds, the goal (0:1, lamp, "SCORES!", one horn), the serve, the goal limit and the result window, Play again, two fingers, the bot's speed / attack / defence per level), `tools/test_shashka_ui.js` (+5: the 3D key tilts and is remembered, a tap through the tilt, the hint), `tools/test_mafia_ui.js` (+3), `tools/test_world_board.js` (+4). Texts: 13 new lines in `tools/xt.txt` (`pro_xt7` - `pro_xt9`, 15 languages).

## Render deploy failure of Nov 26 (the owner's e-mail "We encountered an error during the deploy process for billiard8", commit "Table tennis ...")
Render's logs are not visible from here, so what was done is what could be checked: (1) a clean clone of that commit, `npm install` in `server/` exactly like `render.yaml` (`rootDir: server`), starts, answers `/api/health` and `/api/tc`, **also on Node 20** (`NODE_VERSION` of render.yaml; checked with the `node@20` npm package); the commit changed no file of `server/` (the page, the tests, the texts), so it is not a code error of that commit. (2) **A memory regression, fixed, but it was NOT the cause of the failed deploys (see (5)): the stake commit (Nov 24) made the server read the whole 50 MB `index.html` at start to cut two blocks out of it (`boardrules.js`): +100 MB of RAM for good (RSS 155 MB instead of 53 MB, peak 203 MB instead of 97 MB on an empty database; the real database in memory comes on top of that), which on the free 512 MB plan can end in "out of memory" while the new instance starts.** Now the rules are a 22 KB file (`server/boardcores.js`, made by `tools/build_cores.js`, kept in step by `server/test_boardcores.js`), and the server is back at 53 MB / 97 MB. (3) `/api/health` now also says `node` (the version), `memMB` (the resident memory in MB) and `stakes` (the stake rooms of shashka / chess are on): open `https://billiard8.onrender.com/api/health` after a deploy to see them. (4) Still to know: **a clone of the repository was 1.9 GB** (cleaned the same day, now ~400 MB: see "History cleanup of Nov 26" below; an earlier version of this line blamed the 50 MB `index.html` and `bilyard8-y8.zip`, which was wrong); the log of a failed deploy (Render -> billiard8 -> Events -> the failed deploy -> Logs, scroll to the bottom) says what happened. (5) **THE REAL CAUSE of the failed deploys (the owner's screenshots of that log, Nov 26 21:52): `Could not load the database: Your account or project has exceeded the quota. Upgrade your plan to increase limits.` and then `==> Exited with status 1`.** That is the Neon project (the free Postgres behind `DATABASE_URL`) being over a quota (compute hours, data transfer or storage: Neon console -> the project -> Billing / Usage shows which one and when it resets). `server.js` exits with status 1 when `store.load()` fails, ON PURPOSE (it must never start with an empty database and overwrite real rows), so EVERY new instance fails, whatever the code or the size of the repository. The last deploy that went live is `a62a649` (old hash, Nov 24); the failing ones include commits that changed no file of `server/`. The `SECURITY WARNING ... verify-full` lines above it in the log are only a notice about `sslmode=require` in `pg` 8.x, not the cause. **While the database is unreachable the OLD instance keeps running from memory and retries its save every 2 s, so when the quota is lifted it writes everything it holds: do NOT restart or redeploy the service before that** (a restart cannot start and loses what was not saved; a free instance that has had no request for 15 min spins down and cannot start again either, so an uptime pinger on `/api/health` every 5 min keeps it up). Ways out: a paid Neon plan, waiting for the reset, or another Postgres (tables `users, purchases, meta, clubs, dms, p2p, replays`, each `id text, data jsonb, updated timestamptz`; there is no copy script yet). Every full start also reads the whole database (data transfer), so many deploys and spin-downs add up.

## History cleanup of Nov 26 (the owner: "can the 1.9 GB of old pushes be deleted?")
**What it was.** A clone of the repository downloaded **1.9 GB** (GitHub's own figure: 1958 MB); every Render / Cloudflare build and every new session clones it. **It was NOT the 50 MB `index.html`**: its 800+ versions are stored as deltas, 177 MB in all (the old note "~37 MB per commit" only measured the loose objects of one working copy), and **not `bilyard8-y8.zip`** (ignored, never committed). **1.6 GB were 56 test screenshots at the repo root** (`all_*.png` 22 tables, `modal_*.png` 22 windows, `open_*.png`, `tr_*.png`, `wk_*.png`): committed by accident on Oct 1 - 8, overwritten and committed again by every test run (50 - 60 versions each), deleted on Oct 17 (commit "... remove stray test screenshots ...") and put in `.gitignore`, but still inside the old commits.
**What was done (with the owner's OK).** `git filter-repo --invert-paths --paths-from-file <the 56 names>` over all three branches (14 commits that held only those files became empty and went: 1028 -> 1014 commits), then ONE atomic force-push with a lease on each tip (`claude/shunaqa-oyin-yaratish-5dujy5`, `ccr-4e16287f-vf75ee`, `ccr-0d4fca54-c8q2b8`). **Proved before the push, for EVERY commit:** the file list (mode, blob, path) equals the old one minus the 56 names; author, dates, messages and parents equal; the tips' file trees are identical (`d5c82b55...` and `7f8d4207...`); `git fsck` clean. **After the push** a fresh clone from GitHub: **398 MiB in 27 s** (was ~1.9 GB), the same tip tree, fsck clean. The game, the server and `web/` did not change by one byte (`web/.src` = sha1 of `index.html` is still `f1f62ef5...`).
**What changed for people.** **Every commit hash is new** (the old tip `884bbd7` is now `8618ad8`); hashes written in older notes or in zip names (`bilyard8-y8-v1.8-d81ea92.zip` = now `ad9856c` ...) do not exist any more, and the old-to-new list is not kept in the repo. A session that still holds the OLD history: `git fetch origin && git reset --hard origin/<branch>` (or clone again), never push it back. GitHub's size figure (1958 MB) does not drop at once: the old objects stay on its servers until it collects garbage and PR #1's refs (`refs/pull/1/*`) keep them; clones do not download them. If the figure matters, ask GitHub Support to run a garbage collection and drop the PR #1 refs.
**So that it does not happen again.** Test scripts write their screenshots to the scratchpad or `/tmp`, never into the repo; never `git add -A` / `git add .` with test output around (look at `git status` and `git diff --cached --stat` before every commit); `.gitignore` keeps `all_*.png modal_*.png open_*.png tr_*.png wk_*.png` and the zips out. A quick size check: `git count-objects -vH` (size-pack) and a test clone.

## The Mafia screen: the owner's pictures (four roles, the night street, the town square, the alley of the shot) and its sounds (the owner, Nov 27: "your Mafia is a toy, the doctor, the commissar, the citizen and the mafia each a beautiful animated picture", then his own four illustrations, then: "put the 3 pictures as the background, animated, with sound: an owl when the night falls, a real rifle at the shot, for the day you know yourself; I did not touch the citizen's background")
The reference (a Gramora recording, 720 x 1594; then the owner's mock-up of four screens): a night street (moon, lamps), the players on a ring round a dark centre circle, a shot player's avatar under CRACKED GLASS, the dawn, then a big illustration of the role of the dead ("U ASLIDA FUQARO"), the town square by day. All of it is in the game; the code is in `pro_h0_mfimg.js` (the role pictures), `pro_h1_mfbg.js` (the three backgrounds + mist), `pro_h_mfart.js` (the scenes, the motion) and `pro_d_mafia.js` (the screen); the sounds are in `Snd` (`index.html`).
- **The four role pictures are the OWNER'S OWN illustrations** (a sheet of four cards, 2752 x 1536; every card 1336 x 732 with its words "YOUR ROLE / CITIZEN / ..." painted in English at the bottom quarter). The game uses each card cut at 72 % of its height, WITHOUT the words (WebP, 1100 x 435, ~55 - 95 KB), and a square round the head (176 x 176 WebP) as the face; **the words are drawn by the game** (the role's name and its line, 15 languages). `MFA_IMG[role] = {card, face}` is all the code knows; to change a picture, replace the two data URIs (cut a new sheet the same way). The CSS classes `.mfa-face.f-<role>` and `.mfa-art.a-<role>` carry them as backgrounds, so the browser decodes a picture once. In the light build the cards become files in `web/img/`. **The role cards were NOT touched by the second message (the citizen's background stays as it was).**
- **Motion of the role cards** (CSS only): the card slowly zooms (`mfaKen`), a coloured glow behind the face breathes (`.mfa-aura`, a screen blend), sparks rise (`.mfa-dust`), the card flips in; the face badges breathe and their ring pulses. (An earlier version had four portraits PAINTED in code: commit `34f3dbd`; the owner preferred his own pictures. A turning sunburst over his pictures made a moire over the faces: do not add one.)
- **The three backgrounds are the owner's pictures too** (`MFA_BG` in `pro_h1_mfbg.js`): the night street (moon, lamps, BAR / HOTEL neon), the town square by day (the Town Hall, a fountain), the alley of the shot (a man in a hat, shattered glass). WebP 1920 x 1072 (100 - 335 KB each; the sources were 2752 x 1536 JPEGs from the chat, not kept in the repo) and three tileable mist strips (1024 x 256 RGBA: night grey-blue, day warm white, red smoke; the colour is baked in, so no CSS `mask` is needed - a mask image from another origin is blocked). **The shot picture had its English words ("YOU WERE SHOT IN THE NIGHT / ELIMINATED") painted out** (the band of the two lines was filled by OpenCV Telea inpaint at 1/4 size + fractal smoke texture + grain, feathered into the picture: it reads as a smoke bank with the lamp's light cone) because the game writes the words itself, in the player's language. To change a picture: replace the data URI (1920 px wide, proportions 1920:1072, or change `aspect-ratio` and the `1.791` of `.mfb-st`).
- **`mfbScene(kind)`** = one scene (`night` / `day` / `shot` / `dusk`): a **stage** `.mfb-st` that covers the screen like a photo does (`width: max(100cqw, 100cqh * 1.791)`; container-query units of `.mfb`, so it is right under the CSS `zoom` and on the turned page; `@supports not (width:1cqw)` falls back to 100% x 100%), a slow camera drift (32 s), and over the picture a living layer placed in % OF THE PICTURE, so a lamp stays on its lamp on every screen (the positions were MEASURED on the pictures: bright blobs for lamps / neon / moon, a top-hat filter for the crack vertices). **Night:** 7 street lamps that breathe (two fail now and then, `mfbFlick`), the BAR / key / HOTEL / shop signs, the moon's halo with a cloud that crosses it, 3 mist banks (`.mfb-fog`: a strip 200 % wide moved by -50 % = exactly one tile, seamless), steam puffs from the manhole and from a car, 2 bats. **Day:** the sun's glow and 3 rays of light, 2 cloud strips over the sky and a haze over the street, the fountain (9 drops on a parabola, 9 sparkles), 6 glints on glass and chrome, 5 birds, 14 specks of dust in the sun, and a warm flash (`.mfb-bloom`) when the morning comes. **Shot:** red smoke, flickering red lamps, glints on 10 vertices of the cracks, 26 embers, the muzzle flash at the bullet hole and a shake of the camera (`mfbHit`). **Dusk:** the day picture dimmed (a vote's verdict). Only `transform` / `opacity` animate (compositor), the scene that is not on show is paused (`#mfScr .mf-bg ... animation-play-state`), `prefers-reduced-motion` stops everything.
- **The screen**: the picture fills it; the players stand on a ring (me at the bottom) as round avatars with a name pill, a gold pulsing ring on a seat I may name, a red dashed ring on the one I picked; the question sits in a dark circle in the middle (`#mfMsg`); the right column keeps the clock (the seconds are a ring, red at the last five), my role chip (the face) and the keys. A dead seat has a red cross and the small face of its role; a seat that is alive and hidden shows no face (the secrecy is unchanged: the server only sends what a phone may know).
- **Events** (`mfEvent`, one picture after another over the screen, a tap goes to the next): the role card when the match opens (6.5 s); the morning after a kill: **the alley picture** - for another player the victim in a silver-ringed medallion under cracked glass (`mfaCrack`) with "Tunda otib ketildi" and his name, for **ME the words of the owner's picture** ("Siz tunda otib ketildingiz" / "O'yindan chiqdingiz" = "You were shot in the night / Eliminated", cream-to-blood-red letters, their size set by their length - `--n` - so a long language still fits: German, Uzbek checked), cut in at once with a white flash - and then the role of the dead ("U aslida" + his card); a morning without a kill: the town square tinted green with the cross (the doctor stays anonymous); the vote: the dusk square with the voted-out player under a cross and then his role (the title already has the name); a tie: a banner. The warm flash of the morning plays when these pictures are gone (under them nobody would see it).
- **Sounds (the owner: an owl at nightfall, a real rifle at the shot, the day my choice).** No recordings: the machine that built it has no access to any sound library, so they are **synthesized** by pure DSP (`mfs` inside `Snd`: noise through filters, envelopes, echoes; it fills an `AudioBuffer` once, ~50 - 130 ms of work each, built ahead by `Snd.mfWarm()` when the Mafia room or screen opens, and the same code was rendered offline to WAV and checked as spectrograms). **`Snd.owl`** (as the night falls, 0.9 s after the drone): a tawny owl far off - one long "hoo", a pause, "hu-hu-hooo" that quavers; **`Snd.shot`** (the morning with a kill; was `Snd.knife`): a rifle in a street of brick walls - a 1 ms crack, the blast (a mid body a phone speaker can play + a thump), the hot gas fading, six echoes off the walls that get darker, a diffuse tail, and the glass of the broken window (a crackle and a few ringing shards); **`Snd.day`** (2.6 s after the shot / 0.6 s after a rescue, i.e. when the shot picture gives way): a morning - four birds from left to right (a finch, a sparrow, a warbler, a blackbird) and a breath of air. **If the owner has real recordings, put them in `ui` (like `REAL_UI`) or replace the three builders.** `window.__sfx` hooks in the tests count `owl` / `shot` / `day` by name.
- Texts: 4 new (`pro_xt11`, `pro_xt12`: 15 languages). Tests: `node tools/test_mafia_art.js` (40 checks, ~1 min, no server: the pictures decode and carry, both scenes and every layer, the stage covers the screen, only the shown scene runs, every event incl. "I am the one shot", the ring at 5 sizes and 5 / 8 seats) and the old `tools/test_mafia_ui.js` (a real match with bots, counts the owl and the shot) both pass.
- **Traps found on the way:** a CSS `transform` animation REPLACES the SVG `transform` attribute of the same element (put the `translate` on an outer `<g>` and the animation on an inner one); a `text-shadow` on `background-clip:text` letters is drawn through the glyphs (use `filter: drop-shadow`); a 6 ms moving average used to "blur" an echo notches every multiple of 167 Hz and ate a 392 Hz owl note (use one-pole low-passes); **screenshots taken by several Chromium pages at once are late** (a frame caught during the role card's flip-in looked like a transparent blue overlay): take them one run at a time.
- The server was down these days (the Neon quota, see the section below), so the screen was checked on made-up matches and on screen recordings of them, not on a live match.

## Profile photos: the Google / Telegram picture as the avatar (the owner, Nov 27, a screenshot of Gramora's Mafia ring where every player is his own Telegram photo: "a player must be able to put his own picture: Google sign-in shows the Google photo, Telegram sign-in the Telegram photo")
**Before:** the game never read a photo from Google or Telegram; the avatar was one of the game's own (numbers 0 - 563). **Now:** a player who signed in with Google or Telegram can wear his provider picture as his avatar, and the other players see it everywhere an avatar is shown (the Mafia ring, the board games, lobbies, friends, rankings, chat, the top bar). Uploading a picture from the phone's gallery is NOT done (see the end).
- **The idea (one number, so nothing else had to change).** A photo is an AVATAR whose number is **>= 1000** (`PH`, `isPhotoAv`): the server hands the account a random number (`u.pid`, 1000 .. 2^31 - 24: the phones read it with `|0`) and keeps the picture's address (`u.pic`) or the Bot API file (`u.picTg`). `u.avatar = u.pid` when the player wears it, so every place that already carries an avatar number (about 40 server payloads, 52 `avHTML` calls) carries the photo too. The phone draws it from **`GET /api/photo/<pid>`** (`avatarURL(i)` returns that URL; `normAv` leaves numbers >= 1000 alone; `avName0` = "Mening rasmim"; `avDrawable(i)` gives a canvas the loaded `Image`, a game avatar until it has loaded; a global capturing `error` handler swaps any `<img>` of `/api/photo/` that cannot load for a game avatar).
- **Where the picture comes from (server, `verifyProvider`).** Google: the `picture` of the verified ID token (asked for as `=s192-c`). Telegram: the `photo_url` inside the SIGNED initData; if it has none (or for a player who signed in long ago) the server asks the **Bot API** (`getUserProfilePhotos`, the 320 px size, then `getFile`; `ensureTgPic`, in the background on login and on `/api/sync`, at most every 6 hours per player; the bot token never leaves the server). Facebook: none (its button is off, and its CDN addresses expire).
- **Safety (this is the part to keep when changing anything).** (1) Only an address that came inside a VERIFIED sign-in and sits on the host list (`PIC_HOSTS`: `t.me/i/userpic/`, `lh*.googleusercontent.com`, `*.cdn-telegram.org`) is ever stored or fetched: an arbitrary address would let a player find out the other players' IP addresses or make the server call any host. (2) The phones never talk to the provider's CDN: `photoOf(u)` fetches the picture ITSELF (3 redirects at most, each hop on the list too, 6 s, 160 KB; the Bot API calls have a 6 s timeout too) and caches it (6 h, at most 400 pictures and 24 MB, failures 5 min; `picBusy` makes one fetch of many requests). (3) The answer is sniffed by its first bytes: only JPEG / PNG / WebP / GIF are served, **never SVG** (it would run scripts if opened on its own), with `nosniff`, `Content-Security-Policy: default-src 'none'; sandbox` and CORS `*` (the page may draw it on a canvas). (4) `/api/sync` accepts an avatar >= 1000 only if it is the player's OWN number and he has a photo (a made-up or someone else's number is ignored). (5) The number is random, so the photos cannot be collected by counting up; the endpoint is rate-limited (900 / minute / IP). (6) `POST /api/photo/off` forgets the picture, its address and its number (the avatar goes back to a game one); deleting the account does the same; the privacy page now says what is taken and that the photo is shown only if the player chooses it.
- **In the game.** After a Google / Telegram sign-in the sync that follows brings `pic: {id, src}`; **once per account, on the home screen, when the picture has loaded**, the game asks "Use your photo as your avatar?" (the picture, where it comes from, Yes / No; `profile.picAsked`, never again). The avatar shop (Avatars tab) has a **"My photo · Google / Telegram" card first** (select, or "Remove photo" after a question); the avatar before the photo is remembered (`profile.avPrev`) and comes back when the photo is gone (removed, or another account) at the next sync. A guest, Y8 / Playgama / GameMonetize / Android-without-login players have no photo and see nothing new.
- **What is needed to see it live:** the server has to be deployed (it holds the new routes); until then (the Neon quota, see the next section) a phone gets no `pic` and nothing changes. Existing Telegram players get their photo by themselves (the Bot API on their next sync); existing Google players get it at their next Google sign-in (the token is the only source).
- **Tests:** `node server/test_photo.js` (43 checks, ~40 s: fake Google / CDN / Bot API; the host list, redirects, SVG / fake image / oversized / slow / missing pictures, caching and 304, numbers that give nothing, sync ownership, the Bot API path, photo off, account delete) and `node tools/test_photo_ui.js` (28 checks, ~2 min: the real page and server; the offer, "Yes / No", the nav picture, the shop card, another player's page, the fallback, a canvas, the Mafia ring, removing, Telegram). Texts: 8 new lines (`pro_xt13`, 15 languages).
- **Not done, and why:** **uploading a picture from the gallery.** It needs a place to keep the pictures (Neon's free 0.5 GB and Render's wiped disk are not it: an image store such as Cloudflare R2 / Cloudinary) and **moderation** (anybody could show anything to everybody: Google Play and Telegram expect a report / block path and removal by the admin for user-made pictures). A provider picture is the person's own Google / Telegram profile picture, already public there. If the owner wants uploads: store a 160 px WebP per player outside the database, add a report button and an admin "remove photo" action (the avatar number scheme above would not change).

## Neon quota ran out (Nov 26): why, and what the server does now
**Why.** The free Neon plan gives **100 CU-hours of compute a month** (about 17 days of a compute that never sleeps) and the compute sleeps only after **5 minutes WITHOUT a query**. The server saved 2 s after every change, `auth()` sets `u.lastSeen = now` at EVERY request and `POST /api/sync` (a phone on the menu screen syncs every 60 s) marks the database dirty, so every active player changed his row every minute: the compute never slept, the 100 CU-hours were gone shortly before Nov 26, and Neon answered "Your account or project has exceeded the quota" to every new connection (the owner's Neon page: "Monthly compute allowance used (100 CU-hrs). Resets in 4 days"; the idle branch was archived on Nov 26 21:09: the data is safe, the next query unarchives it). The old instance (`a62a649`) kept running from memory, every new instance exited with status 1 = every deploy after that failed.
**What changed (`server/storage.js`, `server/server.js`; written on Nov 26 while the quota was out, so it could NOT be deployed that day):**
- Rows are compared with `lastSeen` **rounded down to 30 min** (`SEEN_WRITE_MS`): the field still moves in memory exactly as before (online status, pushes, friends' "ago"), but alone it no longer writes a row; any real change writes the row in full with the exact value. One active phone: 10 writes in 23 s before, 1 after.
- With Postgres the changes are saved **in batches**: at most one per `SAVE_EVERY_MS` (default **15 min**; the first change after a start goes at once). Money (`fulfill`, Google Play refunds) and a shutdown (SIGTERM: a deploy, Render's spin-down) are saved at once (`flush(true)`). A failed save is tried again in about a minute. `SAVE_EVERY_MS=2000` is the old behaviour (use it with a paid database); a file store (local, the tests) keeps 2 s. **The price:** a kill without SIGTERM (out of memory) loses up to 15 minutes of changes that are not money (the phones send their progress again at their next sync).
- The start-up read is tried up to 5 times (a suspended or archived Neon branch needs a few seconds to wake), except for "exceeded the quota", which fails at once (the deploy must fail fast so that the old instance keeps serving).
- `/api/health` has `db: {kind, everyMs, pending, lastOk, ageSec, err}`: `lastOk:false` and `err` = the database refuses writes right now; `pending:true` = changes wait for the next batch. Each batch logs one line, `db: N row(s) written ...`: the Render log shows how often the database is really touched.
**Order of things when the quota is back (after the monthly reset or an upgrade):** do not touch the live service before the database answers: the OLD instance writes what it holds by itself (it tries every 2 s). Then deploy this code (Manual Deploy). Test: `node server/test_storage.js` (28 checks, ~45 s; `server/fake_pg.js` is a stand-in for `pg`, tests only).

## Table tennis (the owner, Nov 26: "Stol tennis qani": the arcade game after the air hockey)
`pro_g_tennis.js`, the second card of the "Arcade games" row (since Nov 27 the right half of the owner's Arcade picture: a key over it opens 3 bot levels or two on one phone, then 3 / 7 / 11 points). **A 3D table seen from behind my end**: a pinhole camera over a table of 274 x 152.5 cm with the net (15.25 cm), drawn on ONE 1280 x 720 canvas (scaled to the screen like the air hockey, so it works on the turned page); the ball is drawn with its shadow on the table and a short trail (bigger than a real one: a real-sized ball would be a dot on a phone), the paddles have the **flag of their player** on the rubber (mine red, the opponent blue), the HUD has both flags, the score, "TABLE TENNIS" and "country - country - First to 7 points", and a point shows "{name} scores a point!" with its reason (Net / Out / Missed / Double bounce / Landed on its own half). Units are cm; X along the table (my end is -X), Z across, Y up (`TBT_*` constants, state in `TBT`).
- **Controls: one finger moves my paddle in the upright plane at my end** (left / right, up / down). The finger is mapped back through the camera onto that plane (`tbtUnprojPlane`; the three marks `.hk-m` at the canvas' corners make it work under any CSS turn / zoom), the paddle follows at a limited speed (1300 cm/s, it cannot teleport) and stays in its reach. **A hit is made by the game, the swipe gives the power and the aim:** when the ball has bounced once on my half and crosses the paddle's plane within 15 cm of its centre, `tbtShot` finds the flight that clears the net (at least 3.5 cm above it) and lands where the swipe says (the speed of the paddle = how deep: 36 - 120 cm beyond the net; its sideways speed + where on the paddle the ball hit = the side) - **tested for 400 random hits on both sides: every one clears the net and lands inside the other half**. The serve is automatic (the ball hovers over the server's paddle for a moment, then it is hit toward the other half) and **changes every 2 points**. **A faint green ring shows where the ball will come to my paddle** (easy and mid; not on hard): `tbtSim` flies the ball ahead with the table bounce, the net and gravity.
- **Rules (the real ones, a bit simplified):** after a hit the ball must land on the OTHER half (a ball that lands on the hitter's own half, a ball stopped by the net, a ball that goes out without landing on the other half: a point for the other side); it may bounce ONCE there and must then be hit (a second bounce, or a ball that is not hit and goes out: a point for the hitter); **there is no volley: a paddle only touches a ball that has bounced on its half**; the match is to 3 / 7 / 11 points (no deuce); the result window and "Play again" as in the other games; results are kept (`proStore().rec["tt_<level>"]`). No coins against the bot.
- **The bot** (easy / mid / hard: paddle speed 330 / 540 / 840 cm/s, reaction 0.32 / 0.16 / 0.06 s, how far it misjudges 17 / 9 / 3.5 cm, how often it misses on purpose 20% / 8% / 1.5%, how hard it hits): the moment I hit the ball it flies it ahead (`tbtPlanBot`), goes to where the ball will come (a little late, a little wrong), and returns it to the side away from my paddle. **Two on one phone:** a finger low on the screen moves my paddle, a finger high on the screen the far paddle.
- **Sounds** (`Snd`, not switched off on the menu screens): `paddle`, `tbounce`, `netHit`; a point is the coin sound, the end is win / lose.
- **Test:** `node tools/test_tennis.js` (42 checks, ~3 min: the way in, the painted canvas, the camera round trip and the perspective, a finger on the plane at 3 spots and on the turned page, the speed limit, the 400 flights, a double bounce / own half / net / out / missed ball, the paddle hit and the "no volley" and "too far" rules, 80 random hits that are all legal returns, the serve every 2 points, the end of the match, the bot's speed per level and how often it returns my serve (easy < mid < hard), an idle human loses, a human who follows the ring beats the easy bot, the ring on easy and not on hard, two fingers). Texts: 12 new lines in `tools/xt.txt` (`pro_xt10`, 15 languages). The air hockey test now expects two cards in the row.

## The Board games row is the owner's picture (the owner, Nov 24: "put the 1st in place of the 2nd, let it go through in full")
The three cards of the mini-games screen's **Board games** row (a row of painted cards: Checkers / Magic Chess / Mafia, `.sh-card`) are gone: the row is now **one picture, the owner's own artwork** (`SH_BAN` in `pro_8_shashka_ui.js`, WebP, 1500 x 844 px = 1672 x 941 scaled; three cards in a gold frame: **Checkers, Chess, Mafia**, with the titles and the lines "Play with friends and computer" / "5-8 players, role with votes" PAINTED IN THE PICTURE in English for every language) with **three invisible keys lying over the cards** (`.sh-pan`, `SH_PANELS` = left edge and width of each card in % of the picture's width; a tap = the same list as the old card: against a bot / with a friend). **In full:** `.sh-ban` is the largest picture that fits the screen's height (`width: min(100%, 900px, (100 * --u-vh - 80px) * 1.7768)`, `aspect-ratio: 1672 / 941`), so nothing is cut off whatever the phone (the turned Telegram page too). To change the picture: put a new WebP into `SH_BAN` (keep the proportions or move `SH_PANELS`). The painted card pictures (`shArt` / `mcArt` / `mfArt`) are no longer shown here (they stay in the code). Tests: `tools/test_shashka_ui.js` checks the picture is shown in full (fits the screen, its proportions, the three keys inside it side by side), `test_magic_ui.js` / `test_mafia_ui.js` that the keys are there and work.

The same thing as the Board games row, for the Arcade games row (`hkMiniRow` in `pro_f_hockey.js`): the two painted cards (`hkArt` / `tbtArt`, still in the code, not shown) are gone; the row is **one picture, his artwork** (`HK_BAN` in `pro_f0_arcban.js`, WebP 1020 x 1024, ~190 KB; two tall cards in a gold frame with a crown each: **Air Hockey** (blue) and **Table Tennis** (green), "Play with friends and computer" in a gold plaque; the words are PART OF THE PICTURE, in English for every language) with **two invisible keys over the cards** (`.sh-pan`, `HK_PANELS` = left edge and width of each card in % of the picture's width: the gold frames run x 10 - 494 and 520 - 1005 of 1020; `aria-label` = the game's translated name). A tap = what the card did: the list of opponents (`hkPick()` / `tbtPick()`). **In full:** `.sh-ban.ar` is the largest picture that fits the screen's height (`width: min(100%, 900px, (100 * --u-vh - 80px) * .996)`, `aspect-ratio: 1020 / 1024`), left-aligned under the row title like the Board games picture, so nothing is cut off on any phone (the turned Telegram page too: the two cards then stand one above the other, as the owner's screenshot shows).
