## Glass windows: the lists of the games and the online rooms (the owner, Nov 27: four screenshots, "put the 2nd in place of the 1st, the 4th in place of the 3rd, and make the others [chess ...] as beautiful as the 2nd and the 4th")
The owner sent the Checkers list and the Checkers "Online" room as they were (flat dark-blue rows, a flat window) and, next to each, a far prettier picture of the same window (glossy glass rows, sparkles, a purple-blue starry room with gold filigree and glass stake chips). **Every window of that kind now looks like the pretty ones**, in ONE place: the PRO part `pro_i_glam.js` (a `<style id="glamStyle">` + two small generated SVG pictures: the four gold corners, the star field with comet tails; no picture files, nothing animates).
- **The lists** (`proPickList`: Shashka, Magic Chess, Mafia, Air hockey, Table tennis, Trick shot, Lessons, the bot levels, 9-ball, 2v2): the window is `#modal .panel.achp.glam` (`openModal({..., glam: true})` adds the class after `skinModal`; every other window is untouched), the rows are glass tubes (a light rim above and below, a dark middle, a gloss on the upper half, a violet tint to the right), the **online row is purple with a glow** (`.pro-row.pro-on`, id `online`), a finished row (`.me`) is gold, the level pictures glow green / yellow / red (`data-gl`), the picture of the window (the white ball, the knight ...) sits in a **sunburst**, the text is gold, the Close key is a **blue glass pill with a gold rim**. Sparkles + a violet bloom are the window's background (over the old picture).
- **The small windows of the same games** wear the same glass keys: the length of an air-hockey / table-tennis match (orange / blue / green / blue pills) and the table-coins wallet (`glam: true`). Any new window can ask for it the same way.
- **The online rooms** (`#brRoom`: Shashka / Magic Chess / Mafia, `#tnRoom`: 2v2): a purple-blue starry window (`.tn-box`: four background layers of gold filigree drawn over the border, the star field, three colour blooms, a gold gradient border), glass seats (mine green), glass team plates, the room code in a glowing frame, a glowing code field, the stake plate with **glass chips (the chosen one in gold)**, the green Create / Start keys glow. Only colours, borders and shadows changed: **the layout of the rooms (rows, order, sizes, scrolling) is the old one** (the owner: "do not touch the other places"); on a short screen (`html.mh460`) the padding gives back the 1 px the thicker frame took, so no window got taller there.
- **Checked** (`mfui/glamfit.js`-style screenshots): 915x412 (his phone), 740x360, 568x320, 640x300, 480x270, the turned page 412x915 and 360x640, 1280x720: no list scrolls, nothing is cut off, the rooms scroll no more than before (Mafia's 8 seats at <= 360 px high did already).
## Air hockey face to face + real table-tennis / air-hockey sounds (the owner, Nov 27: "Air Hockey and tennis need improving: the sounds are not like real tennis; and the Air Hockey should be played 'lob ma lob' like the tennis")
**"Lob ma lob"** = the Russian "лоб в лоб", face to face: the table tennis already stands behind my end of the table, the air hockey stood at the side of its table (long side across the screen, the two players left and right). Now the air hockey is **face to face too** (`pro_f_hockey.js`): `hkCamBuild` is a pinhole behind MY end looking up the table (the same maths as `tbtCamBuild`: forward / right = forward x up / up = right x forward), `hkUnproj` casts the finger's ray onto the plane y = 0, `hkDraw` paints the table from this side (my near end rail and goal slot in front of the objects, the far end rail with its slot and lamp behind them, both side rails' inner faces, the two goal trays and lamps, the mallets' flags measured across Z). **The physics, the bot, the sizes and the finger mapping are the old ones** (a finger is mapped onto the table by the same projection, so a finger up the screen = up the table, the near half of the table is player 1 in the two-on-one-phone mode). The score moved from the top centre (it would hide the far goal) to the free space at the top right, the "drag the mallet" hint to the bottom left. `test_hockey` 30/0, `test_tennis` 42/0.
**Sounds**: the old ones were single oscillator / noise beeps (`Snd.puck / wallHit / paddle / tbounce / netHit / horn`). They are now **struck-object sounds** (`arcs` in the `Snd` closure of `index.html`, next to the Mafia `mfs`): each impact = a few decaying sine modes (the ball's thin plastic shell rings high and short, the rubber blade / table board / puck's plastic / rail ring lower and longer) + a click + a thump, filled into an AudioBuffer once (no recordings): `ttPaddle` (ball on a rubber blade), `ttBounce` (ball on the table), `ttNet`, `hkHit` (mallet on the puck, a loud bright clack), `hkRail` (puck on a rail, woodier, a quick second tap), `hkGoal` (the puck drops into the slot and rattles down the tray; the old horn follows 0.14 s later) and `hkHum` (the table's blower, a quiet loop while the air hockey is open, louder the faster the puck slides: `Snd.airHum(on)`, `Snd.airLevel(k)`). Three variants of each, a random one per hit and a hair detuned; the harder the hit the louder and the brighter (a lowpass opens with the speed); the stereo side follows the side of the table the ball / puck is on (`pan`, up to 0.5). They are built ahead one by one when a game opens (`Snd.arcWarm()`), the first cold call costs 1 - 12 ms. The old beep code stays as the fallback when the audio context is missing. I cannot listen to them: the numbers were checked (no clipping / NaN / DC, 56 - 160 ms audible, centroids 0.9 - 2 kHz) and the buffers play in the real page; **the owner is the judge of how real they sound** (the modes are the first thing to tune: `strike(...)` calls in the `arcs` object).
## Shashka: "when I tap to move, the screen goes off and on" (the owner, Nov 28, 00:22, Telegram on Android, the turned page, a game against "Друг" on one phone)
**Not reproduced** (headless Chromium on a desktop CPU): a video of four real-tap moves on the turned page (412 x 915) shows no flash in the game (brightness per frame steady; the only dip is the screen opening), CDP tracing of a select + move costs ~60 ms of raster even with the CPU slowed 6 x, the JS heap of the built page is 19 - 25 MB, nothing reloads / goes fullscreen / vibrates on a tap. So the cause is on his phone's GPU. The heaviest thing the board paints is the stone: every one of the 64 squares has an **SVG feTurbulence noise (5 octaves)** as its background (`SH_LT` / `SH_DK` in `pro_8_shashka_ui.js`), which the browser re-runs whenever a tile of the board is painted (a tap toggles `.sel / .opt / .last` on squares and rebuilds the pieces). **Change (same picture, pixel-checked: mean difference 0.08 / 255, max 2):** `shBitmap()` drew each stone ONCE into a 160 x 160 canvas and the squares used that bitmap (`--lt` / `--dk` on `#shScr`, a blob URL). **(Since the same day the squares have no noise at all: the board is the owner's wooden picture, see the next section, and `shBitmap` is gone.)** **Second change (the owner: it blinks in ALL shashka modes, i.e. it is the board, not the game logic; the Magic Chess board has plain gradients and no noise):** `shRender` no longer writes what did not change: the squares' `left / top / data-l` are set only when different (writing the same attribute again makes the browser repaint all 64 squares), and the discs and the two player cards (framed avatars = big pictures) are rebuilt only when their markup differs from what is on the screen (`pcs.__h`, `cards[i].__h`; `shAnim` edits the discs in place and clears the cache). `test_shashka_ui` 44/0, `test_shashka` 27/0, `test_board_online` 33/0, `test_magic_ui` 42/0. **If it still blinks**, what is needed from him: does it happen against the bot / online / with the 3D key on or off, only on the first tap or on the move, does the game restart (the loading screen) or only the picture blink, the phone and Android version, and a screen recording; the next suspects are the pieces' blurred `box-shadow` + `transition` on the compositor and the GPU memory of the turned page (`html.rot` + `zoom`).
## Shashka: the owner's wooden board and wooden discs (the owner, Nov 28: two pictures, "put this board into the shashka, with these stones")
**His pictures:** a walnut-and-maple board seen from an angle on a dark table (the letters A - H and the numbers 1 - 8 in gold in the frame, a gold inlay line round the squares) and discs of ebony and maple with concentric grooves (two pictures: a heap, then a second one of 15 discs). **What was made of them** (all in `pro_8z_shwood.js`, three WebP pictures as base64 constants, ~130 KB together; the web build puts them in files):
- **The board** (`SH_WOOD`, 1160 x 1160): the 4 corners of the 8 x 8 squares were found by hand, the picture was warped (a perspective transform) into a flat square, then a small affine correction made every grid line fall within 2 px of the ideal 128 px (measured: residuals <= 2 px) and the frame was cropped. The squares are the middle 1024 x 1024: a margin of **5.862 %** of the board on each side (`--f:.05862` in the CSS: `#shScr .sh-board` has a transparent border of that width, the 64 invisible `.sq` buttons lie over the squares, as before). The picture is a layer (`<i class="sh-bg">`) under the squares and the discs. **The dark (walnut) squares are the playing ones**, exactly as in the game (a1 bottom left is dark). The letters / numbers are in the picture (the game's own labels are off: `.sq::before{content:none}`); **the picture turns 180 degrees when the board is flipped** (`#shScr.flip`, set in `shRender` from `SHU.flip`): the letters then read upside down, as on a real board seen from the other side, and the squares, the labels and the pieces stay in step.
- **The discs** (`SH_DISC_W` maple, `SH_DISC_B` ebony, **360 px wide, WHOLE discs with their own ridged wooden sides**, transparent background, WebP q92 with alpha, 21 KB + 30 KB; the first cut was 200 px wide and the owner asked again *"make it in good quality"*: the disc in his picture is ~456 px across, so 200 px used less than half of the detail. `cut5.py` cuts the same masks and scales them with `INTER_AREA` to 360 + a light unsharp mask (0.35, radius 1.1) on the colour only, which makes the ridges and the grain sharp on a 3 x phone; 360 is about 2 x the biggest disc a phone / tablet draws, so the browser's own down-scaling stays clean): the first version used the flattened TOP face of the first heap picture and drew the thickness with stacked CSS shadow bands; the owner pointed at the tan base with arrows and then, on Nov 28, sent a **second picture** (15 discs, 8 ebony and 7 maple, seen from above at ~55 degrees) with *"you did not fix it; if not, make these stones in good quality"*. So the pieces are now **cut out of that picture as whole discs** (one maple, one ebony, `cut4.py` / `cut5.py`: LAB-colour segmentation of the disc against the dark table, closing + erosion so that no cast shadow stays in the black one, threshold L < 84; a light, a bit tilted disc with its thickness is what a real top view of a stone looks like). In the CSS (`shWoodStyle()` in `pro_8z_shwood.js`) `.pc.w i` / `.pc.b i` only set `--dsp` (the sprite) and `background:none`; **the sprite is the `::before` layer** (`left:0; top:50%; width:100%; height:94%; background:var(--dsp) center/100% 100% no-repeat`), the disc is **84 % of the square** (`.pc i`) with a soft ground shadow that is a **gradient ellipse behind it** (`.pc::before`, `radial-gradient(closest-side, #000a, #0008 55%, #0004 78%, #0000)`, 96 % x 68 % of the square, a bit lower; the lifted `.pc.up` piece throws a bigger one, `translateY(8%) scale(1.1)`; `.tilt3d` a taller one). The king's gold crown and ring (`.pc.k i svg`, `::after`) are lifted a little (`margin-top:-9%` / `-6%`) to sit on the top face of the disc; the selection / last-move / landing lights are as before. The 3D key still tilts the board (the discs tilt with it) and the shadow grows a bit there. **A gotcha found on the way:** the old rules `#shScr .pc.b i::before{background:radial-gradient...}` and `.pc.w i{background:...}` out-specified a plain `.pc i::before` rule and made the black sprite invisible: the sprite rules need the `.pc.w i::before, .pc.b i::before` selectors and the `background:none` on `.pc.w i, .pc.b i`. **The tan "base" came back (found Nov 28 on the owner's "make it in good quality")**: the discs still had a circular `box-shadow` on the round `i` box, and a box-shadow is **never painted inside its own box** - under the picture (which is an ellipse, shorter than the circle) a hole of un-shadowed, lit board showed between the disc and the shadow: a light tan crescent under every disc, and under the selected one (`.pc.up`, whose rule still had the old `--side` band and out-specified mine) a whole tan plate. Same for the `.pc.must` pulse (`@keyframes shglow` carried the band). Now `box-shadow:none` on `.pc i`, `.pc.up i` and `.tilt3d .pc i`, `shglow` keeps only the 3 px salmon ring, and the shadow is the gradient above (no blur filter: cheaper to paint than the old blurred box-shadow too). **Look at close-ups of the DISC EDGES on both a light and a dark square, and of the selected piece, whenever this CSS changes** (the screenshots at normal size hid it).
- **Gone:** the SVG feTurbulence stone and the `shBitmap` helper of the blink fix (the squares have no texture of their own any more).
- **Tests:** `test_shashka_ui` 44/0, `test_shashka` 27/0, `test_board_online` 33/0. Pictures: landscape 915 x 412, the turned page 412 x 915, the king, the flipped board, the 3D key.
- **To change the pictures:** the scripts that cut them are not in the repo (they were in the work folder): the board = `cv2.getPerspectiveTransform` of the quad (668, 275) (2076, 272) (2310, 1236) (431, 1237) of the 2752 x 1536 picture onto a 128 px grid with a 1.2-square margin, then `warpAffine` [[.985, 0, 13.9], [0, .9894, 8.7]] (inverse map) and the crop (665 +- 580); the discs = `cut4.py` of the second picture (see the paragraph above): LAB segmentation, a whole disc cut out at 200 px wide, WebP with alpha.

## Shashka: "the screen blinks and the menu shows when I tap a piece" + a square that stays lit (the owner, Nov 28, 14:18, Telegram on Android, the turned page, a game against the bot "Majd")
**His words** (with an orange arrow on a lit square): *"after the move that place stays lit until the other side moves, and then when I tap a checker the screen blinks and the menu shows."* The arrow points at the **square the bot's piece came from** (an olive square: `.sq.last::after`, `#e8c45a55` over the dark wood); the piece's landing square was lit too.
- **The lit squares: removed.** `shRender` no longer lights the two squares of the last move (`last = new Set()`; `G.last` is still kept, the tests read it). The piece slides over its route, so the move is seen anyway. **Magic Chess still lights them** (the owner did not mention it): one line, `last` in `pro_b_magic_ui.js`.
- **The blink: not reproduced, not proven, but three things were measured that make a weak phone work for nothing while the board is on, and they are gone.** Headless Chromium, a game on the mini-games screen: **behind the board the home screen's cue showcase canvas (`hCue`) drew ~1200 times a second** (two infinite `requestAnimationFrame` loops with `shadowBlur`, they only stop when their canvas leaves the DOM), **28 CSS animations ran** (`spinRays` of the full-screen `#shade`, 17 sparks, the red dots of the menu keys) and `fitNav` forced a layout every 1.2 s. And **every tap on a square built a golden ring** (`.tapfx`, the "press feedback", appended to `body` for 0.5 s) with `z-index: 9999` - under the board's `99983`, so nobody could see it, but the phone built a layer for it and dropped it again at every tap (the `<button class="sq">` squares match its selector and shashka's `mode` is "menu"). A glitch of the compositor on a phone that is busy like that shows what lies under the board - **the menu** - which is what he describes. (The first blink report, Nov 27, was on the OLD board: so the cause is not the picture of the wood.)
- **What was done.** `html.bgm` is on while `#shScr` or `#mcScr` is on (`bgmWatch()` in `pro_8_shashka_ui.js`: a `MutationObserver` on the screen's class, so it holds whoever turns the screen on or off). Under it (CSS next to `.tapfx` in `index.html`): `#shade` is `display:none`, `#nav` and every `.screen` except `#modal` are `visibility:hidden` (layout stays, so nothing measures wrong) and their animations are paused; in JS: the page's frame loop (`frame()`: the demo match is neither simulated nor drawn), `fitNav` and the two cue-showcase loops rest (`bgmOn()`); the tap ring is not built under a board game's screen or a pop-up (`#modal`, `#toasts`). Measured after: **0 canvas draws a second, 0 running animations, no long task, no ring** (before: 1170 / 28 / a 53-87 ms task / one ring per tap).
- **Tests:** `test_shashka_ui` 49/0 (5 new: html.bgm on, the menu hidden and still, no canvas drawn, a REAL touch tap builds no ring and still picks the piece, no square lit after a move, the menu back and animating after the board closes; **3 of them fail on the old build**), `test_magic_ui` 44/0 (2 new), `test_shashka` 27/0, `test_board_online` 33/0, `test_stake_ui` 33/0, `test_pro_modes` 69/0, `test_mini_fit` 31/0, `test_table_scroll` 27/0 (that one is timing based: a 4x slowed finger run, it failed once and passed on the repeat).
- **If it still blinks** (say it plainly to the owner): a **screen recording** is what is needed (Android's own recorder), and the answers to: does the game restart (the loading screen) or does only the picture blink; which key / piece he taps; Telegram's version. The suspects left: the phone's GPU memory with the page turned (`html.rot` = a rotated `body`), a renderer restart of Telegram's web view (the match would be lost: ask whether the match is still there after the blink).

## One place for every room code: the 🔑 key (the owner, Nov 28: "I press Create, it gives a code, I send it to my friend: where does the friend type it? Fix that")
**What was wrong.** Shashka / chess / mafia rooms made a 4-letter code, but the only way in was the room window of the SAME game's list (or the world-chat message), 2v2 had its own window, and the 8-ball lobby its own card ("With a friend" -> "Join the lobby"): a friend holding a code had to guess which of them it was for.
- **One key, one box.** A 🔑 key sits on the menu bar (`#codeBtn`, left of the language key; hidden on the narrowest bar `#nav.nf8` like the fullscreen key). It opens the room window (`#brRoom`, `BR.want = "code"`, `codeOpen()` in `pro_9_room.js`) with ONE input (`.tn-code`, `maxlength="90"`): a typed code (`QX7K`), a lower-case one, or a **whole pasted share message** ("Join my game: Shashka, code QX7K ...") all work (`codeWords()` takes every 4-8 letter / digit word of it). The host's room window says it too: *"Your friend presses the 🔑 key and types this code."* The old "With friends" card of the 8-ball lobby takes ANY code now (it calls `codeJoin()` first; the lobby's own `lobbyJoin` stays as the fallback).
- **The server decides what the code is** (`server/server.js`): `{t:"jcode", codes:[...]}` -> `{t:"jcode", code, kind, ...}`, `kind` = `broom` (a board / arcade room: `game`, `state`, `stake`, `full`) / `troom` (online 2v2) / `lobby` (8-ball lobby, only while a seat is kept) / `none`; for a pasted message the first word that is a real room wins; 10 asks per 10 s per socket. `codeTaken(c)` makes `tcreate` / `bcreate` / the lobby never hand out a code that is already a room of ANY kind (before, the three tables were separate: a 2v2 room and a shashka room could share a code and a typed code was ambiguous).
- **Routing** (`codeRoute(m)`): `broom` -> that game's room window (`codeBoardRoom`: shashka / magic / mafia -> `shRoomOpen(game, code)`; `hockey` / `tennis` -> `arcRoomOpen` when it exists), `troom` -> `tnOpen(code)`, `lobby` -> `lobbyJoin(code)`, `none` -> the toast *"Room not found"* and the window stays open so he can retype.
- **An OLD server** (the Render deploy not done yet) does not know `jcode`: 2.5 s without an answer -> `codeFallback()` tries what the shape of the code says (a 6-letter word of the lobby alphabet = the lobby, otherwise a board room, and a `berr "none"` of that room then tries the 2v2 room). So the page works on both; the new server only makes it exact.
- **Two small bugs found on the way:** `BR.busy` (a 4 s guard against a double tap) silently blocked creating a SECOND room within 4 s of leaving the first - it is cleared on `broom` and on `shLeaveOnline`; the code window used to cut a pasted message to 8 characters and strip its spaces.
- **Tests:** `tools/test_code_join.js` 24/0 against the new server (a real server on port 8793 + two phones: the key, `jcode` for broom / none / first-word-wins / troom / lobby, a typed lower-case code joins a shashka room, a Magic Chess room from a pasted share message, an online 2v2 room, a 6-letter 8-ball lobby, an unknown code = toast and the window stays, the old With-friends card takes a board-room code, no page error); **17/7 against the OLD server** (only the 7 checks that need `jcode` fail, as they must - the fallback joins the rooms anyway). `test_lang_gaps` 58/0 (the two new phrases have 15 values in `tools/xt.txt`), `test_board_online` 33/0, `test_stake_ui` 33/0, `test_shashka_ui` 49/0, `test_magic_ui` 44/0, `test_mini_fit` 31/0, `test_web_serve` 17/0, `server/test_stake.js` 67/0, `server/test_team.js` 36/0, `test_html_lang` 22/0.
- **Render:** the server changed (`jcode`, `codeTaken`): the owner must press **Manual Deploy** on Render for the exact check; until then the page's fallback does the job.

## Table tennis: the swing steers the ball, a short ball can be returned, a human has time to answer (the owner, Nov 28: "there are many errors in the tennis: if I swing the racket to the left it only stands straight and goes back and forth, and to the right the same; it must be like real table tennis; the ball passes by itself")
**Measured on the old model first** (a page driven by a script, `pro_g_tennis.js` stepped by hand): (1) a *perfect* player (goes exactly where the ball will cross the plane) never lost a legal ball, so it was not a missed hit; (2) the aim used the paddle's speed **in the one frame of the contact**: a finger that rests under the ring (the normal way to play) gives 0 -> every return went **straight back, slow and short** (landing 35 cm beyond the net, 364 cm/s, z = 0) whatever the racket did; a swipe counted only if the frame happened to carry it; (3) the paddle's plane is 25 cm behind the table end, so a **short, slow ball bounced twice before it reached the plane** = "a double bounce" nobody could touch; (4) the bots' balls crossed in 0.45 s (hard, no ring) - no time to answer; (5) **my serve was automatic** (the ball went by itself). That is the "it plays itself" feeling.
- **The swing** (`tbtSwing`): the paddle's velocity smoothed over ~50 ms, and the **strongest swipe of the last 0.16 s is kept** (a flick that ended just before the ball came still aims it; one that ended 0.4 s before is forgotten). **The aim** (`tbtAim`): the sideways swipe is the direction (`66 x swipe/380` cm, saturating at the corners: a swipe of 100 cm/s lands 17 cm off the middle, 300 cm/s 52 cm, 600 cm/s 68 cm), **where on the racket the ball touches adds to it** (`off x 26`: the right side of the racket sends it right) and a little `- z x 0.16` across; the power is the speed of the swipe plus the energy of the ball that comes (a block of a fast ball is fast); an **upward swipe = a flatter, faster ball (top spin), a downward one = a higher, slower one (chop)**. With no swipe at all the ball goes back straight (that is what "waiting under the ball" gives, as in real play). Measured (the same ball, the swipe set to -600 / -300 / -100 / 0 / +100 / +300 / +600 cm/s): it lands at z = -68 / -52 / -17 / 0 / +17 / +52 / +68 cm, and a hard swipe lands 121 cm beyond the net at 815 cm/s against 48 cm and 367 cm/s for a rest.
- **The tube** (`TBT_RX = 56`): the ball is met **anywhere in the 56 cm in front of the paddle's plane** (the first moment it is within the racket's reach in the (z, y) plane: `TBT_PR` 15.7 = the head 12.5 + the ball 3.2; the easy bot forgives 19, mid 17), not only at the plane. A short slow ball that used to be a double bounce is returned. The rules are unchanged: it must have bounced once on my half, no volley.
- **My serve is a swipe** (`TBT.humanServe`; two on one phone: both servers): the ball hovers over the racket, the swipe aims and powers it (the same `tbtAim`), a swipe in the first 0.45 s is ignored (it belongs to the last point), and after 3.6 s the ball is served for him (the match never stalls). The bot serves by itself. Hints: *"Swipe the racket to serve"*, then *"Swipe the racket at the ball: it goes the way you swipe"* (15 languages in `tools/xt.txt`).
- **The face of the racket** (`tbtFace`, `tbtPaddle`): the head is drawn as a real disc in 3-D that **turns about the vertical toward the side of the swing and nods forward / back when brushed up / down** (tilt follows the swipe, at the hit it shows where the ball was sent for 0.3 s); a white ring spreads on the plane at every hit. **A mark on the other half (easy / mid)** shows where the ball would land if the racket hit it now: it moves with the swipe, which teaches the steering.
- **Time to answer**: each bot has a fastest ball (`vm`: easy 400, mid 470, hard 540 cm/s); a human now has at least **0.62 s (easy), 0.53 s (mid), 0.48 s (hard)** between the bot's hit and the ball at his racket (was 0.55 / 0.49 / 0.45 s). The bot aims at least 30 cm away from my racket (its own error is its weakness).
- **Measured after:** a perfect player never loses a legal ball at any level; a human-like player (answers after 0.2 - 0.32 s, a finger of 1000 cm/s, an aim off by 6 cm, 12 cm on hard where there is no ring) returns 100 % of what easy and mid send and 83 % of hard's; the **idle human still loses** (12 test matches each: easy 10 lost, mid and hard 12 lost; the easy bot can lose a match to its own missed serves).
- **Tests:** `tools/test_tennis_play.js` 32/0 (new: the swing aims and powers the return, left and right are the same, a flick before the ball counts and an old one does not, the side of the racket, the tube, the serve (swipe / by itself / early swipe ignored / bot / two phones), the face, the aim mark, the time to answer, the perfect and the human-like player, **a real finger (pointer events) swiped through the ball**, 160 random swipes all give legal returns), `test_tennis` 42/0 (two checks changed: the bots now return more balls, so "an idle human loses" is counted over 10 matches).
- **NOT done (say it to the owner):** **no spin physics** (no Magnus curve, no spin kick off the table: the "top spin / chop" are only a flatter / a higher flight), the aim is still assisted (the game finds the flight that clears the net and lands there: the swipe chooses WHERE, not the physics of every collision), and the finger covers the paddle on a phone (it is drawn under the finger; no offset). Online play of the tennis: see the next section when it exists.

## Air hockey: the laws of physics (the owner, Nov 28: "in the tennis and in the hockey too, the physics and the math must work correctly")
Measured on the old `pro_f_hockey.js` with `tools/test_hockey_physics.js` (it **fails 7 of 16 checks on the old build, passes 16 of 16 on the new one**):
- **A fast mallet jumped over the puck.** `hkMoveMallets` moved a mallet by its whole step of the frame (560 cm/s = 9.3 cm at 60 fps, **18.7 cm at 30 fps**) and only THEN looked at the puck (the 4 sub-steps moved the puck alone). The puck is 7.6 cm across: at 30 fps **36 of 63** test hits did not touch it at all (54 of 63 at 20 fps); at 60 fps, 20 % of the hits that did touch it were turned the wrong way. Now `hkStep` takes the mallets in the **same small steps as the puck** (`hkMalletPlan` = the plan of the frame, then `n = max(4, ceil(dt x 240))` steps of both): at most 2.3 cm per step at 60 fps. 63 of 63 hits at 20, 30, 60 and 120 fps.
- **The same shot ended 13 cm apart at 30 fps and 60 fps** (the mallets moved in jumps): now 1 cm (limit of the test 1.5).
- **The goal slot let a puck through a post.** The slot is 38 cm wide, the puck 7.6 cm; the old code let in any puck whose CENTRE was within 17.5 cm of the middle (a puck 17.4 cm off the middle **scored through the post**), and a puck that hit the corner bounced off a flat wall. Now the posts are **points at the corners of the slot** (`hkWalls`): a puck touching one with its edge bounces along the line from the post to its centre (so a puck turned by a post can go in or out, as in the real game), only a puck whose centre is within **15.2 cm** of the middle goes in without touching; the flat wall face is only beside the slot.
- **A mallet pressed against a puck at the end wall pushed the puck THROUGH the wall** (it sat at x = -103.3, the table ends at -100, under the mallet). Now the wall holds the puck and **the puck holds the mallet** (the mallet is pushed back along the line between their centres and its speed is zeroed for the rest of the frame): 5 situations (end wall, rail, corner, post, goal mouth) x 30 and 60 fps: the puck never leaves the table and the two never overlap (> 10.4 cm).
- **A mallet's centre could be 1.5 cm from the centre line** (its edge 5.7 cm in the other half) and the two mallets could overlap (2634 times in the random games). Now the **edge** stays in the half (`HKMR + 0.5` from the line).
- **What was right and stays** (checked now): the rail reflection (the angle out follows `tan(out) = 0.94 tan(in)`, the speed along the rail is kept), the mallet's impulse (`1.9 x` its speed: restitution 0.9 against a heavy mallet, limit 380 cm/s), the energy (with the mallets still nothing speeds the puck up: 120 flights of 8 s), 300 random games of two wild mallets at 30 / 45 / 60 fps: the puck always on the table, never above 380 cm/s, no NaN, the mallets in their halves and never above 560 cm/s.
- **Tests:** `test_hockey_physics.js` 16/0, `test_hockey.js` 30/0. **Not done:** no spin / friction between mallet and puck (the puck leaves along the line of centres), a puck squeezed between the two mallets at the centre line (the only way to overlap, up to 4 cm), the speed limit of 380 cm/s is a game choice (a harder swipe than 200 cm/s of the mallet gives the same speed).

## Table coins and stakes in shashka / Magic Chess / Mafia (the owner, Nov 24: "yutgan davom etadi ... 1 dollarga 2 ming tanga ... eng past 3 tanga, 100000 tangagacha ... mafiyada 3 dan 9 gacha")
**What it is.** A SEPARATE, **virtual** coin, the 🎲 "stol tangasi" (table coin), played for in the three board games. It is not the 🪙 coin, not 💵, not 💎. **Safeguards (the owner was told, and went on with the virtual coin): real-money gambling is illegal in Uzbekistan and against the rules of Telegram, Google Play, the portals and the card providers, so these coins cannot be turned into money or goods and cannot be sent to another player** (no gift, no transfer, no market, no cash-out; they move only through the result of a match, and the house keeps 10% of every pool). The wallet window says so in 15 languages. Before this goes wide: ask a lawyer and re-read Telegram's, Google Play's and Stripe's rules on "stakes / simulated gambling" (a virtual stake for a coin that is sold for money is what a reviewer looks at).
- **Wallet (server).** `u.tc` (new players, and old ones, start with **12**: `TC_START`), `u.tcHold` (stakes of running matches), `u.tcLog` (the last 40 results), `u.tcAd`, `u.tcBuy`. `GET /api/tc` = balance, hold, history, tiers, ads left today, purchase limit left, packs. The server pushes `{t: "wallet", tc, hold}` on the socket whenever the balance changes; `/api/sync` carries `tc` too. Coins live ONLY on the server (a match holds them there), never in the phone's save.
- **Where coins come from.** (1) **A rewarded video: +1** (`POST /api/tc/ad`; the page shows the video with `y8Ad("reward", "try-tc")` and then asks; the server cannot see the video, so it limits what a modified page could take: one claim per `TC_AD_GAP_MS` (25 s), `TC_AD_DAY` (40) a day). A loser with 0 coins needs 3 videos for the smallest stake (3). Ad-free players are NOT paid for nothing: the key always plays a video. (2) **A purchase: $1 = 2000** (`tc_2k` $1, `tc_4k` $2, `tc_10k` $5; flat prices in `FLAT_USD`, the same Stars / card / card-to-card flows as the other packs; `fulfill()` credits the server wallet, not a grant), **at most $5 (10 000 coins) in 24 hours** per player (`tcBuyLeft`, checked by `/api/checkout`, `starsRefuse` = Stars + Telegram Payments + card-to-card; a refused order says `tc_limit`). Not sold in the Google Play app (`playSkus` has only gems / cash), in Y8 (`NO_CARD`) or in the portals' builds (no card shop there): coins are bought on the site and in Telegram.
- **Stakes.** A room carries `stake` (0 = a friendly room, as before): 3, 5, 10, 25, 50, 100, 250, 500, 1000, 2500, 5000, 10000, 25000, 50000, 100000 (`TC_TIERS`; for Mafia up to 25 000, because the mafioso puts in 3 times). `bcreate {game, stake}`; a join / a world-chat join is refused with `coins` (the wallet window opens with "Needed: N") when the balance is below the stake (Mafia: below 3 stakes). **1-on-1 (shashka, Magic Chess):** at the start both put the stake on hold (a rematch holds it again; a seat that cannot pay stops the rematch: `berr coins` with the seat), the winner gets **90% of the pool** (`floor(2*S*0.9)`: 3 + 3 -> 5, +2 for the winner, -3 for the loser), a draw gives both stakes back. **Mafia:** a citizen / doctor / sheriff puts in S, a mafioso 3S (everybody must have 3S at the start: the roles are dealt after), the **winning team shares 90% of the pool equally**; a player who LEFT the running match loses his stake whatever the result; if nobody on the winning side stayed, those who stayed get their stake back. **A stake match of Mafia has no bots: 5 to 8 real players** (a free Mafia room still fills up with bots; the start key is off until 5 sit). The portals' builds do not offer stakes at all (`tcPortal`): the policies of Y8 / Playgama / GameMonetize on betting-like features; a stake room that a portal player meets by a code is left at once.
- **The SERVER judges a stake match** (the first design trusted the first phone's `bover`: one modified page could have claimed every win). `server/boardrules.js` loads `server/boardcores.js`, which `node tools/build_cores.js` cuts out of `index.html` (the `/*SH_CORE_BEGIN*/` and `/*MC_CORE_BEGIN*/` blocks: ONE copy of the rules, the phones and the tests use the same text; **run `node tools/build_cores.js` and commit the file after changing those blocks - `node server/test_boardcores.js` fails when it is out of date**). **It is a file, not a read of `index.html`, because index.html is 50 MB (every picture, font and sound is inside it): reading it at start held ~100 MB of RAM for as long as the server ran (RSS 155 MB instead of 53 MB, peak 203 MB instead of 97 MB) - a 512 MB Render plan with the whole database in memory has none to spare; the deploy of Nov 26 failed, see "Render deploy failure" below** and the room plays every move on its own copy (`shFind` / `mcFind` + `shPlay` / `mcPlay`): an illegal move is refused (the sender gets `bsync` with the real list), the END is the rules' end (mate, no moves, repetition...), a phone's `bover` is ignored in a stake match except `why: "resign"` (and only against its sender), a draw needs an offer from one seat and a "yes" from the OTHER one, a phone that leaves or stays away past the grace loses, and **a clock** (`BROOM_TURN_MS`, 120 s a move; the match tells the phones `tl` / `tlm` and the page shows it) makes a stalling player lose. **A match that ends by a resignation / leaving / the clock in its first 4 plies moves no coins** (`void`: nobody can hand a stake over by resigning at once, and a stake cannot be taken back by running away from a lost game after that). If the rules cannot be loaded (`BR_RULES.ok` false: an `index.html` that is not the game's) stake rooms for shashka / chess are refused (`stake`).
- **Holds and restarts.** A stake is `tcHold(u, matchId, n)` (the balance drops at once) and `tcSettle` (the payout, one history line with the net result). A room that is closed while a match runs (everybody gone, the 6-hour sweeper) gives every hold back (`bGiveBack`); **a server restart ends all rooms, so `tcRecover()` at boot gives back every hold that is left** (no coin is lost with a Render restart).
- **The phone** (`pro_e_stake.js`): the room window has **stake chips** (Friendly + the tiers; the ones the balance cannot pay are off; the choice is remembered in `profile.tcPick`) and the wallet key `🎲 12 ＋`; a room with a stake says "Stake: 3 🎲 · the winner gets 5 🎲" (Mafia: "citizens 3 · mafia 9"); the match has a chip with the stake and the clock of the seat on turn (in the info column of the board, in the right column of Mafia, floating over the 3D hall); the result is a toast (+2 / -3 / "draw: stake returned" / "ended in the first moves: stake returned"; Mafia: a line in the result window); the world chat card shows `🎲 3` (Mafia `🎲 3/9`); the **wallet window** (`tcWallet(need)`): balance, held, "Watch an ad: +1" (off, with the reason, where there is no ad network), the three packs with the limit left today, the note that the coins are virtual and cannot be exchanged or sent, the last results. A new player is told about the 12 coins once. 26 new lines in `tools/xt.txt` (15 languages, `pro_xt6.py`).
- **Known limits (say them to the owner):** two accounts that agree can still pass coins by "losing" on purpose (after 4 plies; the house takes 10% of every such pass, and the coins are worth nothing outside the game: watch `db.users[*].tcLog` for pairs); a modified page can claim up to 40 ad coins a day; the Telegram Stars price of a pack is `starsFor(usd)` like every other pack. **Server changes: the owner must press Manual Deploy on Render** (the page itself is served by Cloudflare / Render from `web/`).
- **Tests:** `node server/test_stake.js` (67 checks, ~1 minute: the wallet, the ad limits, the purchase (a signed Stripe webhook) and its $5 limit, a stake that is not on the list, not enough coins, the hold / payout numbers (3 + 3 -> 5), an illegal move, a claimed win that is ignored, an early resignation = void, a rematch, a draw offer / "yes" rules, the fool's mate in Magic Chess ends by the rules, the clock, leaving, a dropped phone, a rematch the poor seat cannot pay, Mafia with 5 real players (hold 9 / 3, 90% shared, a leaver loses), the holds given back after a restart, the admin page `GET /api/tc/admin?key=ADMIN_KEY`) and `node tools/test_stake_ui.js` (33 checks, ~2 minutes, `SHOT=dir` for pictures: chips, the room banner, the world card, the match chip, +2 / -3, the wallet window fits, "Needed: 10", Mafia chips / banner, a portal build has none of it, the turned page).

## Two fixes from the owner's screenshots (Nov 23): the 3rd place of the weekly results, and a spectator's stickers
- **The weekly results window ("Itogi nedeli", `showWeeklyResults()`): the 3rd place was a huge empty card** (the owner: *"why did you make the 3rd place so much bigger than the 1st and 2nd?"*, a Telegram screenshot of an upright phone). Cause: the player on the 3rd place had an avatar **frame**. `avHTML()` draws a framed avatar as `.avx.avp`, and the old CSS gives it `margin: 12%` (floral frames, `.avi.avj`: `36%`) - a percentage of its cell's **width**: in the weekly row that cell is wide, so the row grew to 5-9 times the height of the others (467 px against 53 on an upright phone, 355 against 92 on a computer) and the real content sat in the middle of it, out of sight. The leaderboard rows (`.lbr .avc .avx.avp`) and the home bar (`.hci`) already zero that margin; the weekly rows and the player's own bar did not. Now `.wk .wkrow .wkpl>.avx.avp, .wk .wkme>.avx.avp` have `margin: 0` and `transform: scale(.8)` (`.avj` frames `.45`), next to the other `.wk` rules. Found by trying every one of the 128 `FRAMES` on the 3rd place.
- **The green "+100 cash, country prize #3 UZ" line is real money.** It is built from `profile.prizeLog`, which `applyGrant()` fills only when the server's grant was applied (`profile.cash += grant.cash`): the server pays the country top 3 `COUNTRY_PRIZES = [400, 200, 100]` cash (`server.js`: `grant(u, {cash, kind: "country", from: "#3 UZ"})`) and the world top 3 `WEEK_PRIZES = [1500, 750, 400]`, once, when the week rolls over (`server/test_week_prizes.js`, 15 checks).
- **A spectator's sticker was shown as a bare emoji character** (the owner: *"why do other things come out when I send a sticker?"*). In the corner feed (`#liveFeed`) and in the spectators' chat (`specDrawLog()`) the text of a `schat` message (the sticker's emoji key: the ring sticker is a medal character) was written as plain text, so a small medal glyph appeared instead of the picture he had chosen; only the players' own bubbles and chat log drew `STK_IMG`. Now `chatBody(msg)` (the feed) and `specDrawLog()` draw the picture when `STK_IMG[msg]` exists (`.lfs` in the feed, `.splb.stk` in the chat).
- **Test:** `node tools/test_wk_stk.js` (6 checks, ~20 s): every frame on the 3rd place and on the player's own avatar leaves the rows the same height (upright phone and computer), the feed and the spectators' chat draw the sticker's picture, and the `schat` handler uses `chatBody()`. It fails on the old code.

## Shared shots: the replay window's Share sends the shot to people (Nov 23)
The owner (Telegram screenshots): *"on the replay screen it looks strange; Share does not send it to people, it only pulls it to the phone; and the avatars look wrong in places"*. Before, **Share** recorded a video and, where the page could not share files (Telegram's WebView, a PC), showed it with a "Save" link. Now:
- **What is sent is the shot, not a video.** `proPackReplay(R)` takes the frames the window shows (at most 10 s of 60 Hz = 600 frames), writes every ball as two `Int16` (x, y in 1/16 px; `-32768` = the ball is off the table; little endian), deflates it with the browser's own `CompressionStream("deflate-raw")` when there is one (`z: 1`; otherwise plain, `z: 0`) and sends `{v: 1, z, nb, n, nums, city, tbl, f (base64), kind, wk, why, rec}` to **`POST /api/replay`**: 3-16 balls, ~4-12 KB deflated (a plain 16-ball shot is ~50 KB). The table's look rides as ids (`city` + the table skin `tbl`): the receiver draws it with his own `drawTable`, his own balls, his own language. **`kind`** (`best` / `worst` / `trick`) + `rec` (n, combo, bank, brk, won ...) or `wk` (the trick's name key) let him read the caption in HIS language (`proShotText` / `proBadText` / `T(wk)`); `why` is the sharer's text, only a fallback.
- **The server** (`server/server.js`, `POST /api/replay`, `GET /api/replay?id=`; both need a sign-in): checks the shape exactly (version, 2-20 ball numbers 0-15, 2-620 frames, base64, **the data must be exactly n x nb x 4 bytes**, inflated for `z: 1` with a size cap, so it cannot be used to keep other data), takes the name / avatar / frame / country from the ACCOUNT (not from the page), answers `{id}` (10 letters / digits, random). Limits: a body of 110 KB, 20 uploads a minute per IP, 30 an hour and 120 a day per player, **400 shots in all** (the oldest go first) and **30 days** (`pruneReplays` every 6 h and on every upload); a deleted account takes its shots with it. Stored in `db.replays`: a new table `replays` in Postgres (`storage.js`, created at the first start), part of `db.json` in the file store. `server/test_replay.js` (27 checks) covers all of it.
- **The links.** Telegram: `<TG_LINK>?startapp=r_<id>`; web: `<origin>/?r=<id>&ref=<invite code>`. In Telegram the share key calls `TG.openTelegramLink("https://t.me/share/url?url=<link>&text=<text>")` - **Telegram's own chat picker**, like the invite and the daily shot (`shareInvite`, `shareDailyShot`); elsewhere `navigator.share({url, text})` or a copy to the clipboard. A phone's browser that can share FILES (`navigator.canShare({files})`, not Telegram) gets the clip itself (a real video file) and nothing is uploaded. No connection: nothing is shared, the player is told, Share works again.
- **Whoever opens the link.** `tgBoot` has a branch for `r_<id>` (before the lobby-code branch: **`parseLobbyCode("r_Ab3dEf9x")` would have taken it for a lobby code**), the start-up block reads `?r=`; both call `proSharedWant(id)`, which **remembers the id in the game's own `store` (`b8rp` = `{id, at}`, two minutes; not in `sessionStorage`: the Playgama build refuses any page that mentions the browser's storage - `tools/test_html_lang.js` caught it)** (the page restarts itself once while it starts, to apply the HD pictures: the address is already cleaned by then and the shot was lost - found by `tools/test_replay_share.js`; its first version of the two-minute check used `w.at | 0`, which cuts a 13-digit time stamp to 32 bits, and the test caught that too) and `proSharedOpen(id)` waits until the player is in the menu with no window in the way (never in the middle of a match), fetches the shot and plays it in `#proRep` (under the sharer's name, the caption in his own language; Share there passes the same link on, no second upload). An unknown / old link: a toast, and it is forgotten.
- **Why not the video.** A video would need a hosted file, a format every phone plays (iPhone's WebView does not play WebM) and ~1.5 MB per share; the shot is 4-12 KB and plays in the game itself, which is also what brings the receiver to the game. The 10 s video stays for phones that can share files.
- **Test:** `node tools/test_replay_share.js` (26 checks, ~4 min: the real server + a mock of Telegram's WebApp): the window fits on an upright phone / a small landscape phone / a computer and does not change after Share; the share URL; the trip of the positions (to 1/32 px, with and without deflate); a Telegram receiver (start parameter, language, not a lobby), the web receiver, the restart; the file-sharing phone and the one without it; no connection; an unknown link; the best shot of a match read in Russian.
- **Texts:** one new line in `tools/xt.txt` ("Zarba topilmadi yoki eskirgan", 15 languages).

## Avatars with frames: the margin cap (Nov 23)
The owner: *"however many avatars we have, fix the visuals of all of them"*. **The cause of the weekly-results bug was general.** An avatar with a premium frame is `.avx.avp` and carried `margin: 12%` (floral frames `.avi.avj`: `36%`) - a percentage of the CONTAINER's width. In a wide flex / grid / block it became a huge margin: in a chat line the avatar of a framed player squeezed the message into a one-letter-wide column and made the line 5-10 times higher; spectator lists, the world chat, the lobby host, the versus screen, the cup ceremony, the profile had the same risk (the lists of the ratings, the home bar and the lobby slots had their own overrides). The fix is at the root: `margin: min(12%, calc(var(--s) * .2))` (floral `min(36%, calc(var(--s) * .36))`), where `--s` is the avatar's own size (`avHTML` writes it into its style): in a narrow cell nothing changes (the percentage is the smaller one), in a wide one the margin is the frame's own reserve (the frame art overhangs 19.5 % / the floral 86 %). The per-screen overrides stay. Measured before / after in 13 kinds of container x 3 widths x 3 sizes x every premium frame: the worst margin went from **9 x the avatar to 0.36 x**, the worst growth of a row from **18 x to <= 0.8 x** the avatar.
- **The art was checked too:** all **322 avatars** (100 drawn, 12 zodiac, 76 portraits, 70 premium portraits, 64 horror) paint a picture that is neither blank nor one flat colour, all **128 frames** have their picture, and the match HUD (`drawAvatar` on the canvas) draws every avatar with every kind of frame (1610 pictures) without an exception.
- **2v2 / 9-ball lists:** the rows of "2v2 teams" and "two people on one device" show **avatars** (my avatar + portraits of the owner's set, a team a colour, a bot partner with a little robot) instead of the painted pictograms; the friends on this phone are portraits of the owner's drawn set (`proAvPick`: three different ones, none is mine) instead of the plain cartoon faces 5-8.
- **Test:** `node tools/test_avatars.js` (8 checks, ~30 s; fails on the old CSS).
